## 2026-11-08 — stage 7d2: the renderers as readable C

### D124. Stage 7d2: every B2 renderer function has a readable build behind `fast_render=3` (`NAME_hand_rc.c`): the control registers it sets are set as before, and it uses no GTE data register, FLAG or GTE command, no PS1 scratchpad, spill area or hand stack, which the compiler now enforces. The presenter build takes the same code, so its context has no register file. On hardware (7D2a/7D2b) the pictures and memory card are identical and the renderers are 5% lighter, and the owner made `fast_render=3` the Vita's default (2026-11-09); the host's stays 2
- **What D119 asked** (its 7d2): the renderers' hand C to readable C (B2 → B1) by D87's rule, behind `fast_render=3`, the presenter build on the same code; checks: the oracle replay with D83/D87-style shadows, `host_checks.sh` byte-identical at `fast_render=3`, `--render-ctx-check`, `--fast-render-check`, `--native-draw-check` and `--walk-check` at 0 differences on the demos, gems, wsi, lod, routes A and B and the tour, ctest, Vita3K `realtime=0` identical; then a hardware run proposed, not run.
- **The order:** the owner chose on 2026-11-08 to run 7d2 and 7e in parallel, over D119's "7d2, then 7e" (in the coordinator's chat). 7d2 owns the renderer files; the presenter's entry points (`NAME_pres`, four `u32` arguments) are unchanged, and the 7e session was told which context fields go (below).
- **The builds** (one more per renderer file, `NAME_hand_rc.c`, six lines: `NGTE_RC 1` then the source):
  - the seven renderer files (environment, mobys, Spyro, sky, shadows, flame, particles), and the three B2 files beside them that had one register-file version (the moby culls, the sky's fog and portal clipping, the environment's animation update; `render_hand.h` `HAND_UNIT_FN_RC`). With them all 25 B2 functions of D119's count have a readable build;
  - `NGTE_RC` implies `NGTE 1` and `NGTE_MATH 1` (the native maths and native_draw's records), and the entry is `NAME_rc`. `fast_render=3` picks it (`HAND_PICK_RC`, `HAND_UNIT_RC_PICK`); `native_draw=1` now needs `fast_render` 2 or 3;
  - `NAME_hand_pres.c` is `render_ctx_names.h`, then `NGTE_RC 1`, then the source: the presenter is the readable code (7e calls it).
- **D87's rule, and how it holds:**
  - **The control registers a renderer sets stay set:** the rotations and translations (`CTC2` 0–7), the light and light-colour matrices (8–12, 16–20), the back colour that carries the depth parameters and the camera offset (13–15), the far colour (21–23), DQA/DQB and ZSF3. Where the native code took them as values and the register-file builds replayed the `CTC2` at a flush, the readable build writes them: a colour command's far colour at the same flush points (`ngte_flush` writes the last one's, `render_hand.h`), the animation blends' and frame blends' at their ends, Spyro's colour filter and floor clip, the specular normal's diagonal (OP), the sprite's cleared rows. `--render-ctx-check` compares all of them but FLAG after every call (below).
  - **The data registers go,** with every `NGTE_TAIL` register-file step and every `NgteSync` replay. In the readable builds `MTC2`, `MFC2`, every data-register `C2_` name, FLAG and every GTE command expand to undeclared names, and `gteRegs` is a view of its control half only (`render_hand.h`'s end): a leftover is a compile error, not a judgement call. The `NgteSync` calls stay in the shared source for the register-file builds and compile to nothing (NCLIP's winding and the far colour aside); they go with those builds.
  - **What was still on the register file at `fast_render=2`** now has native code: the lit mobys' lighting (CC, the light-colour MVMVA), the specular mobys entirely (their transforms, OP, SQR, CC, the metal UVs), the moby sprites, the frame blends' tails, Spyro's set-up, floor heights, colour filter and floor clip, the particles' turned quads, types 4–7 and the sparkles' cross, the culls' matrix turns, the sky's fog and portal clipping (DPCS, GPL, NCLIP), the environment's animation update (GPF, GPL) and grids. New native commands in `gte_native.h`: GPL sf 1, MVMVA sf 0 on any matrix, OP, SQR, CC and the light matrices' getters; `tests/gte/test_gte_native` checks each against the register-file command on 1,000,000 random states (and PCSX-Redux's vectors where present).
  - **The scratchpad** becomes each build's own working memory (`g_render_work`; the presenter's `g_pres_scratch`): nothing reads it across calls (the audit below), so it is a renderer's scratch, not the PS1 scratchpad. The subdivision tables' scratchpad addresses are taken as offsets into it (`env_hand.c` `SC_TABLE`).
  - **The spill area and the hand stack go** (`hand_spill_regs` is empty, the particles' and the sky's saves are locals or gone); in the readable builds `g_mips_sp` and `D_80077DD8` are names that don't link.
- **The poison audit** (host `--render-poison`, `render_rc.c`): at `fast_render` 1 and 2, after each renderer call (or B2 function) returns to the game, the scratchpad, the GTE's 32 data registers and FLAG, the spill area and the hand stack are overwritten with a pattern that changes every call. Any later reader, a renderer's next call included, would see it. **`tools/host_checks.sh` with `--render-poison` at `fast_render=2`: all 279 files byte-identical to main's** (the demos 26, the tour 141, routes A and B 56 each; 580,202 renderer calls each followed by the poison; `logs/s7d2/cmp-poison.txt`). So nothing reads the scratchpad, the data registers, FLAG, the spill area or the hand stack across calls, and the readable build need not write them. The audit bites: `--render-poison-bite` also overwrites OFX, which the renderers do read, and all six shots and the trace of the demo's first 6,000 vblanks change (`logs/s7d2/q-bite`).
- **One read of a data register across calls,** found by reading, not by the audit: Spyro's colour filter (`pete_render_hand.c` `FilterColours`, when he is hurt or fairy-kissed) takes INTPL's code byte from RGBC, which the renderer never sets, so the asm's colour words carry whatever colour word an earlier renderer moved there in their top byte. The GPU ignores that byte in a Gouraud polygon's second and third colour, so no picture depends on it; the readable build writes 0. No run of the checks reaches it: a probe on the demo, gems, routes A and B and the 100,000-vblank tour found no call (`logs/s7d2/q-fc*`). Listed as a known difference.
- **The presenter's context** (`render_ctx.h`, `.c`, `render_ctx_names.h`):
  - no register file: `g_pres_gte` keeps its type for the shared headers, but only its control half is used; `render_ctx_check` fills the data half with a pattern before each call and requires it unchanged. `render_ctx_gte_regs.c` and `render_ctx_gte_ops.c` are gone; `pres_ngte_vcache` is in `render_ctx.c`;
  - gone: `g_pres_spill`, `PRES_SPILL`, `g_pres_mips_sp`, `PRES_STACK`, `render_ctx_stack_top`, and the subdivision tables' copies (`g_pres_grid2`, `g_pres_grid4`, `g_pres_inner4`; `render_ctx_init` is empty). 7e's session was told before this landed.
- **The checks' changes:**
  - `--fast-render-check` at 3 (`hand_check_rc`): each call runs `fast_render=1`'s build, then the readable one, from the same state; RAM but the scratchpad, the spill area and the stack below `$sp`, the control registers but FLAG, and the result of the `_v0` entries must match. The arena build only, as before: on the native build the check's snapshot doesn't cover the data labels (ordinary globals there), main's `--fast-render-check` included.
  - `--render-ctx-check` compares the control registers but FLAG, not the register file, and no scratchpad; the presenter's working memory starts each call filled with a pattern. At `fast_render=2` it now compares the register-file code with the readable code on every call. With `--fast-render-check` too, the hand check's readable call is the presenter check (`NAME_rcx`), so one run does both.
  - **The oracle replay's shadow** (`render_shadow`, `hand_check.c`; on in the replay at `--fast-render 3`, `SPYRO_REPLAY_RENDERSHADOW=0` turns it off; `SPYRO_SHADOW=render` over a whole host run): each call runs the renderer's mips2c unit first, then the readable build; RAM but the scratchpad, the spill area and the stack, the control registers but FLAG, and the `_v0` results must match (`rendershadow` lines), then the call leaves the unit's state where nothing was compared, so a sample compares whole with the emulator's (D83's method). `SPYRO_RENDER_SHADOW_BITE=1` flips one word the unit wrote in the readable result: the shadow's own test.
- **Known differences** (none reachable in the checks' runs, none visible in a picture):
  - Spyro's colour filter's code byte (above).
  - Spyro's first vertex step: the asm transforms whatever V0 held into a slot it then writes again, and the readable build skips that projection, so only the vertex cache (`subpixel=1`) sees one stale vertex fewer. The exception is a first part of two vertices, which Spyro's models don't have: there the slot keeps it, and the readable build's holds the projection of (0, 0) instead (RAM differs; unreachable with the game's models).
  - The flame's strip with no head drawn earlier in the call projects V0–V2 with V2 as an earlier renderer left it; only its first two points are read, so the readable build projects those two. Again only the vertex cache sees it.
  - The sky's fog colours of a sector with no colours (the asm's loop doesn't end) and other zero-count loops the game never builds.
  - `InvSqrt`'s LZCR counts leading zeros as the register file does (gte_regs.c, PsyCross's), not a real GTE's leading sign bits; the same as `fast_render` 1 and 2.

## 2026-11-08 — stage 7e: the presenter's draw, the game's draw code built a second time

### D125. Stage 7e: the in-between frame in play can be drawn by the presenter, `draw.c` and the files its play branch calls compiled a second time onto a snapshot (`src/game/pres/`), behind `interp_mode=2`. Every tick on the host its list is D30's pass's record for record, and with it on every file is D30's. D30's pass stays the reference and the fallback for the other gamestates
- **What D119 asked** (its 7e): `src/gamestates/draw.c` and its callees built again with `-include` of a names header, each global they read on the snapshot's copy, each renderer on its 7d build, D30's hooks as stubs, no decomp file edited; a snapshot of 7a's read globals plus the moby array and the particle space, copied and blended, with the pointers into them rebased (an audit finds which); a link check; the callees inventoried from the link map; on the game thread at D30's pass's point, behind `interp_mode=2`.
- **The owner's order:** 7d2 and 7e run in parallel (the owner's decision, 2026-11-08), over D119's "7d2, then 7e". 7e works against `fast_render=2` (the default) and treats 7d's presenter entries and context as a fixed interface. Its only edits in 7d2's files are two additive hunks (below), which the 7d2 session agreed to (2026-11-08). 7d2 merged first (PR #108, D124, 2026-11-09), so this entry is D125, and the presenter draws at `fast_render` 2 and 3 alike (3 the Vita's default since D124).
- **The second build** (`cmake/presenter.cmake`):
  - the decomp's `gamestates/draw.c`, `camera.c` and `environment.c` (the patched copy, as `spyro_game_main` compiles them) and our `order_table.c`, `moby_lists_hand.c`, `cyclorama_hand.c`, `libgte.c`, `math_hand.c`, `images_hand.c`, `moby_cull_hand.c` and `sky_clip_hand.c` (the last two through wrappers, `src/game/pres/moby_cull.c` and `sky_clip.c`, that give their entries the plain form the native build has), each compiled again with `-include src/game/pres/pres_names.h`;
  - the objects are linked into one relocatable object, `pres_draw.o` (`gcc -r`), and `objcopy --keep-global-symbol=GamestateDraw_pres` makes every other function in it local. So inside it, a call of any function the set defines goes to the set's own copy, without renaming any of them, and a name the set doesn't define stays the game's;
  - **no decomp file is edited**, and the matching build doesn't see any of it. The game's own objects are unchanged.
- **The names** (`src/game/pres/pres_names.h`):
  - 7d's context (`render_ctx_names.h`: the GTE, scratchpad, spill area, work area, sector flags, primitive buffer and OTs, records, LOD);
  - the snapshot's labels (`pres_snap_names.h`), below;
  - the twelve renderers onto their 7d builds (`NAME_pres`);
  - D30's hooks: `VSync` doesn't wait (`pres_VSync`, D30's `pass_vsync`); `LoadImage`, `StoreImage`, `MoveImage` and `ClearImage` are dropped (no `Spu*` call is on the draw path);
  - present.c's and native_draw.c's hooks the draw calls, onto the presenter's own (`pres_draw.c`): `PortPolySpace` on its buffers, `PortProjectionHook` on its 16:9 switch, the HUD audit as nothing, `nd_list_linked` on its record cursor;
  - what stays the game's is the presenter's output on the game thread: `DrawOTag`, `PutDrawEnv`, `PutDispEnv`, `DrawSync` and `port_present_note_2d` (7f routes them).
- **The snapshot** (`src/game/pres/pres_snap.c`), taken after the update at D30's pass's point:
  - **37 labels,** each at its own offset in a mirror of the data region (`g_pres_mirror`, 61 KB; `tools/pres_snap_syms.py` makes each copy's symbol from `psx.map`, so the copies keep the labels' layout and an access past a label's end reaches its neighbour's copy, as the game's does). Only the 37 are copied each tick: the camera and Spyro (blended); the DBs, the DB drawn, the moby array's pointer; what the draw writes (the HUD mobys' place and the sky's limit, the pacer's vblanks `D_80075950`, the HUD, the sky's angles, the portal's depth, box, outline and points, the fog, the sky list); and what the update changes that the draw reads (7a's read set: the gamestate, demo mode, delta time, level ticks, fade, border, flight level, Spyro hidden, the sky colours and portals, the light colours, the tracers, and two read only by the other gamestates' branches);
  - the renderers' 7d builds read the same copies: `render_ctx_names.h` includes `pres_snap_names.h` (so their `g_Camera` and `g_Spyro` are the blended ones);
  - **the moby array and the particle space** are copied into the presenter's area (7d's `render_ctx_area`, after the game's memory), with the presenter's own primitive buffers and OTs: a mirror of the game's layout (the same sizes, so the same bounds trip; its OTs zeroed each frame, as the game's are by linking). The camera, Spyro, the mobys and the particles are blended in the copies by present.c's own blend (`blend_on`, the code `blend_state` runs on the live state for D30's pass);
  - **the pointers into them** are rebased onto the copies. `--pres-audit` scans the copies for words pointing into the moby array or the particle space (the demo, 1,972 snapshots): `g_LevelMobys`; each moby's `+0x4` (its next in a collision chain); each particle's `+0x18` (its moby); each glow's `+0x8` (the position it follows); the camera's `+0xD0`; Spyro's `+0x21C` (the moby he uses); and the work area's moby lists, which the presenter's draw rebuilds before it reads them. The first five are rebased by field, only where the word does point into a copy (a glow may follow a position in the level's data); the DBs' buffer and OT pointers go to the presenter's buffers, `g_CurDB` to the mirror's `g_DB`, the particle list's pointer to the copy;
  - **the renderers' context** as D30's pass finds the game's: the register file, the scratchpad, the spill and work areas, the sector flags and bytes, the shadow list, the glows (rebased), sparkles and flame; LOD's state as `port_lod_draw_begin(1)` leaves it (the game's put back), its buffer addresses moved; the records into the pool the game isn't writing (the walk of the game's last list joined first), under a serial of the presenter's own.
- **In `present.c`:** at D30's pass's point, with `interp_mode=2` and in play, `presenter_pass` draws the frame (16:9 included; in an attract demo at 16:9 it starts from the 16:9 copies of the visibility state and leaves its own there, as D30's pass does). It saves no state but the GPU environments its `PutDrawEnv` and `PutDispEnv` change. D30's pass leaves libgpu's environment put last (`currentDispEnv`) changed for the logic's `PutDispEnv`; the presenter in its place leaves it as the pass would. Elsewhere, and in the other gamestates, D30's pass runs as before; `interp_mode` 0 (the default) changes nothing.
- **The check** (`--pres-check`, `src/game/pres/pres_check.c`): in play the presenter draws each in-between frame first, its list recorded and not walked (`currentDispEnv` put back), then D30's pass, recorded and shown. A frame record is the draw and display environments put, then the list in its order: each packet's words after its link, a native_draw marker's records expanded. The polygons' bits the PS1 GPU ignores aren't compared (the high half of a textured polygon's third and fourth UV words, a gouraud polygon's later colour code bytes): they hold what the buffer held before, and the presenter's buffers are its own. Without that, the first difference was such a pad word (`logs/s7e-try7`).
- **What the check found on the way** (each fixed before the results):
  - `D_80075950` is defined in `draw.c` itself, uninitialised: a common symbol, so the presenter's copy of `draw.c` wrote the game's own pacer (the link check had taken the common symbol for the presenter's). Now a snapshot label, and the link check names common symbols as the game's;
  - the particle space begins where the world OT ends, so the particle list's pointer was taken for the buffers' end and moved into the buffers' mirror (a crash, `logs/s7e-try2`): an address is matched inside a region first, a region's end only after;
  - libgpu's `currentDispEnv` (above): the check's presenter, run first, moved D30's display environment;
  - the glows' positions (above): a glow on a blended moby drew from the game's moby.
- **The link check** (`tests/pres/link_check.py`, ctest `pres_link`): from `GamestateDraw_pres`, every function reached in `pres_draw.o` (65; the other gamestates' draws, 7g's, are where it stops) may name outside itself only the presenter's own (`g_pres_*`, `pres_*`, `NAME_pres`: 80 names), a read-only name (the constant tables `D_8006CB8C`, `D_8006CBF8`, `D_8006CC78`, the square roots `D_80074B84`, the level's `g_Environment`, `g_Models`, `g_SkipLowPolyWorld`, `g_LevelId`, the config `g_present`, the arena base, libc), its output (`DrawOTag`, `DrawSync`, `PutDispEnv`, `PutDrawEnv`, `port_present_note_2d`), or a game function that names no writable data, itself or through its callees, which the check reads in the game's objects: `Memcpy`, `Memset`, `Memset16`, `SetDrawMode`. Nothing of its own but functions and constants. 7d's `render_ctx_link` still passes for the renderers.
- **The inventory** (`tools/pres_inventory.py BUILD`, from the game's objects): the play branch reaches 65 functions in eleven files; those naming any writable global get the presenter's build (the eleven files), the four above are shared, and the twelve renderers are 7d's. `logs/s7e/link-list.txt` has the list.
- **With 7d2** (D124, merged into main before this branch, 2026-11-09): its presenter builds become fast_render=3's readable code, with no GTE data registers, scratchpad, spill area or hand stack of their own, and `render_ctx.h` loses the spill area, the hand stack and the GTE's copies. So that either PR can merge first, 7e keys on a marker 7d2 adds to `render_ctx.h` and `render_ctx_names.h` (`RENDER_CTX_READABLE`, 0 until then):
  - the culls and the sky's clipping are built on the readable code (`NGTE_RC 1`; 7d2's `HAND_UNIT_FN_RC` makes their `NAME_pres`, which `pres_names.h` maps the callers onto);
  - the context leaves out the spill area and the hand stack;
  - `libgte.c`'s matrix product sets only the rotation in the presenter's build (`LIBGTE_CONTROL_ONLY`): the readable renderers read the rotation, never the data registers or FLAG.
  - **Checked** on a local merge of this branch with 7d2's `14cb74e` and the marker (`.claude/worktrees/s7e-on-7d2`, scratch): it builds and links, `pres_link` passes (68 functions), and `--pres-check` on the demo to 12,000 gives 1,971 of 1,971 frames identical with and without `--native-draw` (`logs/m1`, `logs/m2` there). Without the marker the host and Vita builds' code, data and constants are byte-identical to the binaries the results below ran on.
- **Not done:** the other gamestates (7g's; they keep D30's pass); the presenter off the game thread (7f's). On the Vita a list whose head is outside the game's memory is walked in place (`walk.c`), so the presenter's list is walked on the game thread there until 7f gives it its own walk. LOD's game state isn't advanced by an in-between pass with the presenter (with draw distance on, the logic's LOD limiter sees one draw a tick, not two); with the default (`draw_distance` off) nothing reads it.
- **After 7d2's merge** (2026-11-09, this branch merged with main `3de4929`; logs `logs/s7e-m*`, gitignored): the presenter's renderers are 7d2's readable code, and the presenter draws at `fast_render` 2 or 3 (`pres_draw_available`; 3 is the Vita's default since D124, so a gate on 2 alone would have left `interp_mode=2` on D30's pass there). `tools/pres_check_ab.sh` takes `FR=N`.
  - `--pres-check`, 0 differences: at `fast_render=3` the demo (1,971 of 1,971 frames), wsi (1,971), gems (1,211), route A (2,475) and route B (2,019), the demo with `--native-draw` (1,971) and the native build's demo (1,971); at `fast_render=2` the demo (1,971). Every file of each pair byte-identical. `--interp-mode 2` at `fast_render=3`: the demo's 26 and gems' 19 files identical to D30's.
  - ctest: arena 28 of 29 and native 24 of 25 passed (`gte` skipped); `pres_link` 55 functions on the Vita's objects. `check_no_assets.sh` passes.
  - Vita3K (`realtime=0`, against main's VPK at `3de4929`): gems with the switch off, 17 of 17 shots, 17 of 17 captures, the card identical; gems at 16:9 60 fps with `interp_mode=2` against main's D30 frames, 17 of 17 shots, 17 of 17 captures, the card identical; the demo at 16:9 60 fps, 12 of 12 shots and the card identical, 4 of 11 captures (the others differ by a few hundred scattered pixels, the title among them, which the presenter doesn't draw; with the switch off gems' captures differed in 2 of 17 against main's the same way: Vita3K's capture timing, as `win-6500` before).
  - Vita3K crashed in its own code (`Unhandled access to 0xF200000000000000`) on some runs that day, on main's VPK and the pre-merge VPK as on this one (gems at 60 fps: main 1 of 2, the pre-merge VPK 1 of 2, this one 4 of 6 reached the end); each run that reached the end compared as above.
- **Results** (2026-11-08, branch on main `2e73e77`; logs under `logs/s7e*`, gitignored; the host runs on one frozen binary, `logs/s7e/bin-1`):
  - **`--pres-check`, record for record** (`tools/pres_check_ab.sh`, `MODE=chk`: each scenario with `--interp`, against the same with the check; every in-between frame in play compared, every file of the pair byte-identical):

Their traces, WAVs and cards are also byte-identical to the same runs with `--interp` off (host_checks' runs below): demo, wsi, routes A and B, the tour.

The first round's 60 fps gems runs stopped at vblank 6,600, before play (the opening cutscene runs to about 6,600), so they were run again to 8,768.

- **What it costs:** with `interp_mode` 0 (the default) nothing runs: the game's objects are unchanged, and the Vita ELF gains 47 KB of code (the draw path's copies: text 3,702,858 → 3,749,968 bytes) and 80 KB of `.bss` (the 61 KB mirror, the 16 KB buffer `config.txt` is pre-read into, LOD's two state copies). With `interp_mode=2` the area (2.25 MB) is allocated after the game's memory, and the presenter draws in play in place of D30's pass, on the game thread: no snapshot of the game's memory is taken or put back there, but the draw costs what it cost. Vita3K's timing can't judge it (D112); the game thread's time on hardware is 7f's measurement.

- **Size:** 1,825 lines added with comments, docs aside: `src/game/pres/` and `cmake/presenter.cmake` 1,040 (the snapshot, the context and its calls, the check, the names, the build), `present.c`, the switches and the two hunks 260, the tools and tests 530. D119's estimate was ~0.8–1.5k; the check, the audit and the inventory are most of the difference.
- **This PR:** `src/game/pres/` (new), `cmake/presenter.cmake` (new), `cmake/game.cmake`, `present.c`/`.h`, `libgte.c` (the control-only guard), the host's `main.c`, the Vita's `main.c`, `present_vita.c`, `vita.h`, `tools/pres_inventory.py`, `pres_snap_syms.py`, `pres_check_ab.sh`, `savestate_globals.py`, `tests/pres/`; two additive hunks in 7d2's files, agreed with the 7d2 session: `render_ctx_names.h` includes `pres/pres_snap_names.h`, and `render_ctx_check.c` calls `pres_snap_from_live()` where it sets the presenter's inputs; docs (this entry, `docs/native-plan.md`, `docs/presentation.md`, `docs/host-build.md`, `docs/translation-log.md`).
- **Next:** 7f, the presenter on its own core (its own command buffer and walk instance, the join before the next update); then 7g, the other gamestates in the presenter build and D30's pass retired. The owner decides whether `interp_mode=2` becomes the Vita's default before 7f (it saves the game thread nothing yet: the presenter still runs there).

## 2026-11-09 — stage 7f: the presenter on its own core

### D126. Stage 7f: with `interp_mode=2` the presenter's in-between frame in play is drawn on a thread of its own on the Vita (`pres_thread=1`, core 1), beside the logic draw, into a buffer the render thread runs where the game thread placed it in the command ring; the game thread joins it before the next update. Its output calls and its OT walk are its own, so it shares nothing with the logic draw but read-only data; the one shared write left, the moby renderers' face replacements, goes into a copy while it runs. On the host it runs inline, and every check is 7e's: D30's frames, record for record and file for file. After hardware runs 7F1–7F5 `interp_mode=2` is the Vita's default (the owner's decision, 2026-11-09)
- **What D119 asked** (its 7f): the presenter's frame recorded into its own command buffer and handed whole to the render thread; its leftover primitives walked with a second `PsxGpuWalk`; started after update N beside the logic draw N, on core 1 (or 3 after D71's check); joined before update N+1 (the update changes the level's animated bytes, and the wait keeps the frame exact with no copy); a frame not ready by its due vblank not shown (7c's rules); in play `snap_take`, `blend_state`, the pass and `snap_restore` off the game thread, and `interp_budget` gone for play. D30's pass stays the reference and the other gamestates' (7g). The prerequisite, the exits that stop the render thread first (D120, #103), was merged.
- **What 7e left on the game thread** (D125): the presenter's `DrawOTag`, `PutDrawEnv`, `PutDispEnv`, `DrawSync` and `port_present_note_2d` were the game's, so its list went through libgpu's GPU state (`activeDrawEnv`, `currentDispEnv`), present.c's hooks (the 2D notes, the substitutions, the capture) and the game thread's walk; and its records went into the game's other pool. (`snap_take`, `blend_state`, D30's pass and `snap_restore` already didn't run in play with `interp_mode=2`: 7e's presenter replaced them.)
- **Its own output** (`present.c`, `pres_names.h`): the five calls map onto `pres_*` versions that work on the presenter's own state: copies of the draw and display environments (taken when it starts, after the game's walk is joined), libgpu's `PutDispEnv` rule on its own `currentDispEnv`, its own 2D notes and substitutions (present.c's `squeeze_2d` split into a `squeeze` both use), the capture from its own draw environment, and a second walk (`gpu_walk.c`, D122's `PsxGpuWalk` with new fields): `own` (no VRAM calls, which D30's freeze dropped anyway; no check hooks; its own scene start, `psx_gpu_walk_own`: its pending splits, its isbg clear), `nd` (the records' mode of its list, which `pres_nd_list_linked` now sets instead of the game's `g_psx_walk_nd`), `nd_pool` (its records go to a 1 MB pool of its own, not the game's other pool, which the logic draw could write while it runs) and `out` (its draws, clears and vertex buffers, `PsxGpuOut`; NULL: the GR_* calls). `pres_link` now allows it no output of the game's: on the host's objects and the Vita's ARM objects (55 functions) every name it reaches is its own, read only, or shared code that names no writable data.
- **`currentDispEnv`:** 7e's presenter left libgpu's environment put last set to its own for the logic draw's `PutDispEnv` (which decides `activeDispEnv`). The presenter's `PutDispEnv` is now its own, so the game thread puts the same value when it starts the job: the display environment of the DB the draw goes to (the one `GamestateDraw` flips to; the play branch puts it once). The join compares it with what the presenter put ("display environments not the presenter's": 0 in every run).
- **The job** (`present.c` `presenter_pass`): the game thread takes the snapshot and the context after update N (`pres_draw_prepare`: 7e's `pres_draw_frame` without the draw), then:
  - **with a platform worker** (`g_present_pres`, the Vita), in play and not in an attract demo at 16:9 (whose 16:9 pass after it reads the presenter's visibility copies): it reserves the frame's slot and due vblank in present.c's queue (`reserve`, push_capture's arithmetic, so the queue is 7e's), places the buffer in the ring (`submit`), and posts the job (`GamestateDraw_pres`) to the worker. The logic draw N follows on the game thread. `interp_budget` doesn't apply in play then: the game thread's part is the snapshot;
  - **else** (the host, the Vita with `pres_thread=0` or with the vertex cache on: `persp` or `subpixel`, whose tables are the game thread's), inline at the same point through the GR_* calls (on the Vita its walk's direct ranges through `g_nd_ranges_walk`), and its capture is queued after it as in 7e;
  - **the join** (`port_present_pres_join`) at the main loop's top before the update and before a save state's point, when a state is loaded, before the next job, and at the Vita's own exits (`vita_exit`, before the render thread stops).
- **The face replacements** (the one write D123 found two draws could race on): the logic's moby renderers write a frame's replacement faces into the model's face list and put them back after the record (`ReplaceFaces`, `RestoreFaces`), and the presenter reads the same lists (its copy, and `PickFaces` reads a model's list directly for a record without swaps). Beside each other the presenter could draw the logic's replaced faces. So while the worker runs (`g_faces_copy`, `render_ctx.c`) the game's build replaces in a copy as the presenter's build does (`moby_hand.c`, three functions; with it off the code path is the old one); a list too long for the copy (64 KB) is still replaced in place, and counted (the `pres` line). The sector animations the logic draw writes into the level's data (D123's other write) don't race: both draws read the same animation bytes from before the draw, the presenter animates its own copy of any sector it draws whose animations are pending, and the animation writes are absolute values, the same from either. The host's `--faces-copy` keeps the copy on all run: every file must be the same. **The check that shows it** (the coordinator's ask): `--pres-check-thread` runs `--pres-check`'s presenter on a host thread of its own (`src/platform/linux32/pres_thread.c`; its list only recorded, never drawn, so the software rasterizer stays the game thread's) beside D30's pass and the logic draw, and compares its frame with D30's at the join, as the Vita's presenter runs beside the logic draw; `--faces-race` (diagnostic) leaves the face replacements in place meanwhile, as before the copy. It also counts the game's draws' records with replacements and the presenter's reads of a model whose faces the game's draw has replaced in place at that moment (`g_faces_swaps`, `g_pres_face_overlaps`, `g_pres_faces_busy` in `render_ctx.c`; set in the same three functions). Results (`logs/s7f/thr.sh`, `logs/s7f-thr-*`, binary `bin-6`):

  | Scenario | Faces | Frames identical | Records with replacements | Reads during a replacement |
  |---|---|---:|---:|---:|
  | gems | in a copy | 1,210 of 1,210 | 41 | 0 |
  | gems | in place (`--faces-race`) | 1,210 of 1,210 | 41 | 0 |
  | route A | in a copy | 2,474 of 2,474 | 65 | 0 |
  | route A | in place (`--faces-race`) | 2,474 of 2,474 | 65 | 0 |

  So the presenter on a thread of its own draws D30's frames, and the copy is cheap: replacements are rare (41 and 65 records in whole runs, a few a minute of play). But on these routes the race never happened: the presenter never read a model in the window the game's draw had it replaced, so `--faces-race` can't show a difference there. The race stays real by construction (the same model's list read and written at once, D123), the copy removes it, and the counter says whether a run reached it. (One frame fewer is compared than inline: the last tick's compare waits for a join the run's exit doesn't make.)
- **The Vita** (`gpu_thread.c`, `pres_thread.c`):
  - **The buffers:** three, each 256 KB of commands in the ring's format and a 65,536-vertex area. Job k's buffer is written once the render thread has finished with job k-2's (its vertices are then two vertex buffers back, the slabs' rule). The recording (`PsxGpuOut` and `PresentPresOut`'s hooks) makes the commands the ring's functions made: `C_TEX`, `C_ENV` (the presenter's display state, before its first clip or capture, as `env_sync` sent it), `C_CLIP`, `C_BLEND`, `C_DRAW`, `C_CLEAR`, `C_BEGIN` (with `present_due=1`), `C_CAPTURE`, `C_COMMIT`, and `C_PVERTS` (its vertex area; not counted as a slab). A buffer too big is run empty, its frame never filed (counted).
  - **The ring:** the game thread's `C_BUF` (job number, the frame's due vblank, and the display state the presenter leaves, which the game thread also takes as sent) comes before the logic draw's commands. The render thread runs the buffer whole when it gets there; the commit inside counts at the `C_BUF`, so 7c's count of frames filed stays right. Not closed yet: it waits as with nothing to run (due frames shown meanwhile, a millisecond at most at a time); with `present_due=1` a buffer whose frame is a vblank or more past its due is passed by and its frame never shown (7c's rule), the real frame after it is. At `realtime=0` it always waits, so scripted runs don't depend on the threads.
  - **A slot never filled:** the frame's slot is reserved when the job starts, so a buffer passed by, too big, or with no capture leaves a slot in present.c's queue that holds an older picture. The render thread marks the slot at the `C_BUF` and clears it at a `C_COMMIT` into it; a `C_PRESENT` of a slot still marked (the presents posted from VSync: `realtime=0`, `present_due=0`, HD shots aside) keeps the picture on screen instead, and is counted.
  - **The thread:** `spyro_pres`, core 1 at priority 0x9A (behind the parse thread's 0x98, so the logic's list goes first), or core 3 with `pres_core=3` when D71's start-up check allows it. Its line, every report: frames on the worker and inline, its busy ms per vblank, the game thread's wait at the join and in how many joins, the buffers the render thread ran and passed by and its wait for them, waits for a free buffer, buffers too big, display environments not the presenter's, frames uncaptured, face lists replaced in place.
  - **Not routed:** the records' converter's counters (`nd_report`'s) are bumped from both walks without a lock, so on the Vita they may lose counts while the worker runs (statistics only).
- **Found in review before the results** (a reviewer agent on the first commit): the presenter's walk had no output set, so on the worker its draws went to the game thread's command ring through the GR_* calls, racing the game thread's and the parse thread's writes. Fixed before any of the runs below (`s_pw.out` is the buffer's on the worker). The first Vita3K round, on the unfixed VPK, is discarded. The same review asked for the slot guard above and a sequentially consistent close.

- **What it costs:** with `interp_mode` 0 (the default) nothing runs: no thread, no buffers. The Vita ELF gains 8.5 KB of code and 235 KB of `.bss` (the face copy in the game's moby builds, 64 KB each, and the presenter's substitutions); with `interp_mode=2` the three buffers (3 × 1.5 MB), the walk's buffers (about 1.9 MB) and the records' pool (1 MB) come from the heap. On the worker, the game thread's part per tick is the snapshot and the context (0.36–0.48 ms per tick in Vita3K's play windows, against 1.0–1.3 ms for D30's pass there), plus the face copies in the logic draw.

The game thread's wait at the join: 0.000–0.027 ms per vblank. The worker's busy time, 2.6–7.2 ms per vblank, is its wall time, which includes core 1's other threads and, in Toasty, its waits for a free buffer (2,420): there the render thread is the wall (16.6 ms a picture, as with D30's pass, 7F2), so the worker runs two jobs ahead of it. The worker takes the demos only once 7g (or a 16:9 pass of its own) removes D30's 16:9 pass after it.

The hardware runs above are on the VPK from before the merge.
- **The default (the owner's decision, 2026-11-09, after 7F1–7F5: "make it the default"): `interp_mode=2` on the Vita,** with `pres_thread=1` as before. The owner took it without the repeat of 7F2/7F3, so the game-over windows above stay unexplained.
  - **How:** `g_vita_opts.interp_mode` starts at 2 (`src/platform/vita/main.c`), so an `interp_mode=` line still wins (0 for D30's pass). `config_wants_presenter`, which reads `config.txt` before the arena is set up, now starts from the default and takes the last `interp_mode=` line, as `read_config` does; with no file or no line it reserves the presenter's area. The host's default stays 0, so `host_checks.sh`, the scenarios and the oracle replay run as before.
  - **What it costs:** what `interp_mode=2` cost above (the 2.25 MB area, the three buffers, the walk's buffers and the records' pool, about 9.6 MB of heap, and the presenter's thread) is now taken at every start, whether or not 60 fps is on. 60 fps itself stays off by default (D44); with it off the thread waits and nothing else runs.
  - **Run configs:** 7F2 and 7F4 gain `interp_mode=0` so they stay D30's runs; the earlier runs in `tools/vita/fps-runs/` with `interp=1` ran D30's pass, and repeating one now needs that line (its README says so).

All four reached `frames=` on the first try. Not run on hardware with the new default itself: 7F3 and 7F5 ran the same code with `interp_mode=2` set in their configs.
- **Then:** 7g (the other gamestates in the presenter build, D30's pass retired) builds on this.

D119's estimate was ~0.6–1k.

## 2026-11-09 — the register-file renderer builds removed

### D127. The renderers' register-file builds leave both builds: `fast_render` is 3 (the readable renderers, D124) or, in the arena build, 0 (the mips2c units), and 3 is the host's default too. `fast_render` 1 and 2, `ngte_fx`, the `_ngte`, `_ndr` and `_rc` files, NgteSync's register-file half, `--render-poison` and `--gte-audit` are gone, and `--fast-render-check` compares the hand C with the mips2c unit. Every check's output is main's
- **What D124 left** (its "Next"): with `fast_render=3` the Vita's default, the register-file builds and every `#if !NGTE_RC` block can leave the native build, which is where D119's "mostly deletions" happens. The coordinator's brief: take them out of the native and Vita builds, keep what the oracle replay needs, make 3 the host's default unless the inventory says not to.
- **The owner's choice (2026-11-09, through the coordinator: "(B) Both builds (Recommended)"):** out of the arena build too, over keeping `fast_render` 1 and 2 there. The inventory below found that nothing the oracle replay runs needs them, and keeping them in the arena build would have kept the register-file code in the sources (the native build would only have stopped compiling it), so the line count D119 expected would not have come.
- **The inventory** (main `213e808`):
  - **Only for `fast_render` 1 and 2:** the seven `NAME_hand_ngte.c` (2) and three `NAME_hand_ndr.c` (2 with `ngte_fx=0`) wrappers; in the ten B2 files (the environment, mobys, Spyro, sky, shadows, flame, particles, the moby culls, the sky's clipping, the animation update) every `#if NGTE`/`#if !NGTE`/`#if NGTE_MATH`/`#if !NGTE_RC` branch for the register file; `render_hand.h`'s register-file NgteSync (the recorders, `ngte_flush_r/c/s`), `hand_spill_regs`, `NGTE_TAIL`, the `fast_render` 1/2 arms of `HAND_PICK`, `HAND_PICK_UNIT`, `HAND_UNIT2` and `HAND_CHECK_UNIT`; `hand_check.c`'s `hand_check_unit` (1 and 2 against the unit) and `--gte-audit` (the register reads of 1's build); `render_rc.c`'s `--render-poison` (D124's audit of 1 and 2); `g_ngte_fx` (`mips2c_rt.c`, the host's `--ngte-fx`, the Vita's `ngte_fx=`); the host's and the Vita's `fast_render` 1 and 2. The ten `NAME_hand_rc.c` wrappers existed only because the same source also built 1 and 2.
  - **What the oracle replay needs** (`oracle_replay.c`): the mips2c units and their entries (`NAME_gen`, `NAME_gen__m2c`, `m2c_index.c`), `gteRegs` as the arena build's record, and at `fast_render=3` D124's render shadow (`render_shadow`, `hand_check.c`: the unit, then the readable build, the unit's state where nothing was compared). None of it is `fast_render` 1 or 2: the replay at 3 runs the shadow by default.
  - **What the checks need as a reference:** `--fast-render-check` at 3 compared each call with `fast_render=1`'s build. That build was itself held to the unit since D72 (the oracle replay, `hand_check_unit`, the host runs byte-identical to `fast_render=0`), and the render shadow already compares the readable build with the unit on the same terms, so the check now takes the unit as its reference (`hand_check_rc(name, NAME_gen__m2c, hand)`): RAM but the scratchpad, the spill area and the stack below `$sp`, the control registers but FLAG. The B1 functions of D96 (`HAND_UNIT_FN`) keep their whole-state check against the unit (`hand_check`). The native build links no unit, so it has no `--fast-render-check` any more (D124 had already found its snapshot misses the data labels there).
  - **`--render-ctx-check` and 7e's `--pres-check`** don't depend on any of it: the presenter check runs the logic's build (now the readable code itself) beside `NAME_pres`; the pres check gates on `fast_render` 2 or 3 (`pres_draw.c`, 7f's file, left as it is: 2 can no longer be set).
- **What changed:**
  - **The ten B2 files** are the readable code only: `unifdef -DNGTE=1 -DNGTE_MATH=1 -DNGTE_RC=1` over each, then by hand the four expressions that named `NGTE_RC` (the environment's and the lit mobys' loops to their last step, the regular mobys' two colour fades), the `hand_spill_regs` calls, and the files' header comments. Each defines `RENDER_READABLE` before `render_hand.h`, which keeps D124's compile-time ban: in these files any GTE data register, FLAG, GTE command, `g_mips_sp` or `D_80077DD8` fails to compile or link. The `_pres` wrappers lose their `NGTE_RC` line; `src/game/pres/{moby_cull,sky_clip}.c` (7f's) still define it, now unused, and their `RENDER_CTX_READABLE=0` branch is dead, as before.
  - **`render_hand.h`:** NgteSync is D124's readable half only (what still shows: V0 after the last vertex, the screen words NCLIP's winding takes, the last colour command's far colour; the recording calls stay as no-ops in the sources); the entries are `HAND_RENDERER`, `HAND_UNIT_FN(_V0)` and `HAND_UNIT_FN_RC(_V0)`, each with the presenter's (`NAME_pres`), the native build's and the arena build's forms. The arena build's C entries store the stack arguments for the unit through `hand_args_push`/`hand_args_pop`, defined before the ban takes `g_mips_sp`'s name away.
  - **The checks:** `hand_check.c` compares the unit with the hand C (`hand_check`, `hand_check_rc`, `--particle-sweep` with either; the log lines say "the mips2c unit" and "the hand C"); `render_rc.c` is `g_render_work` alone.
  - **The switches:** `mips2c_rt.c`'s `g_fast_render` is 3; the host's `--fast-render` takes 0 or 3 (0 refused in the native build, as before) and refuses 1 and 2 with a line saying why; `--ngte-fx`, `--render-poison`, `--render-poison-bite` and `--gte-audit` are gone. The Vita's `fast_render=` line gives 3 for anything but 0, and 0 only in the arena build; an `ngte_fx=` line is logged as an unknown key.
  - **The native build's hand stack** (`port.c`'s `s_hand_stack`) goes: no code stores below `$sp` there now, so `g_mips_sp` stays 0. The spill area `D_80077DD8` is still a data label of the game's, but nothing writes it in the native build.
  - **Tools and docs:** `fast_render_ab.sh` compares 0 with 3; `native_draw_ab.sh` runs at 3 and loses `MODE=poison`; `pres_check_ab.sh`'s note; `savestate_globals.py` (`env_hand.c:s_proj`, no `g_ngte_fx`, no `s_hand_stack`); `native_audit.py` counts the renderer files as B1 and lists no `_ngte` file; `cmake/mips2c.cmake`'s comments; `docs/host-build.md`, `docs/vita-testing.md`, `docs/native-plan.md`, `docs/translation-log.md`.
- **The checks** (logs gitignored, under `logs/cleanup/`; "main" is `213e808` built from a copy of its tree):

  - **`--render-ctx-check`** (`native_draw_ab.sh MODE=rc`, demo, gems, wsi, lod, vc, routes A and B): 0 differences and 0 writes to the game's memory in every call; the call counts D124's (demo 31,778 ... routeB 35,795); every file identical to the run without the check (`m-rc.txt`, `m-*-rc/`).

  - **7e's `--pres-check`** (`pres_check_ab.sh MODE=chk`, demo, wsi, gems, routes A and B): 8,647 frames compared (demo 1,971, wsi 1,971, gems 1,211, routeA 2,475, routeB 2,019), every one identical, 0 differ; every file identical to the run without it (`p2-chk.txt`, `p2-*`).

A config with `fast_render=2` and `ngte_fx=1` logs `config: unknown key ngte_fx` and `fast_render=3` and runs its 900 frames to a clean exit (`v3k-br-fr2line/spyro.log`).

## 2026-11-09 — stage 7g, part 1: every gamestate in the presenter

### D128. Stage 7g, part 1: the presenter build holds every gamestate's draw, the overlays' included, and with `interp_mode=2` it draws the in-between frame in every gamestate and the attract demos' 16:9 frame. Every one is D30's pass's record for record on the host (by gamestate), with it on every file is D30's, and the presenter writes none of the game's memory. On the Vita the in-between frame goes to its thread in every gamestate but the credits' first stage. D30's pass stays the reference and `interp_mode` 0's; D129 retires it
- **What D119 asked** (its 7g): in D104's coverage order (credits, title, cutscenes, pause and inventory, transitions, dragon, balloonist, fairy, exits), each gamestate's draw function in the presenter build, the overlays included; then D30's pass, the arena snapshot, `pass_vsync`, `g_psx_spu_frozen` and `g_psx_vram_frozen` go (D129); the HUD's 16:9 narrowing out of the OT's primitives (done ahead, D122); the attract demos' 16:9 pass onto 7f's thread if it can be. A gamestate left without the presenter would need a switch and the owner's decision; none is.
- **The inventory** (`tools/pres_inventory.py BUILD --all-gamestates`, which now follows every branch of `GamestateDraw` and reads the overlays' objects): the other gamestates reach 102 functions that name writable data, in eleven more places than play's: the rest of `draw.c`, `credits_hand.c` (`func_credits_8007BFD0`), `completion.c` (the inventory's percentage), `spu.c` (`PlaySound` and two helpers: the flight results play sounds from their draw), libc's `rand` (`PlaySound`'s pitch), the title overlay's `TitlescreenDraw`, and three overlay functions the draws call through a pointer the level sets (`overlay_pointers.c`): the flight results' draw `D_8007567C` (`Flight5_LEVEL_*`), their time `D_800758C4` (`Flight4_LEVEL_*`), the balloonist's talk `D_800758D8` (`Balloonist4_LEVEL_*`). The inventory and the link check found the first; the pointers turned up in the presenter object's indirect calls (below).
- **The presenter build** (`cmake/presenter.cmake`, `src/game/pres/`): `spu.c`, `completion.c` and `credits_hand.c` join the two object libraries, and a third compiles the overlays' preprocessed sources again (`gen/ovl/titlescreen_code/titlescreen.c`, `LEVEL_{15,25,35,45,55}/flight.c`, the ten balloon levels' `balloonist.c`), all with `-include pres_names.h`. `objcopy` also renames `LoadImage` to `pres_LoadImage`: `libgpu.h` `#undef`s `LoadImage` (a Windows guard), so the pause screens' calls in `draw.c` kept the game's name.
  - **The overlay pointers** (`pres_ovl.c`, in the presenter build): `pres_names.h` turns a call through `D_8007567C`, `D_800758C4` or `D_800758D8` into one through a pointer `pres_flight5`, `pres_flight4` or `pres_balloon4` returns, the presenter's copy of the same level's function. Which level's is picked on the game thread from the game's pointer (`pres_draw.c` `ovl_pick`); a pointer that is none of them draws nothing and is counted (the host's `pres:` line; none seen).
  - **The sounds:** `PlaySound` and its helpers run on the snapshot's copies of `g_Spu` and the rand seed (`D_80075AC0`), as D30's pass ran them on the game's and put them back; `SpuSetVoiceAttr`, their one SPU write, is dropped, as libspu dropped it under D30's freeze. `rand` is `pres_rand`, rand_psyq.c's generator on the copy.
  - **The snapshot's labels** (`pres_snap_names.h`): 106 names, 70 more than 7e's 36: the pause, inventory and flight-results screens' state, the transitions', the respawn's and game over's, the dragons', fairy's and balloonist's, the title's and cutscenes', the credits' letters, the level's environment (the credits set its culling distance) and `g_Buffers`, and the tables the game sets once. `g_Buffers`' five buffer pointers are rebased onto the presenter's buffers and copies: the flight results, the pause screens and the exits draw from the lower buffer up and put the HUD's mobys under the HUD OT by them. `--pres-audit` (now over every label) found three more words pointing into the moby array: `g_DragonCutscene`'s cutscene Spyro, dragon and rescued dragon (+0x88, +0x8C, +0x90), now rebased. It also lists `g_Spu`'s active sounds' mobys, which no draw reaches with a moby (the flight results play sounds with none), and words in the particle space pointing into it outside the play gamestates, which no frame's list showed a difference for.
  - **The whole data region each frame:** 7e copied only the named labels into the mirror, which left zeros in between. The game reads past a table's end into the next label: the title's border, `g_TitlescreenAnimIn[m_SubTick - 16]` (the first check run's one title difference, a y of −119 for 105), and the rescued dragon's name, which crashed the first runs in `strlen(NULL)`. Now the mirror is the whole region, 61 KB a frame (`pres_snap.c` `mirror_take`).
  - **A guard for labels outside it:** seven of the names were the decomp's `strings.c` tables, which in the port are C data at addresses of their own, so their copies were zeros (the second crash's cause). They are now the game's own, read only (`link_check.py`), and the generated table carries each label's own symbol (weak): `pres_snap_misplaced()` names the first that isn't where the mirror copies from, and the presenter then doesn't draw (`pres_draw_available`; logged by both hosts).
- **The link check** (`tests/pres/link_check.py`): no stop list now; it also follows the overlay functions `pres_ovl.c`'s tables hold, and refuses an indirect call (x86 `call *`, ARM `blx` through a register) anywhere but the three dispatches. That rule is what found `D_800758C4` and `D_800758D8` (the first run passed by name while two draws called the game's overlay functions through snapshot copies of their pointers). Read only now also: the kernel RAM base the port's small addresses go to, the `strings.c` tables, `strcpy`, `strlen`. 121 functions reached on the host's objects.
- **`present.c`:**
  - the presenter draws the in-between frame in every gamestate with `interp_mode=2` (and with `--pres-check`, beside D30's pass);
  - the attract demos' 16:9 real frame (the two-draw form: the logic draw stays the original 4:3 one, hidden) is the presenter's too, unblended (`pres_draw_prepare`'s blend 0), from the 16:9 visibility copies the in-between frame left, inline, queued after the logic draw as D30's 16:9 pass's was; `--pres-check` compares it with D30's 16:9 pass;
  - inline, libgpu's environment put last is the presenter's own last one after its frame, as D30's pass left it (7f set the DB's before the draw, which is the same in every draw that puts one);
  - on the worker (`files_a_frame`): the slot is reserved and that environment set before the draw runs, so the draw must file a frame and put the DB's environment. Every gamestate's draw ends with `PutDispEnv(&g_CurDB->m_DispEnv)` and a `DrawOTag` except the credits' first stage (`g_CreditsStage == -1`), which only copies the last picture into the other buffer: D30's pass filed no frame there, so the presenter draws it inline, where none is filed either. The host's runs count both per gamestate (`disp_miss_gs`, `uncaptured_gs`): none.
- **The check** (`pres_check.c`): the counts by gamestate (a "by gamestate" line), and a gouraud line's later colour code bytes left out like a gouraud polygon's: the pause screens' `LINE_G2` frames (`func_8001844C`) never set them, the PS1 GPU ignores them, and they hold what the buffer held before (every pause and inventory frame differed in that one byte, 0x07 against 0x00, before).
- **The presenter's `VSync`** (`pres_draw.c`): D30's `pass_vsync` returned the clock + 1000 for every call, so the draws' "two vblanks since the last frame" loops end at once. Each `VSync(0)` now moves the presenter's own count on one, as a vblank would: on the flight results' first tick the draw sets the pacer from `VSync(-1)` and then waits for two vblanks after it (`flight.c`'s tail runs after both its branches), which a count that never moves waits for forever. Neither D30's pass nor the in-between frame draws that tick (a gamestate's first tick has no in-between frame); D129's check, which draws every tick, hung there. The draws use `VSync`'s value for the pacer alone, so no frame changes.
- **Two host checks the by-name link check can't give:**
  - `--pres-write-check` (`src/platform/linux32/pres_watch.c`): around each presenter frame drawn inline the game's memory is copied (the block, the labels' section in the native build, the scratchpad, the kernel RAM) and compared after: any byte changed is a write through a pointer, or by a name the check takes as read only;
  - `--pres-check-late`: `--pres-check` with the presenter's list recorded at the join, after the logic draw, on the game thread. A frame that depends on anything the logic draw writes, which on the Vita it may read while the logic draw runs, then differs from D30's.
- **Results** (2026-11-09; logs under `logs/s7g*`, gitignored; the host's runs on frozen binaries: main `feb72d5`'s `logs/s7g/bin-main` for the references, this branch's `logs/s7g/bin-5`, which differs from the final code only in what the host never reaches (`files_a_frame`, the Vita's worker) or what no frame shows (`pres_VSync`'s count, above); `--interp --native-draw` throughout; the scenarios `tools/pres_check_ab.sh`'s, with `pause` and `die` new, and `flight`, the gems script with the menu's level warp to Sunny Flight at 7,600, `--cheat 7600:warp:15`, where Spyro drowns and the results come up):

Up to each break the branch's shots were main's (41 and 43 shots). With captures the 16:9 demos ran through on the first try.

- **The attract demos' 16:9 frame on 7f's thread** (D119's last 7g item): it can't go there as it is. Two things tie it to the game thread:
  - its frame is filed after the logic draw (D30's 16:9 pass's was pushed there), and in a demo the logic draw waits a vblank or two (`D_80075784`'s extra `VSync(0)` and the pacer), so its due vblank is only known then; 7f's worker reserves its frame's slot and due vblank and places its buffer in the ring before the logic draw. The render thread would have to take the frame from the buffer's `C_BUF` filled in after the logic draw (or the buffer's `C_BEGIN` and `C_COMMIT` patched), with its commands still before the logic draw's;
  - with 60 fps the in-between frame's visibility state is the 16:9 frame's start, and the snapshot is one: both on the worker would need a second copy of the presenter's state (or the two frames as one job, the unblended state saved before the blend).
  Today both frames are drawn inline (7F5 measured the in-between one there: the game thread 40.1% → 46.1% busy in the Stone Hill demo at 16:9, no late vblank); D30's 16:9 pass is gone from it either way. A follow-up, if the demos at 16:9 ever run late.
- **What it costs:** with `interp_mode` 0 nothing runs. The Vita ELF's `.text` 3,709,318 → 3,819,974 bytes (+108 KB: the other gamestates' draws and the overlays' copies; `.data` +16 bytes, `.bss` unchanged). The snapshot copies the 61 KB data region a frame where 7e copied its labels, about 4 KB: on the game thread, a fraction of the 0.38 ms 7F5 measured for the snapshot and context.
- **Hardware:** none run (the owner's yes first). The runs proposed in D129 cover this too: 7g's VPK holds both parts.
- **Size:** 682 lines added and 99 removed in the code, tests and tools, docs aside: `src/game/pres/` and `cmake/presenter.cmake` 358 and 25 (the names, `pres_ovl.c`, the snapshot's whole region and guard, the rebases, the build), `present.c`/`.h` 105 and 49, the checks and their switches (`pres_watch.c`, `pres_thread.c`'s late mode, `link_check.py`, `pres_check.c`, `pres_check_ab.sh`, `pres_inventory.py`, the host's `main.c`) 209 and 20. D119's estimate for the whole of 7g was ~0.8–1.5k.
- **This PR:** `src/game/pres/` (`pres_ovl.c` new; `pres_names.h`, `pres_snap_names.h`, `pres_snap.c`/`.h`, `pres_draw.c`/`.h`, `pres_check.c`), `cmake/presenter.cmake`, `src/game/present.c`/`.h`, `tools/pres_snap_syms.py`, the Vita's `present_vita.c` (its log line), the host's `main.c`, `pres_thread.c` and `pres_watch.c` (new), `tests/pres/link_check.py`, `tools/pres_check_ab.sh`, `tools/pres_inventory.py`; docs (this entry, `docs/native-plan.md`, `docs/presentation.md`, `docs/host-build.md`, `docs/translation-log.md`).

## 2026-11-09 — stage 7g, part 2: D30's pass retired

### D129. Stage 7g, part 2: D30's pass goes, with its arena snapshot, `pass_vsync`, `g_psx_spu_frozen`, `g_psx_vram_frozen` and stage 7a's audits of it. The presenter draws every frame the logic draw doesn't, always (`interp_mode` is gone), and `--pres-check` now holds it to the logic draw's own frame: from the same snapshot unblended, record for record, every tick. Every file is main's D30 frames'; the native plan's 7g is done, and with it "no vblank emulation driving the logic"
- **What D119 asked** (its 7g, after D128): once no gamestate needs D30's pass, the pass goes with the arena snapshot, `pass_vsync`, `g_psx_spu_frozen` and `g_psx_vram_frozen` (once `g_psyxVramFrozen`, D115); the presenter build drops those calls by name; the HUD's 16:9 narrowing out of the OT's primitives (done ahead, D122); `present.c` `narrow_x` removed if dead.
- **What went** (`src/game/present.c` 1,963 → 1,538 lines):
  - D30's pass (`presentation_pass`), its region snapshot of the game's memory (the scratchpad, the data region, the moby array, the particle space, the sectors' cache bytes, the GTE registers, libgpu's environments, `$v1`: `snap_take`, `snap_restore`), the live-state blend (`blend_state`) and the 16:9 pass's visibility hand-in (`wide_state_in`);
  - `pass_vsync` (the presenter's `VSync` is `pres_VSync`, `pres_draw.c`, as since 7e);
  - libspu's freeze (`g_psx_spu_frozen`, `g_psx_spu_dropped`, `PASS_GUARD` in six calls) and libgpu's (`g_psx_vram_frozen` in `ClearImage`, `LoadImage`, `StoreImage`, `MoveImage`, and the walk's copy of it at the handover, `psx_walk_vram_frozen`): their one setter was the pass. The presenter build never makes these calls: `pres_names.h` maps the VRAM calls onto no-ops and `SpuSetVoiceAttr` onto `pres_ovl.c`'s (D125, D128);
  - the debug switches that measured the pass: `--present-dirty` (its pages), `--present-audit` (stage 7a's per-gamestate costs and HUD check, D104; `order_table.c`'s hook), `--present-reads` (7a's read audit: `readset.c`, `tools/present_reads.py`, `tools/present_audit.sh`), and the stats they filled;
  - `interp_mode`: the host's `--interp-mode` and the Vita's line (now logged as an unknown key), `g_present.interp_mode`, and the Vita's start-up read of `config.txt` for it: the presenter's area is always allocated (`pres_draw_want()` before `psx_arena_init`, 2.25 MB after the game's memory, on both hosts), and its thread always started (with `pres_thread=1`, the default).
- **What stays:** `narrow_x` isn't dead: since D122 it narrows the walk's copies of the 2D primitives (`squeeze`), and nothing edits the OT's primitives in place (the only writes in `squeeze` are into its pool). `g_psyxVSyncHook` stays for `--present-stats`' timing of the logic draw's waits. The blend (`blend_on`), the previous tick (`PortPresentBeforeUpdate`), the 16:9 visibility copies (`wide_state_out`), the frame queue and `interp_budget` (for a presenter drawing inline) stay.
- **Without the presenter** (a build or configuration that has none: the moved arena, `-DSPYRO_ARENA_AT` or the Vita's `-DSPYRO_NATIVE_MEM=OFF`, D97; `fast_render` 0, the arena build's mips2c units; a label outside the data region, D128): there is no in-between frame, and the attract demos at 16:9 show the logic draw's 4:3 frame. D30's pass drew both there before. None of these is a shipped configuration; the Vita's VPK has the presenter.
- **`--pres-check` against the logic draw** (D30's pass was its reference): each tick the presenter also draws from the snapshot unblended, with the logic draw's projection and the game's visibility state, and its list is compared with the logic draw's record for record (`present.c`, `pres_check.c`). The logic draw is the game's own code on the same state, so the two must be the same; with or without `--interp` and `--widescreen`, its frame isn't shown and every file is the same as without the check. `--pres-check-thread` and `--pres-check-late` (D128) record the presenter's list beside the logic draw and after it. ctest's `pres_demo` now asks for 2,000 ticks (the title and the demo's play to vblank 5,000).

### D130. 60 Hz logic, the pilot: the 60 fps option gets a third value, 60 Hz (the Vita's `interp=4`, the host's `--tick60`), read at start, which runs every play update in one vblank through a second compile of the update path. Game state keeps the original's units, so off and interpolated run today's code (11 of 11 touched files preprocess to main's tokens; 279 of 279 host files, the oracle replay, `--pres-check` as main). Artisans and its five levels' moby classes were judged by hand: 59 of 64 bodies at 60 Hz, 172 changed lines, about a quarter of the audit's estimate per class. The owner decides on the other 152 classes
- **Why:** the owner chose to start the pilot that `docs/logic-60hz-audit.md` (committed with this PR) §7 proposed after stage 7. The faithful modes must stay exactly as they are; the 60 Hz mode may differ, and nothing can check it tick for tick (§4).
- **The design** (`docs/tick60.md` has all of it):
  - **Only play ticks at 60 Hz**, outside the attract demos. The menus, cutscenes, transitions, title and credits stay at 30 Hz, as do the demos (§7.2); with `interp=4` they keep the in-between frame. That keeps the pilot to the systems §5 named.
  - **Units stay the original's.** A counter counts 30 Hz ticks, a speed is per 30 Hz tick, and each vblank applies its share. A tick's first vblank takes its once-a-tick steps; its second ends it. So the rule sets hand over at any gamestate change, a class can run at either rate, a save state loads into either, and an unjudged class runs exactly the original's code every other vblank. This is what makes the pilot partial: the 31 other levels, the particles and the HUD run at 30 Hz inside a 60 Hz update, at the right speed.
  - **Sites are macros, not `#if` hunks** where they can be (`src/compat/tick60_sites.h`: `T60_TICK_DO`, `T60_TICK_END_DO`, `T60_TICK_AND`, `T60_SHARE`, `T60_SHARE8`). At 30 Hz each one is its argument's own tokens, so a one-line change at the site keeps the original's preprocessed text; `tools/tick60_cpp_check.py` proves it against main, file by file. The audit's `#if SPYRO_TICK60 … #else … #endif` form is used where new code was needed.
  - **The second compile** (`cmake/tick60.cmake`, `tools/tick60_link.py`) is the presenter's pattern with one difference: its functions are made local but `GamestateUpdate_60`, its data definitions are weakened so the game's own win, and any writable static of its own is refused unless listed (two: the rules' scratch, `camera_hand.c`'s `s_tilt`, set and cleared within one call). It found `s_tilt` on the first link.
  - **The dispatch:** `main.c` calls `PortGamestateUpdate` and `PortTickDelta` (0012). The play draw's two-vblank pacer is shortened through its count of the last flip, so `draw.c` (and the presenter's copy) is untouched. `present.c` draws no in-between frame after a 60 Hz update. Save states carry a `T60 ` chunk (the mode, which a load switches to as §7.2 asks, the half tick, Spyro's `>> 6` carry).
  - **Boot-time, not at a level load** (§7.1's fallback). With units preserved the carried-over list should be short, but it isn't checked.
- **What was converted:**
  - **Spyro:** `m_airTime` (×2), `m_floorIdleTime`, `m_noGamepadUpdateFrames` count 30 Hz ticks. The dust, trail puffs and drowning sink come once a tick. The scripted move by `unk_0x208` is shared. The flight-ceiling follow is shared. `func_8003FE40`'s `>> 6` carries the first half's low bits into the second, so a tick moves him exactly what the original's shift of the sum does.
  - **The camera:** the off-centre and hidden counters, the recentre timer and look mode's speeds.
  - **The moby helpers:** the 10 speed sites (7 changed; the circle move, the wander and the path walker pass their speed to helpers that already scale, and at 3 or 4 the original scales twice there). Also the wander's counters and roll, the path walker's direct turns, the flame heat, the dragon fragment and `MobyMoveWithGravity`.
  - **The animation step:** half a step a vblank, at most one frame a tick, as the original's.
  - **The classes:** four subagents judged 71 classes (64 bodies, ~7,400 lines) from one brief, each edit checked mechanically. The review here made the end-of-tick order a macro and converted `MobyMoveWithGravity`, which moved six classes from 30 to 60.
  - **Verdicts:** 31 bodies at 60 with 141 sites (90 edits, 172 changed lines), 28 at 60 with nothing to mark, 5 at 30 (`func_8003BFC0`'s frame-counted path in three; two scripts). Per class, `docs/tick60.md`.
- **The cost per class:** 2.2 sites and 2.7 changed lines per body on average, and nothing in 28 of 64. Extrapolated to 218 classes, ~700–800 changed lines, against §5's 2,000–4,000 for the moby AI. The judging took the four batches ~30 minutes in parallel (~0.6 M tokens). The expensive part stays the checking: no reference, only statistics and play.
- **Results** (2026-11-10, linux32 host, logs gitignored under `logs/t60/`; main's binary built from `bb62f38` in this worktree):
  - **Off:**
    - `tools/host_checks.sh`: 279 of 279 files byte-identical to main's (`logs/t60-{base,head}-*`).
    - Oracle replay (`s8e-cap`): 75 PASS, 0 FAIL, 20 NOEXIT on both, summaries identical.
    - `tools/tick60_cpp_check.py`: 11 of 11.
    - ctest: 28 passed, `gte` skipped, of 29, as on main. `tools/check_no_assets.sh`: exit 0.
  - **Interpolated:** `pres_check_ab.sh MODE=chk` against main's binary: 31,229 of 31,229 frames identical over demo, wsi, gems and routes A and B, and 183 of 183 files byte-identical to main's runs. With `--tick60 --interp --pres-check --widescreen` (gems), 5,763 of 5,763, 2,420 of them 60 Hz play frames.
  - **60 Hz, Spyro** (`tools/oracle/pads/tick60_moves.txt`, open loop):
    - On open ground the 30 Hz and 60 Hz paths stay 72 units apart on average over 312 vblanks.
    - Jumps gain 547 against 545 units (standing) and 788 against 778 (running).
    - **Running along a wall is ~12% slower.** At 60 Hz the collision updates the wall contact between the two sub-steps the 30 Hz tick shares; not fixed.
  - **60 Hz, mobys** (a tour standing 1,800 vblanks in each of the six levels; `tools/tick60_stats.py`): per class, moved, turned, state changes and animation frames per second are 60/30 = 1.00 ± 0.05 for nearly every class.
    - The outliers are Sparx's summed height (1.1–1.7: Spyro's few-unit floor zig-zag at 60 Hz, which Sparx follows), the Bull's clamped turn (1.20) and a respawner's height (1.4, few samples).
    - None is the 2× or 0.5× of a missed site.
  - **The game thread on the host:** the update per second of play ×1.74 (468 µs a 30 Hz update, 406 µs a 60 Hz one). The draw runs twice as often a second, which dominates, as §7.3 expected.
  - **Vita:** the VPK builds and links (the link check passes on ARM); `.text` +204 KB (3,814,684 → 4,018,820). Not run in Vita3K: it was busy with another project's queued runs.
- **Hardware: T60-1 to T60-3 ran on 2026-11-10** (the owner's yes in chat). They used the VPK `8c51c866…` with `REALTIME=1` and `DEVICE_DEFAULTS=1`; the kit's lock was free. Every run reached `frames=` with no crash dump. Logs are under `logs/hw-t60-*`, gitignored. Full tables are in `docs/tick60.md`.
  - **T60-2, the Toasty route at `interp=1`, against 7G1:** the same within noise. Game ms a vblank 3.30 → 3.31, renderers 1.97 → 1.98, late per 300 1.12 → 1.16, Toasty busy 24.9 → 25.0%. The compiled-in rules cost nothing when off.
  - **T60-1, Toasty at `interp=4`:** game ms a vblank 4.14, renderers 2.89, late 1.02 per 300, no fall to 30. Toasty is **48.2% busy** (max 62), with no late vblank. 7,233 updates at 60 Hz, 7 of them late.
  - **T60-3, the K route at `interp=4`, against 7G2:** game ms a vblank 3.85 → 5.08. Busy: Stone Hill 38.1 → 52.4%, Dark Hollow **38.8 → 69.9%**. No late vblank in either, and no fall to 30. 7,253 updates at 60 Hz, 3 of them late.
  - **Reading:** at 60 Hz the game thread is busy 1.3× to 1.8× as much in play, above §7.3's ~43% estimate for Toasty because every play vblank is a whole draw. 60 fps holds in every pilot level measured; Dark Hollow, at 70%, has the least headroom.
  - **The eboot:** at the owner's request this VPK's eboot (`8bc2750d…`, read back) stays installed. The earlier one (`66f57eff…`) is kept in `logs/hw-t60/eboot-before.bin`.
- **Not done:** the other 152 classes; particles and spawns; the HUD; the switch at a level load (§7.1); Spyro along walls; a Vita3K run of the mode.

## 2026-11-10 — the PAL and Japanese discs: the plan

### D131. PAL (SCES-01438) and Japanese (SCPS-10083) discs: a staged plan, `docs/regions-plan.md`, for the owner's review before any engine work. It recommends (c): one codebase with region switches in the shared source, region versions of the rewritten functions, PAL and JP data read from the player's verified executable through committed address maps, and per-region matching builds as the check. PAL comes first, at its own 25 Hz by default
- **Why now:** the owner asked for PAL and JP disc support so more players can use their own discs in their language (2026-11-10). Until now only SCUS-94228 runs (D60 refuses the rest).
- **D130:** D130 is the 60 Hz logic pilot's number (its branch `claude/tick60-pilot`, not merged), so this entry is D131.
- **The discs:**
  - The owner's images were copied into the gitignored `disc/image-pal/` and `disc/image-jp/`, then extracted to `disc/files-pal/` and `disc/files-jp/`. Their hashes are in the plan, §1.
  - The PAL archive had no `.cue`; a one-track cue was written beside it.
  - The JP image is the Limited Edition's disc 1, SCPS-10083.
  - Neither image's hash could be confirmed against Redump from here.
- **What decided it:**
  - **PAL** keeps NTSC's tick (25 Hz at 50 Hz) and retunes thousands of constants by hand: about 250 sites in Spyro, and about 3,700 over the level overlays. Some of the retune is changed instruction sequences. So PAL's gameplay is its code, not a table.
  - **PAL's language system is code too:** the merged letter spawner with accent models, the title's select, and the cutscenes and dragon voices chosen by language.
  - **JP** is a post-US branch with a 2D font, new game states, a rewritten camera and shifted structs. That's about 13,000 new instructions in the main executable alone (*estimate*).
  - **So (a), the US code with each region's data, is wrong for both.** (b) alone would copy whole 15–40 KB functions to change their constants.
- **Committed now:**
  - `tools/regions/compare_code.py`: function-by-function comparison of the matching build with another release's executable or WAD overlays, relocations masked. It prints names, addresses and ratios only.
  - The plan.

  No engine code.
- **Measured:**

  | Release | Main exe: bytes the same | Main exe: coverage | Level overlays: coverage |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | PAL | 61.4% (about 80% counting the relocation-only renderers) | 96.7% | 96.0% |
  | JP | 47.0% | 92.6% | 72.3% (three overlays not measured) |

  - **PCSX-Redux** loads both discs' executables with the current BIOS (SCPH-1001).
- **The owner decides** (plan §10):
  1. the approach;
  2. PAL's timing, its own 25 Hz and/or "PAL 60";
  3. the order;
  4. the save folders;
  5. data from the disc;
  6. the hashes and other editions;
  7. the port's menu languages and a Japanese font;
  8. hardware runs, each with the owner's yes.
- **Not done:** everything in the plan's stages. Redux at 50 Hz for PAL isn't checked yet (R0).

## 2026-11-10 — the PAL and Japanese discs: R0

### D132. Stage R0 of `docs/regions-plan.md`: one table of the releases Spyro Vita knows (`src/platform/common/region.c`, `psx_region.h`; `tools/extract_disc.py`'s `REGIONS`). The disc's region comes from the executable `SYSTEM.CNF` boots and that executable's SHA-1, and goes in `g_Region` before the game's first instruction. The PAL (SCES-01438) and Japanese (SCPS-10083) releases are recognised and refused with a "NOT SUPPORTED YET" panel until their stages land. Their saves will go to `save/pal/` and `save/jp/`; `save/` stays the US disc's. The oracle takes `DISC=` and `BIOS=`, and PCSX-Redux runs the PAL disc at 50 Hz with the US BIOS
- **Why:** the owner asked for the PAL stages, R0 first (2026-11-10). D60 had one executable name and two hashes, and refused everything else as a wrong disc.
- **The owner's decisions it relies on** (plan §10, not yet answered; the plan's recommendations as defaults): 3, PAL first; 4, per-region save folders with `save/` staying US. **Decision 6 is still open:** the PAL and JP hashes in the table are of the owner's one dump of each, not confirmed against Redump (redump.org was unreachable here too). If Redump's differ, only the table changes.
- **The table** (`region.c`, one row a release): area, serial, executable, the executable's and `WAD.WAD`'s SHA-1, the WAD header's LBA and entry count (37/102, 500/114, 500/116), the six music files, the memory-card file name, the video rate, the save folder, and whether it runs. `extract_disc.py` keeps every file's SHA-1 per release (the PAL and JP ones from `disc/files-pal`, `disc/files-jp`).
- **Detection** (`cdimage.c` for an image, `libcd.c` for extracted files):
  1. `SYSTEM.CNF`'s BOOT file names the release. With no `SYSTEM.CNF`, or one that boots something else, the first known executable on the disc does (as D60 looked for `SCUS_942.28`).
  2. The executable is hashed. A wrong hash is "WRONG VERSION OF THE GAME"; the US text is D60's, the others name the edition they expected.
  3. A known release that doesn't run yet is "NOT SUPPORTED YET" (`PSX_CD_ERR_NOT_SUPPORTED`), with `g_Region` set. An unknown boot file (another game, the JP SCPS-10085 standard edition) stays "WRONG DISC".
  4. The WAD check (D60) uses the region's hash, and its marker goes in the region's folder.
  - **US unchanged on purpose:** extracted US files still aren't hashed at start-up (the extractor checked them); another region's executable is.
- **Saves:** `psx_region_path` puts the cards, the save states and the disc marker in `save/` for the US disc and `save/pal/`, `save/jp/` otherwise (Vita: `main.c`, `savestate_vita.c`; host: under `--save DIR`).
- **Oracle:** `tools/oracle/run.sh` and `tools/oracle/redux` take `DISC=` (a `.cue` or a folder with one; default `disc/image`) and `BIOS=` (default `disc/bios/SCPH1001.BIN`). `run.sh` also finds the emulator where `fetch_redux.sh` puts it. `tools/oracle/vrate.lua` measures emulated CPU cycles between vblanks.
- **Checked:**
  - **Redux's video rate** (`logs/r0-vrate-{us,pal,jp,pal2}`, SCPH-1001): US 564,398 cycles a vblank (60.009 Hz), JP the same, **PAL 673,844 (50.262 Hz)**. Redux starts every disc at NTSC with this BIOS and switches when the PAL game sets its video mode, about vblank 1,400 (`r0-vrate-pal2`: 60.009 Hz to vblank 1,350, 50.262 Hz from 1,650 on). PAL `main` (0x80015DCC) is in RAM by vblank 1,200. **So no PAL BIOS is needed** (plan §8).
  - **Host, detection** (`logs/r0-detect/`): `disc/files` and `disc/image` run; `files-pal`, `image-pal`, `files-jp` and `image-jp` exit 1 with "NOT SUPPORTED YET" and `disc-error.png`.
  - **`tests/cd/image_variants.py`** with the PAL and JP pairs: **29/29 cases ok** (`logs/r0-variants/summary.txt`): D60's 23 unchanged, plus each region's image and extracted files refused as not supported yet, and its image with one executable byte changed refused as a wrong version.
  - **`ctest -R '^cd$'`** passes, with the region helpers' new cases (`tests/cd/test_cd.c`).
  - **`extract_disc.py`** on the three images (`logs/r0-extract/`): all 16 US files match SCUS-94228; all 16 PAL files match SCES-01438 and all 10 JP files SCPS-10083, each with the note that the port can't run it yet (exit 0).
  - **Vita3K** (this branch's VPK, `logs/r0-vita3k-*`): the PAL and JP images show the panel (`win-grab.png`); the US files and image reach the title as before (`r0-vita3k-us/shot-2400.png`), saves in `ux0:data/spyro/save`.
  - **US host checks** (`tools/host_checks.sh`, default build): **279 of 279 files byte-identical** to main `21ae3ca`'s (`logs/r0-main-*` against `logs/r0-br-*`, `logs/r0-hc-cmp.txt`: the demo 26, the tour 141, routes A and B 56 each, cards included).
  - **US matching build:** `PSX.EXE SHA-1 OK` (`logs/r0-matching/build.txt`).
- **Not done:** everything after R0. Hardware: R0 changes nothing a US player sees but the README line, so no Vita run is asked for here.

## 2026-11-10 — the PAL disc: R1, region plumbing and PAL data

### D133.
