Stage R1 of `docs/regions-plan.md`: the region switches `RV`, `RT` and `REGION_IS` (`include/region.h`, from a new patch series `patches/spyro-1-region/` that the matching builds and the port both apply); `tools/build_matching.sh REGION=pal` (the decomp built with `-DREGION=1`, compared function by function with the PAL disc, now with constants too, `compare_code.py --exact`); PAL's data read from the player's executable through a committed map (`src/game/region_data.c`, `region_map_pal.c`, `tools/regions/make_map.py`, `data_map.py`); PAL's layout in the region table. One real PAL retune goes through it end to end: Spyro's invulnerability timer, 90 → `RT(90, 75)`
- **Why:** R1 is the plumbing every later PAL stage stands on (plan §9). The owner asked for the PAL stages in order (2026-11-10).
- **The owner's decisions it relies on** (plan §10, still unanswered; the plan's recommendations as defaults): 1, approach (c); 2, PAL at its own 25 Hz by default (`g_RegionTiming` is 1 for PAL; PAL 60 comes with R3); 5, data from the disc through committed maps. Decision 6 (the hashes against Redump) is still open: the map is tied to the one PAL executable `fdd04c04…`.
- **`include/region.h`** (patch `0001`): with `-DREGION=N` (every matching build now passes it: 0 for US) `RV(us, pal, jp)`, `RT(us, pal)` and `REGION_IS(PAL)` are constants that gcc 2.7.2 folds, so a region's build emits that region's code. Without it (the port) they read `g_Region` and `g_RegionTiming`, set once by `psx_cd_open` before the game's first instruction (D132).
- **One patch series for both builds:** `patches/spyro-1-region/` goes after `patches/spyro-1/` in `tools/build_matching.sh` and after `patches/spyro-1-port/` in the port (`cmake/prepare.cmake` now takes several patch folders). A region edit is written once, and the US build's SHA-1 and the PAL build's comparison both see it.
- **`tools/build_matching.sh REGION=pal`** builds in `build/s1-matching-pal` with the decomp's `non_matching` target (its `all` ends with the US SHA-256 check), then runs `compare_code.py` on the PAL executable and WAD overlays, and once more on the executable with `--exact`.
- **`compare_code.py --exact`:** the plain mask also hides `li` constants (`addiu`/`ori` from `$zero`) and `slti`/`sltiu`/`andi`/`xori` immediates, which no relocation uses, so a retuned constant looked "same". `--exact` keeps them. US against itself: 581 of 581 "same" with `--exact`; US against PAL: 521 masked, **500 exact**.
  - **A correction to the plan (§3.1, edited):** five camera functions the plan called identical are retuned. `func_80034CE8`, `func_800357A4`, `func_80035F58` and `func_80035FB4` differ only in constants (45→38, 90→75, 1024→1229, 2048→2457, …). `func_80033F08` and `func_80034480` are identical even with `--exact`. Exactly 18 PAL functions are "same" masked but not exact (16 of them decomp C), among them `WadInitialize` (37→500, 816→912), `func_80018F30` (240→256), `HudTick` (1008→1208) and `func_80050BD0`, the cyclorama (240→256, four times). R2 and R3 take them.
- **The proof edit** (patch `0002`): `m_invulverabilityTimer`'s 90 becomes `RT(90, 75)` at its five sites in `src/pete.c` (`HandleSpyroDamage` twice, `func_80041270` three times; PAL 0x8004645C and 0x80046764).
  - With `REGION=pal`, **both functions are "same" with `--exact`**: 502 exact (`logs/r1-matching/compare-exe-exact.txt`, `pal3.txt`).
  - With `REGION=0` the US build is still **`PSX.EXE SHA-1 OK`** (`logs/r1-matching/us.txt`).
  - In the port both are decomp C, called from `pete_transition_hand.c`; `RT` reads `g_RegionTiming`, 0 for US.
- **PAL's data from the disc** (plan §6.2):
  - **`tools/regions/data_map.py`** pairs the data addresses that US and PAL code build, instruction by instruction, in every function `compare_code.py` finds "same", and names them from the matching build's `psx.map`: **737 US labels placed in PAL**, one left out (two functions disagree on `D_80075264`).
  - **`tools/regions/make_map.py pal`** writes `src/game/region_map_pal.c` from the player's executable (SHA-1 checked), with addresses, sizes and word offsets only:
    - block `strings`, PAL 0x80010000–0x80015DCC (24,012 bytes): the five languages' strings before `main`, copied as they are;
    - block `data`, PAL 0x80071054 (the end of the code: US `main_TEXT_END` 0x8006BBE0 plus 0x5474) to the end of the text, 0x8007C000 (44,972 bytes): the language tables, the short strings, the card header template. Its 1,427 words that point into either block are relocated to the copies; 26 aimed at the code are left alone;
    - four fills, the music tables, which keep their US shape: `D_8006EEDC` (0x8007465C, 192 bytes), `D_8006EF9C` (0x8007471C, 192), `D_8006F05C` (0x800747DC, 228), `D_8006F200` (0x80074980, 24). Each fill is checked to be as long as its US label, to end at the next placed PAL label, and to hold no pointer.
    - `--check` compares the committed file with what the executable gives.
  - **`src/game/region_data.c`** loads it: reads the executable through libcd (new `psx_cd_read_file`, both disc layouts), checks the SHA-1 again, checks every block and fill against the executable's header, copies the blocks, relocates the pointers (each must land in a block), and copies the fills over the US labels when asked. `region_data_ptr(pal_addr)` gives the copy of any mapped PAL address, for R2's code. The fills' targets come through a function, since a moved arena's labels are only set at start-up (D25).
  - **4-byte pointers:** the blocks' pointer words stay 4 bytes, as the decomp's data does, so `region_data.c` asserts `sizeof(void *) == 4`. Every host is 32-bit (CLAUDE.md); a 64-bit host would need the tables widened first.
  - **Nothing loads at start-up yet:** PAL is still refused (D132), so nothing consumes the data. R2 calls `region_data_load_disc(1, …)` where the game's memory is set up.
- **The region table** (`region.c`) gains the overlay base (US 0x8007AA38, PAL 0x80081428; JP not measured, J0), `LevelHeader`'s size (464, PAL 656) and the WAD header's entries (title overlay, cutscenes and their count, game-over skybox, PETE, first level, credits overlay and data; JP's font and credits text).
- **Host:** `--region-dev` opens a release that isn't supported yet (developers only; players still get D132's panel). `--region-check` loads the disc's region data, prints what it found and exits.
- **Checked:**
  - **`--region-check`** on `disc/files-pal` and `disc/image-pal` (`logs/r1-check/`): 2 blocks, 68,984 bytes; 1,427 pointers relocated; **all 1,088 pointers into the strings land on a string that ends inside the block**; 26 code words left alone; the 4 music tables (636 bytes) checked. Exit 0 in both layouts.
  - **`REGION=pal`** (`logs/r1-matching/pal3.txt`, `compare-*.txt`): main executable 521 same masked (61.4% of bytes, coverage 96.7%), 502 exact; overlays 96.0% coverage, the plan's figures. The untouched functions compare as they did against the US build.
  - **US matching build:** `PSX.EXE SHA-1 OK` with both region patches and `-DREGION=0` (`logs/r1-matching/us.txt`).
  - US host checks and Vita: see the PR.
- **Not done:** R2 and R3 (everything the map and the switches are for). `make_map.py`'s blocks are PAL's; JP's map comes with J1.

## 2026-11-10 — the presenter's particle walk; the 60 HZ restart question

### D136. The presenter's snapshot copies the word after the particle space too: the `-1` the game puts in a 257th slot, where its particles' walk stops when all 256 slots are in use. Without it the presenter's walk went off the copy (the Vita's crash in Gnasty Gnorc's fight, stage 7e's bug). And the menu's 60 FPS row asks, when a change into or out of 60 HZ needs a restart, whether to restart now or undo it; until then a 60 HZ LOGIC row says ON or OFF AT RESTART
- **Why:** the owner chose 60 HZ in the menu during play and went on without restarting (D130 reads it at start, so it was still 30 Hz logic with the in-between frame), and the game crashed in the Gnasty Gnorc fight (`logs/t60-crash-20261110`: the dump, `spyro.log`, `config.txt`). The owner asked for the crash fixed and for a question when 60 HZ is picked: restart now, or undo.
- **The number:** this PR first took D132; main's PAL stage R0 (#116) has it, R1 (#118) has D133, and D134 and D135 are left to the PAL stages R2 and R3 being written now, so this is D136. Logs keep their `logs/d132*` folder names.
- **The crash** (from the dump, the pilot's ELF):
  - The game has 256 particle slots in a 0x2000-byte space (`AllocateBuffers`, `4BEF8.c`). `loaders.c` writes `-1` into the slot after the last (`*(int *)(&g_Particles[256]) = -1`): the first word of `m_SharedAnimations`, which the space ends at.
  - When every slot is in use there is no `-1` inside the space: `Alloc` (`particle_alloc_hand.c`) takes a random slot over when none is free. The game's walk (`func_800573C8`) then stops at that word.
  - `pres_snap_take` (`src/game/pres/pres_snap.c`, D125) copied exactly 0x2000 bytes to the area's `+0x180000`, so the presenter's `ParticlesDraw` (`func_800573C8_pres`) walked on through the area's zeros, which it draws as type 0 points, to `+0x186000`, read the data there as a type 13 particle with subtype 0x38 and loaded a NULL texture-table entry (`particles_hand.c:566`): a data abort on the `spyro_pres` thread. Gnasty's fight fills the slots (his fireballs, the sparks).
  - `blend_particles` (`present.c`) is bounded by `MAX_PARTICLES` and was safe. `--render-ctx-check`'s mirror (`render_ctx_check.c`, the arena build's 7d check) copied the space the same way.
- **The fix:**
  - `pres_snap_take` copies the 4 bytes after the space to the area's `+0x182000` when they are in the arena (else it writes `-1` there). The region's length stays 0x2000, so `pres_snap_rebase`, the `+0x18` moby fields and `--pres-audit` are unchanged; the area's `+0x182000` held nothing (the moby copy is at `+0x100000`, the sectors at `+0x200000`).
  - `render_ctx_check.c`'s `set_presenter` copies the word into its mirror too. Nothing else assumed a 0x2000-byte copy: the check compares the regions by their pairs' lengths, which stay 0x2000.
  - `--pres-particles-full` (host, tests): after the copy, every free slot of it (the first `-1` and every slot after) becomes a hole (`-2`), so the presenter's walk meets what it meets with all 256 in use, without changing the game.
- **Host repro** (`--interp --widescreen --pres-check --pres-particles-full`, the gems script, 8,768 vblanks):
  - Before the fix (`logs/d132/repro-before`): SIGSEGV at vblank ~6,336 in `ParticlesDraw` (`func_800573C8_pres`, `particles_hand.c:310`, the walk's next-slot read), from `GamestateDraw_pres` in an in-between frame; the core and backtrace are in that folder. On the host the area past the copy holds other data than on the Vita, so the walk ended elsewhere, but it is the same walk off the same copy.
  - After (`logs/d132/repro-after`): exit 0; `pres check`: 4,553 frames compared, 4,553 identical, 0 differ (gamestates 0, 13, 14).
- **The restart question** (`src/platform/vita/menu.c`, `present_vita.c`, `main.c`):
  - The menu had no question of its own, so `vita_menu_ask` is the smallest one: a `VitaMenuAsk` (title, up to three lines of text, two choices with a value on the right, a footer, a callback) shown in place of the rows in the menu's own loop and panel (`menu_panel_draw`), so `menu_test`/`menu_keys` drive it like the rows. UP/DOWN chooses, ✕ answers; ○, START, △ or a tap answers "none".
  - The 60 FPS row's `on_change` applies the value (`vita_present_apply`), then asks if this change made a restart pending (60 Hz logic chosen and not running, or the other way), and it wasn't before. **RESTART NOW** saves the options (the menu's own `save()`), stops the presenter, the render thread and the GPU as an exit does (D120), flushes the log, and calls `sceAppMgrLoadExec("app0:eboot.bin", NULL, NULL)`. A 0 from it means the system replaces the app when ready (Vita3K returns first), so the app waits up to 5 s, then exits if it still runs; an error exits at once. **UNDO** puts the row back to its value before the change that made the restart pending and applies it. ○ leaves it for the next start.
  - While a restart is pending, a **60 HZ LOGIC** row (below 60 FPS) says ON AT RESTART or OFF AT RESTART; ✕ on it asks again. Rows can now come and go while the menu is open: it lays its lines out again after each change, keeping the selected row.
  - The memory cards are written whole when the game asks (`libmcrd.c`) and a save state at the main loop's top (D51), and the menu runs in the game's VSync wait, so a restart from it never cuts a write short. Progress since the game's last save is lost; the question says so.
  - The app's log keeps the run before as `spyro-prev.log` (`log.c`), since the new run's log replaces `spyro.log`; `tools/vita/vita3k_run.sh` collects it.
- **Vita3K** (the VPK built with the Mac's VitaSDK, `build/vita-mac`; `logs/d132/v3k-*`; `menu_test=600` at the title, `skip_logos=1`):
  - `v3k-popup`: DOWN, LEFT (OFF → 60 HZ): the question, `win-grab.png`.
  - `v3k-pending`: then ○ and DOWN: the 60 HZ LOGIC row, ON AT RESTART (`win-grab.png`); the log says `interp=4: 60 Hz logic at the next start`.
  - `v3k-undo`: DOWN, ✕ on UNDO, ○: `60 fps back to OFF (interp=0)`, `saved ... interp=0`, then `frames=900`'s exit as before.
  - `v3k-restart`: ✕ on RESTART NOW: the app started again, with `present: 60 Hz logic in play (interp=4, D130)` (`spyro-prev.log`); the same keys there went back to 30 Hz (CAMERA) and restarted it once more (`spyro.log`: `interp 3`). Vita3K's `sceAppMgrLoadExec` returned 0 before replacing the app; the first build of this then exited at once, and Vita3K closed instead of starting the app again, hence the wait.
- **Checks** (the host build of this branch, `build/bins/spyro-fix`):
  - `tools/host_checks.sh` (off): 287 of 287 files identical to main's: `../tick60/logs/t60-base-*` (main before D130) and `t60-head-*` (D130's pilot, which D130 found identical to it), the logs compared with their folder names masked (`logs/d132-hc-fix-*`, `logs/d132/host_checks_cmp.txt`).
  - `tools/pres_check_ab.sh` (`MODE=chk`, `I_FROM` the tick60 worktree's `t60pc-*-i`, main's `--interp` runs): demo 26/26 files identical, wsi 26/26, gems 19/19, routeA 56/56, routeB 56/56; `pres check` 0 frames differ in each (6,169, 6,169, 4,553, 6,669, 6,669 compared) (`logs/d132/pres_check_ab.txt`, `logs/d132pc-*-chk`).
  - ctest: 28 of 29 passed; `gte_inline` failed because Docker's file sharing left its binary without the execute bit (as it did one copy of `spyro`), and passed once the bit was set (`logs/d132/ctest.txt`, `logs/d132/ctest-gte_inline.txt`).
  - The oracle replay wasn't run: the 30 Hz path runs none of the changed code (the snapshot and the 7d check run only with the presenter or `--render-ctx-check`; the host's `main.c` only parses the new option; the rest is the Vita's), and host_checks' files are main's.
  - The VPK builds with no warning in the changed files (`logs/d132/vpk-mac-build*.txt`).
- **Not done:** hardware. Proposed, each with the owner's yes: the Gnasty Gnorc fight at `interp=1` with many particles on screen (the crash's own case), and the question's RESTART NOW on the device (does the system start the app again, how long it takes, and the log's `restart:` lines in `spyro-prev.log`).
### D134. Stage R2 of `docs/regions-plan.md`, part A: PAL's headers, its 512×256 picture and its shared fixes, as region switches in the decomp's C (`patches/spyro-1-region/0003-pal-headers-and-picture.patch`) and our hand C. Every edited decomp function but one is proved against the PAL disc instruction for instruction, constants included; the PAL disc now boots on the host to its title flythrough in the full 256-line picture. PAL stays refused for players (`PsxRegion.supported = 0`) until R4
- **Why:** R2 is PAL's language and picture (plan §9). This first part is what everything else in R2 stands on: the WAD and level headers in PAL's shape, the cutscene and dragon slots by language, the picture, PAL's data loaded at start-up. The owner asked for the PAL stages in order (2026-11-10).
- **The owner's decisions it relies on** (plan §10, unanswered; the recommendations as defaults): 1, approach (c); 5, data from the disc through committed maps. Decision 6 (the Redump hashes) is still open: everything here was checked on the one PAL image `52bba2f6…` (executable `fdd04c04…`).
- **The headers** (plan §2.3, §3.4). `g_WadHeader` and `g_LevelHeader` are PS1 RAM labels in the US layout (D23, D106), so a PAL header can't be copied over them: PAL's WAD header is 912 bytes (114 entries, 16 cutscene slots), its level header 656 (5 × 6 dragon slots).
  - In the PAL matching build `include/wad.h` has PAL's shapes (`m_CutsceneData[16]`, `m_Dragons[5][6]`), so its code is PAL's.
  - In the port `WadInitialize` and the three level-header copies go through `WAD_HEADER_COPY`/`LEVEL_HEADER_COPY` to `src/game/region_game.c`: the US header is copied as before; a PAL one fills the US-shaped label entry by entry (from `region.c`'s `.wad` indices) and keeps PAL's 16 cutscene slots and 30 dragon slots beside it (`g_WadCutscenesPal`, `g_LevelDragonsPal`). Every write is bounded by the label's size.
  - Readers go through `WAD_CUTSCENE(i)` and `LEVEL_DRAGON_PTR(i)` (PAL: `m_Dragons[g_Language][i]`). PAL's two per-cutscene tables (VRAM size, pitch; 16 entries, PAL 0x8007457C and 0x800745BC) come from the player's executable (`CUTSCENE_VRAM_SIZES`, `CUTSCENE_PITCHES` → `PortRegionData`).
  - The cutscene's kind is PAL's `(slot − 1) % 3` (intro, ending 1, ending 2) and the intro is slot `1 + g_Language × 3` (`CUTSCENE_IS_INTRO`, `_OUTRO`, `_OUTRO2`).
- **The picture** (plan §3.2): `GraphicsInitialize` sets `MODE_PAL` and draws and shows 512×256 at the pause menu's screen position (`g_ScreenOffsetX`, `g_ScreenOffsetY`, 4 and 16); `SetGeomOffset(256, 128)`; the logos 8 lines down; PAL's clear of both frame buffers (`ClearFrameBuffersPal`, PAL 0x8001BCF0) where US clears 512×480 (start-up, credits, the cutscenes' start and end); and the title flyby raised for the taller view (start z 0x29A0 → 0x2AA0, its arcs `Sin/2 + 0x2980` → `Sin×3/4 + 0x2AA0`; R1's notes had these as timing, they aren't). The port's presentation already takes the display's height from the `DISPENV` (240 → 8 black lines; 256 → none), so the 256 lines fill the screen with no change there.
- **The shared fixes** (plan §3.5): `func_80039398` and `MobyMove` test again from the corrected point (MobyMove returns 4 then); `MobyMoveWithGravity` on a 4 slows the mover by a quarter on a slope of 36–220 (the plan said vertical speed: it is the horizontal); `UpdateSlopeFloorCollision` skips its fallback floor ray in Spyro's states 11 and 2; and in hand C, `func_8004DF24` (the camera's floor) keeps the floor's normal and Spyro's airborne step copies only x and y of the post-collision velocity (`collision_hand.c`, `pete_dispatch_hand.c`). `ColMoby` turned out identical apart from relocations.
- **Gone for PAL:** the level-warp cheat's call in the inventory (`func_8002EB2C`), and `CheatProcessLevelWarp` from the PAL build. The port keeps the function: its menu warp (D50) and the host's `--tour` call it directly.
- **Save states:** a new `REGN` chunk holds PAL's language, screen position and language slots (`savestate.c`; empty for US and JP), and `tools/savestate_globals.py` lists them.
- **Also:** `func_80032AB0` (PAL's title states, mode 0 and sub-state 7), `func_8002C924` (four per-language words in the rescued dragon's props for two special cutscenes), `region_data_load_disc(1, …)` at start-up (`src/game/port.c`, PAL only; a failure is fatal), `region.h` `REGION_HAS_PAL`/`REGION_HAS_US` (code only one side has: the US matching build never sees PAL's, the port has both).
- **Left to R3, found while checking** (shared notes `r2-for-r3.md`): `Initialize`'s logo hold 210 → 160 and `MobyMoveWithGravity`'s 500 → 600 and −260 → −311 are the only differences left in those functions. `func_8003E90C`'s "`>>4` → `>>12`" is `(x × 307) >> 12`, a ×1.2 speed retune, not a fixed-point change. `func_8002BFE0`'s /60 → /50.
- **Checked** (tables in `docs/translation-log.md`):
  - **Against the PAL disc, per function, constants included** (a single-file `-DREGION=1` compile compared with the executable, relocations masked): `GraphicsInitialize`, `WadInitialize`, `ClearFrameBuffersPal`, `LoadCutscene`, `LoadDragonCutscene`, `InitCreditsSequence`, `StartCutscenePlayback`, `func_8002C924`, `func_80032AB0`, `GamestateCutsceneTransition`, `GamestateCutsceneUpdate`, `func_8002EB2C`, `func_80039398`, `MobyMove` and `UpdateSlopeFloorCollision`: **0 differing instructions**. `Initialize` and `MobyMoveWithGravity`: only R3's timing constants. `EndCutscenePlayback`: two registers swapped in its `% 3` (gcc 2.7.2's allocation; the instructions are PAL's otherwise). The full `REGION=pal` build's `compare-exe-exact.txt`: see the PR.
  - **US matching build:** `PSX.EXE SHA-1 OK` (`logs/r2a/matching-us.log`).
  - **The PAL disc on the host** (`--region-dev`, `logs/r2-pal-boot1/`): boots through the logos to the title flythrough at 512×256. It then crashes in the US title overlay's `TitlescreenDraw`, which draws a Moby PAL's title data doesn't have: PAL's title overlay is its own (part C or later).
  - US host checks and Vita: see the PR.
## 2026-11-10 — the PAL disc: R3, PAL's 50 Hz retune (the main executable and the hand C)

### D135. Stage R3 of `docs/regions-plan.md`, part 1: every PAL timing site of the main executable is `RT(us, pal)` (D133's switch), in the decomp's C through the region patches and in our hand C. A new tool finds them: `tools/regions/retune.py` aligns each US function with PAL's, compares constants strictly, maps each site to its source line through the build's own DWARF line tables, writes the edits, and writes them as patches both builds apply. Spyro's controller and physics, the camera and the moby helpers are done; the main executable's decomp C is checked by `REGION=pal` (functions strictly the same as PAL's), the hand C by site maps (`docs/pal-retune/`)
- **Why:** R3 (plan §9), the owner's request (2026-11-10), in parallel with R2. PAL keeps NTSC's tick at 50 Hz (25 Hz logic) and scales speeds ×1.2, accelerations ×1.44 and durations ×5/6 (plan §3.1).
- **The owner's decisions it relies on** (plan §10, unanswered; the recommendations as defaults): 1, approach (c); 2, PAL at its own 25 Hz by default with "PAL 60" as an option (`RT()` is what makes PAL 60 cheap; the option itself is D138). **Decision 6 is still open:** the sites are measured against the one PAL executable `fdd04c04…`.
- **`compare_code.py --exact` misses retunes:** it masks every `addiu`/`ori` immediate from a register, so `addiu v0, v0, 192` (a speed added) and its PAL `276` look the same. `retune.py sites` masks only immediates that can be relocated: j/jal, `lui` (unless an `ori` follows: a 32-bit constant), and `addiu`/`ori`/loads/stores whose base came from a `lui` or is `$gp`. US against itself: 580 of 581 strictly the same (a 2-word stub placed at the wrong duplicate). US against PAL: 498 (500 with `--exact`).
- **Mapping sites to source:**
  - **Decomp C:** the decomp compiles with `-g3`; `objdump -dl` of the objects gives each instruction's line, and the site's literal is the one on that line (or within two lines) whose value, negation or ±1 the instruction holds. A literal is edited only when every instruction it compiles to is a site with one PAL value. A first attempt replaced literals with sentinels and recompiled: the sentinels changed the code's shape (multiplications, folded comparisons) and alignment failed; the line tables replaced it.
  - **Hand C** (`func_80043FE4`, `func_8003EA68`, `func_80041670`, `func_8003FE40`, the camera's three): no compiler maps those, so each site was placed by reading the US asm beside PAL's (`retune.py context`). The maps, every site and every changed sequence with its line, are in `docs/pal-retune/`.
- **Three patch folders:** the port's own patches rewrite some lines R3 edits (the 60 Hz pilot's `T60_TICK_DO(x -= 8)`, `T60_SHARE(8)`, D130; the skipped logos' `while (LOGOS && …)`). A region patch that must apply after them can't also apply to the decomp's line. So `retune.py patch` puts each change whose lines and context are the same, and unique, in both trees into `patches/spyro-1-region/` (both builds), and writes the rest twice: `patches/spyro-1-region-matching/` (`build_matching.sh` only, the decomp's line) and `patches/spyro-1-region-port/` (CMake only, after the shared ones; the edit goes on the port's own statement, inside its `T60_*` wrapper, matched in order). It applies every patch to copies of both trees and requires the expected files; `retune.py rtcheck` holds both trees to the same `RT(us, pal)` pairs, file by file.
- **What changed** (`docs/pal-timing.md` has the table):
  - **Decomp C** (`0004-pal-timing-main`): `Initialize`'s logo holds, `CDLoadSync`/`CDLoadAsync`'s read timeout (PAL's new code, `(t * 5 + 3) / 6` for `t >= 6`), `func_800189F0`, `func_800334D4`, `HudTick`, `func_80035F58`, `func_80037714`, `MobyMoveWithGravity`, `MobySpawnDrop` (its PAL code reorders: three of four `140`s become 168, the fourth stays), `func_8003CCE4`, `func_8004888C`, `func_800499C0`, `func_8004A200`: 32 literals, and the timeout as PAL's `if`/`else` (`RT(1, maxTime < 6)`, which the US build folds to the old store). Two R2 found (D134): `func_8003E90C`'s ledge push `x >> 4` → `(x * 307) >> 12` (×1.2), and `func_8002BFE0`'s cutscene animation index `/ 60` → `/ 50`, which is the length of an animation in PAL's cutscene data, so `RV(60, 50, 60)`: it stays 50 in PAL 60.
  - **Hand C:** `func_80043FE4` 176 of 178 sites and 34 changed sequences (a `PalSpeed`: PAL multiplies a square root by 4915/4096), `func_8003EA68` 64, `func_80041670` 89, `func_8003FE40` 3, the camera 17 (PAL clamps the first azimuth speed to −2458..2457, the second stays ±0x800). The free camera (D47) matches L2/R2's PAL turn.
- **Left to R2** (not timing, or in R2's files): `InitFairyCutscene`'s layout; `HudTick`'s egg model Y and `func_800334D4`'s 512×256 clear; PAL's three added sounds and a level-43 supercharge gravity in `func_8003EA68`, and the wing trail's vertex indices in `func_80043FE4` (a model difference: `RV()`), marked in the hand C.
- **Checked:**
  - **`REGION=pal`, on main with R2's first part (D134)** (`logs/r3-fin/compare-exe-strict.txt`, `compare-exe-exact.txt`, the three R3 PRs together): the main executable is strictly the same as PAL's in **529 of 581** functions (531 with `--exact`). Of the 18 decomp functions with PAL timing sites, **16 are strictly PAL's**; `func_800334D4` and `HudTick` keep only R2's picture changes. R3's own share, measured before R2 landed (`logs/r3-final/`, on 33aa9ec): 498 → 510 strictly the same.
  - **US matching build:** `PSX.EXE SHA-1 OK` with every R3 patch (`logs/r3-matching/us5.txt`, the final tree on main 8f96a63; and after each step before the rebase: `us.txt`, `us2.txt`, `us3.txt`).
  - **US host checks** (`tools/host_checks.sh`, R3's three PRs together on main 8f96a63): **279 of 279 files byte-identical** both to main's run (R1's `r0-main-*`, main 21ae3ca; D130, R0, R1 and R2's first part each reported 279 of 279 since) and to R2's first part's own run (`r2a-hc-*`) (`logs/r3-hc-cmp-final.txt`, `logs/r3-hc-cmp-final-r2a.txt`; also before the rebase: `r3-hc-cmp.txt`, `r3-hc-cmp2.txt`).
  - **Not checked on PAL in play:** with R2's first part the host runs the PAL disc through its logos and title flythrough (at 50 Hz with D138, `logs/r3-pal-boot3/`), but the title screen is the US overlay's until R2 brings PAL's, so no level is reached yet. Plan §9's Redux traces of Spyro's speed and a moby's path come with R4. `tools/tick60_cpp_check.py` (D130's token check against main) now differs on the files R3 touched, by the `RT()`s.

### D137. Stage R3, part 2: the level overlays' PAL timing sites are `RT(us, pal)` in `moby_update.c`, `moby_spawn.c` and `particle_spawn.c`, the sources every level overlay is built from: 1,134 literals. In those sources, the timing sites (×1.2, ×1.44, ×5/6, ×3/4) in the 35 level overlays fall from 3,519 to 542. The rest are listed (`docs/pal-retune/overlays-left.md`)
- **Why:** R3 (plan §9); D135's tool and patch folders.
- **The owner's decisions it relies on:** as D135 (1, 2; 6 still open).
- **How:** `retune.py propose` over the 35 level overlays, twice. The first pass ran from the US build's line tables. The second ran from the first pass's `REGION=pal` build, which pairs better with PAL, and looked two lines either side (a store the compiler puts on its condition's line, a call's arguments). One literal is often the site in several overlays (a moby class is one source, `overlays/mobys.yaml`); it changes only if every overlay agrees.
- **Taken:** proposals whose PAL/US ratio is a timing one. **Not taken:**
  - `balloonist.c` and `flight.c`: R2 rewrites the balloonist's dialogue and the flight results screen for the languages. Their timing sites (about 75) go with that work.
  - proposals with no timing ratio (layout, misalignment in rewritten code);
  - sites with no literal on or near their line (macros, values computed from others);
  - literals PAL changed in some overlays and not others (conflicts).
  The last two make up the 542 left: `docs/pal-retune/overlays-left.md`, by line.
- **The port:** 65 of the edits are on lines the 60 Hz pilot rewrote (`T60_TICK_DO(props->m_0x14 -= RT(8, …))`, `T60_SHARE(RT(8, 10))`): `patches/spyro-1-region-port/0005`. Both trees end with the same `RT` pairs (1,276 in `moby_update.c`).
- **Checked:**
  - **`REGION=pal`** (`logs/r3-fin/compare-wad-strict.txt`, the final tree on main 8f96a63; the same counts as before the rebase, `logs/r3-final/`): constant sites over all 37 overlays 5,501 → 2,535. No function gained a site. The unpaired instructions are unchanged within 3% (17,823 → 18,225); the inserted sequences are still to do. No overlay function is strictly the same yet: every `UpdateMoby` has PAL's inserted code.
  - **US:** as D135 (`PSX.EXE SHA-1 OK`, `logs/r3-matching/us5.txt`; host checks 279 of 279, `logs/r3-hc-cmp-final.txt`).

## 2026-11-10 — the PAL disc: R3, the platform at 50 Hz and PAL 60

### D138. Stage R3, part 3 (plan §6.3, §7.4): the game's vblank runs at `psx_region_vblank_hz()`, 50 for the PAL disc at its own timing and 60 otherwise. The Vita counts 5 of its vblanks for every 6 of the display's; the CD streams 3 sectors and the SPU mixes 882 samples a 50 Hz vblank; the host's VSync is one 50 Hz vblank. "PAL 60" (`--pal60`, `config.txt` `pal60=1`) is PAL's code with US timing at 60 Hz. The presenter keeps its one in-between frame a tick, so PAL is 50 frames a second on the 60 Hz display; 25 → 60 blending by time is left
- **Why:** R3 (plan §9): PAL's 25 Hz tick is two vblanks of a 50 Hz clock, and today's clock is the display's.
- **The owner's decisions it relies on:** 2 (PAL 25 Hz by default, PAL 60 as an option); decision 6 is still open.
- **The rate:** `psx_region_vblank_hz()` (`region.c`) is 50 when `g_RegionTiming` is set. That's the PAL disc without `g_RegionPal60` (set from `--pal60` or `pal60=1` before the disc opens; `cdimage.c`). Each platform hands the rate to the clock and the SPU once the disc is open (`psx_clock_set_hz`, `g_psx_spu_vblank_samples`).
- **The Vita's real-time clock** (`clock.c`): at 50 Hz `psx_clock_due` counts the display's widened vcount as `floor(display * 50 / 60)` of ours. Its base stays in the display's vblanks. The 7c due-frame check (`vita_vblank_past_due`, D112) converts through `psx_clock_display_due` (the display vblank our vblank starts at) and `psx_clock_display_to_ours`. At 60 Hz each is the old arithmetic, unchanged.
- **The CD** (`libcd.c`): the drive's quarter-sectors a vblank are `150 * 4 / hz` at double speed: 10 at 60, 12 at 50 (3 sectors). XA keeps real time.
- **The SPU** (`libspu.c`, `spu_native.c`, the Vita's mixer): `PSX_SPU_VBLANK_SAMPLES` is now a variable, 735 or 882 (44,100 / hz); the buffers are sized for 882 (`PSX_SPU_VBLANK_SAMPLES_MAX`). The audio ring needs nothing: 50 × 882 is 44,100 frames a second, as 60 × 735 is.
- **The 60 Hz pilot** (D130) is US only for now: `--tick60` and `interp=4` are turned off with a log line on another disc.
- **Not done:**
  - **The presenter at 25 → 60.** It blends at ½ only (`present.c` `mid_i`, `mid_track`) and draws one in-between frame a tick, due at a game vblank. A frame due at each display vblank, with its own fraction, is a change to the 7c pipeline. It needs the Vita (the owner's yes), and PAL can't run until R2.
  - **PAL 60's cutscenes:** PAL's cutscene index (`(tick * 3) >> 2`, R2's) assumes 50 Hz. At 60 it would run 20% fast against the voices, so PAL 60 needs those gamestates at 50 Hz, or the index scaled with the rate.
  - **A menu row for PAL 60:** the config key only, until R2 makes PAL playable (and #117's menu changes have landed).
- **Checked:**
  - **`tests/vcount`:** at 60 Hz its three plays give main's numbers exactly (vblank counts 1,199,970, 2,000,718, 2,399,765, run beside main's `clock.c`). Two new 50 Hz plays across the 16-bit wraps pass, and so do the new conversion cases. The mutant (the raw counter) still hangs and fails.
  - **`tests/spu`:** `spu_native` (735) passes, and so does the new `spu_native_50hz` (882 samples, 5,000 vblanks, native mix against the reference); the mutant still fails. `ctest` for cd, spu and vcount: **6 of 6 passed** (`logs/r3-host/ctest3.txt`: cd, spu_native, spu_native_50hz, spu_native_mutant, vcount, vcount_mutant; the mutants are expected to fail and are marked so).
  - **The host:** `--region-dev --disc disc/files-pal` runs at 50 Hz (`region: SCES-01438, 50 Hz vblanks (PAL timing)`) through the logos and the title flythrough to the title screen in 2,500 vblanks, no hang (`logs/r3-pal-boot3/`, R3's three PRs on main with R2's first part). The title screen is drawn by the US overlay until R2 brings PAL's.
  - **US unchanged:** host checks 279 of 279 byte-identical to main's and to R2's first part's run (`logs/r3-hc-cmp-final.txt`, `logs/r3-hc-cmp-final-r2a.txt`; R3's three PRs together on main 8f96a63: D135). `ctest` as above is that tree's (`logs/r3-host/ctest3.txt`, 6 of 6).
  - **Vita:** the VPK builds (`logs/r3-vita/build.txt`, `VPK OK`, R3's three PRs together), with no warnings from the changed files. No Vita3K or hardware run: nothing a US player sees changes, and PAL can't run yet.

## 2026-11-11 — 60 Hz logic: portals, vortexes and the scripts that move Spyro

### D141. In the 60 Hz rules a moby's hold on Spyro lasts a 30 Hz tick: his update lets go of `m_ControlFlags` bit 31, and of a vortex's or whirlwind's lift in `m_DamageFlags` (0x800), on the vblank that ends a tick, not after every vblank (`patches/spyro-1-port/0015-tick60-script-hold.patch`, `t60_rules.c`). Before this, a moby at 30 Hz held him for half of each tick, so no portal could carry him in (the owner's Gnorc Gnexus report: he hovered at the entrance) and no exit vortex could lift him out. Off and interpolated run main's code
- **Why:** the owner played main `6407a52` at 60 HZ (`interp=4`) on the Vita on 2026-11-10. In Gnorc Gnexus Spyro "just stuttered at the entrance" of a portal and never entered the level (log `logs/hw-60hz-portal-20261110/` in the main checkout, gitignored: 4,000 vblanks of 60 Hz play in GNORC GNEXUS, no level change). D130's hardware runs never went through a portal at 60 Hz.
- **The cause:**
  - A moby that drives Spyro (a portal's path moby, class 398; the exit vortex, 9; whirlwinds, 300; rides, cutscene pad locks: 56 assignments in `moby_update.c`) sets `g_Spyro.m_ControlFlags` with bit 31 in its update. Spyro's update (`func_8004A200`, `pete.c`) ends with `m_ControlFlags &= 0x7FFFFFFF`, and the moby sets the bit again in its next update, which comes before his (`update.c`: `g_UpdateMoby`, then `func_8004A200`). His update also clears `m_DamageFlags` (after `func_80048D10`), which mobys set the same way.
  - In a 60 Hz update a moby class not judged for 60 Hz runs on the vblank that starts a tick (D130). Class 398 is one of them in every level, and in the 31 levels outside the pilot every class is. So on a tick's second vblank bit 31 was already clear: `func_8003FE40` zeroed the flags and Spyro ran on the pad and gravity again for half of every tick, with half the script's move (`T60_SHARE` of `unk_0x208`) on the other half.
  - **Portals:** the flags' 0x400 puts the camera in its path mode (`0x8000000B`, `func_8003740C`), and the path moby fires the level transition only when the camera's sphere touches the portal's surface (`moby_update.c`, class 398, states 1 and 2). With 0x400 gone every other vblank, the camera left its path mode and, entering it again, started the path over (`func_800357A4`), so it never got there. Spyro bobbed in the portal's mouth for as long as the player held on.
  - **Vortexes and whirlwinds** also set `m_DamageFlags |= 0x800` once a tick, and `HandleSpyroDamage` (`pete.c`) holds Spyro in state 17 (lifted) while 0x800 is there and drops him to state 15 when it isn't. At 60 Hz he went 17, 15, 17, 15 and rose about a third as fast, so a vortex never took him home.
  - D130's class table said 398 was "a script Spyro's 60 Hz side already shares". The sharing was right (`ScriptedControl` takes half of `unk_0x208` a vblank); the hold was the gap.
- **The fix** (60 Hz rules only; `#if SPYRO_TICK60` sites in `0015`, each with the original line in its `#else`):
  - `t60_spyro_release` (func_8004A200's end): lets go of bit 31 on a tick's second vblank, or after a late update (2+ vblanks, a whole tick), and otherwise keeps it and notes that it did (`g_t60.spyro_held`).
  - `t60_spyro_damage_end` (its `m_DamageFlags = 0`): clears them on a tick's second vblank or after a late update; on the first it keeps only 0x800. The one-shot hits (0x10 bounce, 0x20 hazard, 0x80, 0x400 charge, 0x8000 knockback and the rest) are still taken once a tick, and every other bit a moby sets is one of those (22 `|=` and 5 `=` sites, `moby_update.c`). 0x2000 (supercharge) comes from Spyro's own surface check, every vblank.
  - `PortGamestateUpdate` (`tick60.c`): when a 60 Hz update on a tick's first vblank leaves play (the portal's and the vortex's own gamestate 1, the pause menu), it lets go of both there, so the 30 Hz update that follows finds Spyro as the original's tick left him. A save state's load clears the note; the state's own bits are let go at its tick's end.
  - No moby reads `g_Spyro.m_ControlFlags` (they assign it or OR bits in), so a 60 Hz class sees no difference. The judged classes that drive Spyro (in the pilot's levels: the vortex, the whirlwind and the rest) set both every vblank, so their hold is what it was.
- **Host repro and fix** (linux32, `logs/p60/`, gitignored; main `8f96a63`'s binary `logs/bin-main`, this branch's `logs/bin-fix2`; every 60 Hz run from a save state made by its own binary at 60 Hz):
  - **Gnorc Gnexus → Gnorc Cove** (`gl*`: the game's warp cheat to level 60, Spyro poked 2,450 units in front of the level 61 portal, holding up+left):
    - 30 Hz, main: gamestate 1 at vblank 7511, Gnorc Cove (61) at 7529, play at 7931.
    - **60 Hz, main: never.** The path moby goes to state 1 at 7482; Spyro stays in state 5 at the entrance, z going 7,676 ↔ 8,011, for the rest of the run: the owner's report.
    - **60 Hz, fixed:** gamestate 1 at 7508, Gnorc Cove at 7526, play at 7928, then 60 Hz play in Gnorc Cove. On the way in Spyro moves the same per vblank as at 30 Hz (+28, −246, +68 units every 4 vblanks in both); the portal fires 3 vblanks sooner because the walk up drifted ~120 units.
  - **Artisans → Stone Hill** (`air*`: from route A's state at 7,800, Spyro poked mid-air where 30 Hz's run touched the portal, holding up):
    - 30 Hz, main: gamestate 1 at 7897, Stone Hill at 7915, the entrance animation (9) at 8317, play at 8385.
    - **60 Hz, main: never** (path moby in state 1 at 7867; Spyro keeps his jump's gravity).
    - **60 Hz, fixed:** 7893, 7911, 8313, 8381: the same gamestates, each the same number of vblanks after the poke as at 30 Hz.
  - **Dry Canyon's exit vortex → Peace Keepers** (`v*`: route B with Spyro poked mid-air where 30 Hz's jump met the vortex, `logs/p60/route_b_vortex_poke.txt`):
    - 30 Hz, main: gamestate 1 at 8629, Peace Keepers (20) at 8647, play at 9049.
    - **60 Hz, main: never**, and with only bit 31 kept still never: lifted, state 17 and 15 by turns, rising ~35 units every 3 vblanks against ~100.
    - **60 Hz, fixed:** 8634, 8652, 9054: the same 402-vblank transition home.
  - **The Peace Keepers balloonist** (`k*`: the warp cheat to level 20, Spyro poked onto route B's dock so the balloonist arms, then beside it facing it, a jump in place, and route B's dialogue and menu lines): gamestate 12 at 6841 (30 Hz), 6840 (60 Hz, main) and 6840 (60 Hz, fixed), Artisans at 7531, 7532, 7532, play at 8233, 8234, 8234. It changes the gamestate in the same update and the ride is a 30 Hz gamestate, so it never needed the hold; unchanged by the fix.
  - **Routes A and B as written** don't reach their portal or vortex at 60 Hz on either binary: from the same spot, the same standing jump with up held goes elsewhere (route A: ~280 units forward instead of ~820 in 24 vblanks; route B: ~430 units to the side), and after route B's long teleport pokes "up" walks Spyro another way. The controlled runs above stand in for them. That difference is D130's open-loop one, not this fix's; it's left as a follow-up.
- **Checks:**
  - `tools/tick60_cpp_check.py`: **11 of 11** files preprocess to main's tokens, `pete.c` through `0015` included. The tool now applies `patches/spyro-1-region/` and `patches/spyro-1-region-port/` (D135) after `patches/spyro-1-port/` to its base copy, as `CMakeLists.txt` does; without that it reported the region headers (D133) as differences in 4 files.
  - The 60 Hz object: 159 functions made local (157 + the two new ones), no new writable static (`build/linux32/tick60_update.o.txt`).
  - **Off:** `tools/host_checks.sh` with main `8f96a63`'s binary and this branch's: **287 of 287 files identical** (demo 28, the whole-game tour 143, routes A and B 58 each; the logs with their folder names masked; `logs/p60hc-{main,fix}-*`, `logs/p60/hc_cmp.txt`).
  - **Interpolated:** `tools/pres_check_ab.sh MODE=chk`, main's binary as the reference: **153 of 153 files identical** (demo 26, wsi 26, gems 19, routes A and B 56 each), and `pres check` **30,229 of 30,229 frames identical** (`logs/p60pc-*`, `logs/p60/pres_check_ab.txt`).
  - **The oracle replay** (`s8e-cap`): **75 PASS, 0 FAIL, 20 NOEXIT** on both binaries, the summaries identical (`logs/caps/rp-{main,fix}`).
  - **ctest:** 28 passed, `gte` skipped (no `logs/gte/gte.bin` in this worktree), of 29, as on main. `gte` first failed to start: Docker's file sharing had left its binary without the execute bit (as in D136); with the bit set it skips (`logs/p60/ctest*.txt`). `tools/check_no_assets.sh`: exit 0.
  - **60 Hz with the presenter:** the Gnorc Cove portal entry with `--tick60 --interp --pres-check --widescreen`: 988 of 988 frames identical (761 in play at 60 Hz, 210 in the transition; `logs/p60/glpc`).
  - **Vita:** built by the PR's CI only; not run in Vita3K or on hardware.
- **Not checked:** hardware (proposed in the PR, the owner's yes first); a whirlwind outside the pilot's levels (the same 0x800 lift as the vortex, by reading); the rides and cutscene pad locks (bit 31 only, as the portal).

### D142. Two 60 Hz fixes from the owner's play. (1) The free camera: on the Vita the 60 Hz rules read their own copy of the control options, which nothing wrote, so at 60 HZ the right stick did nothing and look mode's up/down was flipped whatever the menu said. `g_port_controls` is now defined in `port.c`, outside the files the 60 Hz rules build again, and `tools/tick60_link.py` refuses any global data definition in the set instead of weakening it. (2) Sparx runs at 60 Hz in every level, not only the pilot's: every level's `moby_update.c` is built into the 60 Hz rules (`src/game/tick60/t60_levels.h`), and outside the pilot's levels only Sparx (class 120) runs every vblank (`t60_class_60_everywhere`). Off and interpolated run main's code
- **Why:** the owner played main `3f70f0d` at 60 HZ (`interp=4`) on the Vita on 2026-11-11, with the free camera on and both axes inverted (log and `config.txt` in the main checkout's `logs/hw-60hz-analog-20261111/`, gitignored). Two reports:
  - "when the analog option in the menu is on it doesnt work, and when its off the right analog does work"; asked, the owner said that with ANALOG STICK on the left stick moves Spyro normally and the right stick doesn't move the camera. With it off, the right stick is L2/R2 (`pad.c`), which works. So it was the free camera (D47), not the analog row: that row was right, and so was the pad.
  - Sparx stutters a little while Spyro runs at 60 Hz (Gnorc Gnexus and Gnasty Gnorc, levels 60 and 63: outside the pilot).
- **The free camera's cause:**
  - D130's second compile builds `camera_hand.c` again into the 60 Hz object, and `camera_hand.c` defined `g_port_controls` (the free camera, the inverts and the speed). `tick60_link.py` weakened that definition so that the game's would win at link time, and on the host (i686) it does: GCC refers to the global by its symbol.
  - On ARM GCC reaches a global defined in the same file through a **section anchor**, the section's base plus an offset (`-fsection-anchors`, on at -O2). PAL R3's Vita object (`build/vita-r3/tick60_update.o`, main's code) has a 16-byte `.data` holding only its `g_port_controls` and 21 `movw`/`movt` pairs against `.LANCHOR2`, its address; the symbol's one relocation is in the debug info. So the 60 Hz camera read its own copy, `{0, 0, 0, 100}`: `FreeCameraOn` was false, and `LookInvert` used invert Y 0. The menu's writes (`controls_apply`) went to the game's copy, which only the 30 Hz camera reads.
  - So on the Vita the free camera has worked in off and interpolated since D47, and never at 60 HZ (D130 on). The host never shows it. D130's report, "1 definition weakened", was this one.
  - **Host repro** (`logs/t60a/cam30`, `cam60`: a new game, then `rx=255`, `rx=0`, `ry=0` and `lx=255` with `--analog --free-camera --cam-invert-x --cam-invert-y`, `--peek 80075924`, the turn): `D_80075924` is 0x3F8 and −0x3F0 while the stick is held, at 30 Hz and at 60 Hz, before and after the fix: the host has symbol relocations.
- **The free camera's fix:**
  - `PortControls g_port_controls` is defined in `port.c`, compiled once. `camera_hand.c` declares it (`port_controls.h`), so both rule sets read the game's copy through the symbol.
  - `tick60_link.py` now exits with an error if the set defines any global data, naming it, instead of weakening it. The set's globals are the game's, through an extern or a common symbol (the decomp's `-fcommon` globals, 4 of them, never placed in a section). Its report has no "data weakened" group any more.
  - The Vita object after the fix (`build/vita/tick60_update.o`): `.data` 0 bytes, `g_port_controls` an undefined symbol with 42 relocations against it.
  - **Vita3K** (`logs/t60a/v3k-fc60`, this VPK, `interp=4 analog=1 free_camera=1 cam_invert_x=1 cam_invert_y=1`, the same pad script; the script's sticks go through `read_sticks` and the game's calibration as a real stick's do): the camera turns round while `rx=255` is held and back with `rx=0` (shots 6400 to 6580); `tick60: 275 updates at 60 Hz`. The same run on main's VPK wasn't made; the object above is the evidence that it couldn't turn.
- **Sparx's cause:** D130 judged Sparx (class 120) for 60 Hz (`0014`: his wing trail once a tick, the glow, the chase and fetch steps and the follow lerp shared), but the 60 Hz rules ran only the pilot's six levels' `UpdateMoby` (`T60_LEVELS`). Elsewhere every moby ran the original's update on the vblank that starts a tick (`AT_30`), so Sparx moved every other vblank, two vblanks' worth at once, while Spyro moved every vblank.
- **Sparx's fix:**
  - `cmake/tick60.cmake` builds every overlay's `moby_update.c` into the 60 Hz object (35 levels), and `src/game/tick60/t60_levels.h` lists them, with a pilot flag, for `tick60.c` (the game's `UpdateMoby_*`) and `t60_rules.c` (the set's copies); CMake checks that the list matches the overlays.
  - `t60_update_moby` notes whether the level is one of the pilot's (`s_t60_pilot`, scratch). There every class judged for 60 Hz runs every vblank, as before. In the others only `t60_class_60_everywhere`'s classes do, which is Sparx; every other class runs the original's rules on the vblank that starts a tick, through the 60 Hz object's own loop, as the pilot's 30 Hz classes always have.
  - Sparx's body is one `case` of `moby_update.c` in every overlay (the overlays differ only in which classes they keep, and no `IS_LEVEL_*` switch touches it), so D130's judgment of it holds everywhere.
  - **Running every level through the 60 Hz loop changes nothing by itself:** a build with `t60_class_60_everywhere` returning 0 (`logs/bin-off`, not committed) ran the Gnorc Gnexus script at 60 Hz with main's trace, moby trace, log and 85 of 85 shots byte for byte (`logs/t60a/sp-off-60` against `sp-main-60`).
  - **Gnorc Gnexus** (`logs/t60a/sparx.txt`: a new game, the warp cheat to level 60, then running down, left, down and right; `logs/t60a/sparx_stats.py` over `--moby-trace`, vblanks 7500–7990, while Spyro runs and no run is in a cutscene):

    | | 30 Hz | 60 Hz, main | 60 Hz, fixed |
    |---|---:|---:|---:|
    | Vblanks Spyro moves on which Sparx doesn't | 2% (every tick moves both) | **51%** | 1% |
    | Sparx's step, mean | 127.5 a tick (two vblanks) | 64.5 a vblank, by turns 0 and ~129 | 63.8 a vblank |
    | Step-to-step change, of the step | — | 201% | **5%** |
    | Distance to Spyro, every 40 vblanks | 613–1,625 | 612–1,187 | 608–1,234 |

    Sparx's speed and follow distance are the 30 Hz ones; at 60 Hz main he stood still every other vblank and then jumped. From the first 60 Hz vblank in the level the random seed differs from main's (Sparx's update now runs, and draws, every vblank), so a Gnorc Gnexus rat wanders elsewhere, Spyro's run meets it at 7548 and the fixed run frees a dragon at 8000 that main's run doesn't reach. Main's two runs also fetch with Sparx far off (up to 38,000 units) where the fixed run's doesn't: the same random difference.
  - **Cost:** the Vita's `.text` grows by 843 KB (4,100,260 bytes in PAL R3's build of main's code, 4,943,324 here) for the 29 more levels' copies; 189 functions made local in the 60 Hz object (159 before). In the 29 levels the moby list is now built every vblank, as it has been in the pilot's levels since D130 (`func_80051FEC`, then the animation by rate); not yet measured on hardware.
- **Checks:**
  - **Off:** `tools/host_checks.sh` with main `3f70f0d`'s binary and this branch's: **287 of 287 files identical** (demo 28, the whole-game tour 143, routes A and B 58 each; the logs with their folder names masked; `logs/t60ahc-{main,fix}-*`).
  - **The oracle replay** (`s8e-cap`): **75 PASS, 0 FAIL, 20 NOEXIT** on both binaries, the summaries identical (`logs/caps/s8e-cap-fix`, and the main worktree's copy).
  - `tools/tick60_cpp_check.py`: **10 of 11** files preprocess to main's tokens; `camera_hand.c` differs where `g_port_controls`'s definition was, by design (it moved to `port.c`). A first run, made while the presenter check ran, also reported `moby_helpers.c`; run again it was the same as main's, and the patched copies of both trees are identical.
  - The 30 Hz runs of the Gnorc Gnexus script are identical on both binaries (`sp-main-30`, `sp-new-30`: trace, moby trace and 85 of 85 shots).
  - **Interpolated:** `tools/pres_check_ab.sh MODE=chk`, main's binary as the reference: **183 of 183 files identical** (demo 26, wsi 26, gems 19, routes A and B 56 each), and `pres check` **30,229 of 30,229 frames identical** (6,169, 6,169, 4,553, 6,669, 6,669) (`logs/t60apc-*`, `logs/t60a/pres_check_ab.txt`).
  - **ctest:** 29 passed and `gte` skipped (no `logs/gte/gte.bin` in this worktree), of 30; `savestate_globals` lists `s_t60_pilot` with the rules' other scratch.
  - **Vita:** the VPK builds (`tools/ci/build_vpk.sh`, native memory) and the 60 Hz object links with no data of its own but its scratch.
- **Not checked:** hardware (proposed in the PR, the owner's yes first): the free camera at 60 HZ, and Sparx and the game thread's time in a level outside the pilot (Gnorc Gnexus); Sparx's gem fetch at 60 Hz outside the pilot's levels against 30 Hz (the fetch runs above went different ways on the random stream); the other classes D130 judged, still at 30 Hz outside the pilot's levels.

### D144. Every moby class runs at 60 Hz in the 60 Hz mode, in every level, but the portal path moby. The pilot's 59 class bodies run in every level, not only Artisans' six (a body is the same in every overlay), and the other 157 were judged as the pilot's were, by twelve subagents from one brief with every edit checked mechanically: 156 at 60 Hz (261 edits, `patches/spyro-1-tick60/0001`, a new series applied after the region patches), 1 at 30. Four helpers that moved a fixed amount a call have a 60 Hz form (the frame-counted path step in `t60_rules.c`, three path and target movers in `0002`), and so does pushing a cannon on Spyro's side. Off and interpolated run main's code
- **Why:** after D142 the owner played at 60 HZ on the Vita (2026-11-11): Sparx and the free camera were right, but "the enemy i was chasing for the key looked a little stuttery" (the key thieves of Gnasty Gnorc and Gnasty's Loot, classes 159 and 179). Outside the pilot's levels every moby but Sparx ran the original's rules every other vblank. Asked whether to judge every class, one world at a time, the owner said "Yes, all of them". The owner also asked, once this is done, for a check of whether everything is set up for 60 Hz, to decide on making 60 Hz the default.
- **How** (`docs/tick60.md`, "Every class"):
  - **Step A:** `t60_levels.h` lost its pilot column; `t60_class_60` alone decides, in every level.
  - **Step B:** twelve batches by homeworld (~1,500–2,700 lines each), each a subagent writing its edits as data from one brief; a checker applied an edit only inside its class's body and refused any whose 30 Hz reading isn't the original's tokens. The pilot's case-label count had missed GCC ranges (`case 83 ... 87:`): 16 bodies, the gems among them, were added as a twelfth batch.
  - **The patch series:** `patches/spyro-1-tick60/` is applied after `spyro-1-region-port`, so the 60 Hz sites are written on PAL's `RT()` text and no region patch changes. A later region edit inside a marked line would need `0001` regenerated.
  - **Helpers:** `func_8003BFC0` (the frame-counted path step: trains, planes, boat Gnorcs, the Thief Rocket and five more) takes its frame whole on a tick's first vblank and moves half, the rest moved before the moby's next update (`t60_path_step`, `g_t60.path`). `MoveMobyAlongPath` (its smoothed velocity updated once a tick), `func_8003A16C` and `MobyMoveTowardTarget` share their steps and turns (`0002`). Spyro pushing a cannon (`UpdateCircleMoby`) shares his turn.
  - **Verdicts:** 104 bodies at 60 with sites, 52 with none, 1 at 30 (398, which aims Spyro through a portal, D141). Four bodies take a step whole once a tick because it can't be split without new state (the fireworks rocket, the storm cloud's outward step, the ambient sound emitter, the cannon shot's tracer).
- **Checks:** `tools/tick60_cpp_check.py`: **11 of 11** files preprocess to main's tokens (`moby_update.c` 362,882 tokens, `moby_helpers.c`, `pete_hand.c` among them). The host build and link pass (189 functions made local). The rest are running as the PR opens and are added to this entry and the PR when they finish: `tools/host_checks.sh` against main's binary (off), `tools/pres_check_ab.sh MODE=chk` (interpolated), the oracle replay, ctest, a 30 Hz against 60 Hz tour standing 1,800 vblanks in all 35 levels with `tools/tick60_stats.py`, and the Vita build.
- **Not checked:** hardware (proposed in the PR, the owner's yes first): the key thieves and Gnasty at 60 HZ, and the game thread's time in the heavier levels now that every moby updates every vblank.

## 2026-11-11 — the PAL disc: R2, part B, the languages

### D139. Stage R2 of `docs/regions-plan.md`, part B: PAL's five languages in the main executable and the title. Every function PAL rewrote around its language tables is decomp C that compiles, with `-DREGION=1`, to PAL's own code (constants included, and every relocated field aimed where PAL's is): the merged letter spawner with its accents, the pause, options, screen-position and inventory pages, the game-over, save, transition, balloon-ride and demo screens, the floating level names, and the title overlay with its language select and card header. Our hand C takes PAL's card header write, the dubbed dragons' voice timing, the credits' 512×256 copy and retune and Spyro's three added sounds. PAL's text and tables are read from the player's executable through `PAL_DATA()`; PAL stays refused for players (`PsxRegion.supported = 0`) until R4
- **Why:** R2 is PAL's language and picture (plan §9); part A (D134) did the headers, the picture and the shared fixes. The owner asked for part B on main in 2–3 PRs (2026-11-11). This is the first; the level overlays' dialogue (the balloonist, the flight results) is D144's PR.
- **The owner's decisions it relies on** (plan §10, unanswered; the recommendations as defaults): 1, approach (c); 5, data from the disc through committed maps; **7, the port's own menu stays English** (the recommendation: English at first, the European languages' accents later, stage P). Decision 6 is still open: everything here is checked on the one PAL image `52bba2f6…` (executable `fdd04c04…`, `WAD.WAD` `f1fc05d7…`).
- **One patch:** `patches/spyro-1-region/0007-pal-text-title-menus.patch` (`draw.c`, `update.c`, `init.c`, `hud.c`, `initialization.c`, `moby_helpers.c`, `overlays/titlescreen.c`, `include/loaders.h`, `include/titlescreen.h`, and the PAL build's stand-ins in `main.c`). D139's first commit, the merged spawner and the rescued dragon's line, is `0006-pal-text-mobys.patch` (renamed from R2's `0004`, which sorted before R3's `0004`).
- **How a PAL change is written** (D133's switches, used as part A did):
  - a rewritten function keeps its US name and takes PAL's body first, `if (REGION_IS(PAL)) { … return; }` under `#if REGION_HAS_PAL`, then the US body under `#if REGION_HAS_US`;
  - an edit inside a function is `if (REGION_IS(PAL)) { PAL } else { US }`, or a guard `if (!REGION_IS(PAL))` on the US side. **In the port both sides are compiled**, so two plain `#if` blocks would run the US code after PAL's on a PAL disc: the first drafts did that in two places (the demo's DEMO MODE text and the title flyby's tick, which advanced twice) and are fixed; every pair in the patch was checked;
  - a value is `RV(us, pal, jp)`; a 50 Hz retune `RT(us, pal)`;
  - PAL's data is `PAL_DATA(g_PalSomething, 0x8007XXXX)`: the port reads the copy `region_data_load_disc` made of the player's executable (D133), the PAL matching build links a stand-in. About 150 tables: the five-pointer string tables (EN, FR, DE, ES, IT), per-language x positions, widths and ticks, the level and home-world names, the title's 75 sprites, the card header template. None of their content is in the repo.
  - PAL-only variables are real globals in the port (`region_game.c`): `g_PalCardHeaderDirty` (PAL 0x8007C148: the title's language select or the pause menu's screen position changed) and `g_PalTitleCardLoad` (0x8007BFF0: the title's read of the card header at boot). Both are in the save states' `REGN` chunk.
- **What PAL does** (read from its code and proved by compiling; the tables and per-function notes are in `docs/translation-log.md`):
  - **Text:** every 3D-letter text is the language's, through the merged spawner with shade 11; positions come from per-language tables, and the letters a wiggle walks are the ones actually made (accents included). Accents are Moby classes 365–369: their models are in PAL's `PETE` entry of the WAD (59 shared models against US's 54), which `func_8005B7D8` already loads into `g_Models`.
  - **The 256-line picture:** the freeze-frame grabs, the game-over copies, the fade, the HUD's egg model and gem total, the transition screen's layout are PAL's.
  - **The pause menu** gains a 6th option row, SCREEN ADJUST: a page with HORIZONTAL and VERTICAL (left/right, 0–32 in steps of 1), which writes `g_ScreenOffsetX/Y` and both DISPENVs' `screen.x/y` live, and marks the card header changed. **The port keeps it as a stored no-op on screen** (plan §7.4): its DISPENV `screen` offsets aren't used (`libgpu.c` keeps them 0 and the presentation places the picture), so the value is kept, saved and restored as on the PS1, and moves nothing on a Vita whose picture fills the screen.
  - **The title** (WAD entry 2, its own overlay): START GAME, and under it the language (left/right step it mod 5, load its 160×256 text strip, mark the header changed); every card prompt is a sprite from the language's strip; the card file is `BESCES-01438SPYRO`; at boot the overlay reads the card file's header and restores the language and screen position from it; the flyby's times are PAL's.
  - **The card header** (plan §3.6, checked): PAL's template (0x80074C24) is the standard header, `SC`, one icon frame, one block. The language and screen position, `lang + (x << 4) + (y << 10)`, go in the halfword at +0x7C, the icon palette's colour 14; +0x7E (colour 15) keeps the save's id, as on US. The save stays one block. After a good save, if the header changed, the fairy's save writes it again (`PalCardHeaderWrite`, hand C).
  - **Cutscenes and voices:** the intro and endings are per language (D134's slots); the dubbed dragons' voices start at a per-language tick, props words 17–20 over 16 unless −1, scaled to 25 Hz ticks (×5/6 from 6 on, `RT`).
  - **Also:** the attract demo's end reloads the title's sprite sheet and language strips itself (PAL's title resumes at sub-state 2); the letterbox bars and the VRAM restores when play resumes are PAL's 256 lines; the boot logo is PAL's own image (SCEE's, 12,516 bytes, `Initialize`); the fairy menu's view (`InitFairyCutscene`); quitting a level from the pause menu with every gem sets the level's vortex flag; the transition gems' record is PAL's (`TransGemPal`) and their flight is drawn from their age; the demo's end clears 512×256.
- **The hand C** (no compiler to prove it; read against PAL's code side by side): `func_800314B4` (the card header), `func_8002F3E4` (the voice delay, the cutscene Spyro's floor kept in `g_CollisionPoint.z`, the cutscene dragon at 0 ticks a frame holding its first frame), `func_credits_8007BFD0` and `func_credits_8007AA50` (the 512×256 copy, the 656-byte level header through `LEVEL_HEADER_COPY`, and the letters' fly-in and fall retuned ×1.2 and ×5/6 with `RT`), `func_8003EA68` (PAL's three added sounds and level 43's supercharge gravity, which R3 left marked) `func_80043FE4` (the wing trail's vertices, 89 and 100 in PAL's model) and `func_80050BD0`, the cyclorama (its screen height, 256: R1 had found its four 240s, `compare_code.py --strict` finds 16).
- **Left for later:** PAL 60 keeps the title flyby's PAL literals (they're the 50 Hz cutscene data's, as D138's note on PAL 60's cutscenes says); the port's menu in the other languages (decision 7, stage P); the screen position as a visible option.
- **Checked** (on the one PAL image `52bba2f6…`, executable `fdd04c04…`; logs in `logs/r2b/`):
  - **Against the PAL disc, per function** (`build/r2c`'s scratch tools: a `-DREGION=1` compile of the file compared with the executable or overlay word for word, constants included, every relocation's target checked; tables in `docs/translation-log.md`): every decomp function above exact but `func_8001D718` (Zoe's save menu), which differs only by a register swap and one instruction's place in two of its pages.
  - **The full `REGION=pal` build** (`logs/r2b/pal-base/` before, `logs/r2b/pal-pr1b/` after): the main executable's functions the same as PAL's, constants included, **533 → 552** of 582 (masked 544 → 558, strictly 531 → 550); the title overlay 3 → **5 of 5**. The functions still different are the US-only spawners PAL merged, `func_8001D718`, the hand-written renderers that differ in relocations only, R3's hand C, and code with no PAL text (`LoadLevel`, the CD functions' `printf`s, `SetOverlayPointers`).
  - **The US build** with `-DREGION=0`: every edited file object-for-object identical to before (`uscheck`), and the matching build `PSX.EXE SHA-1 OK` (`logs/r2b/matching-us-pr1b.log`).
  - **The host against PCSX-Redux, in all five languages** (`--region-dev`; Redux's pad scripts shifted 1,450 vblanks for its BIOS boot; `logs/r2b/redux-pal-*`, `logs/r2b/host-pal-*-s`): the title (START GAME / COMMENCER / SPIEL BEGINNEN / … and the language between arrows), the card prompts and slots, the intro's captions, Artisans, the pause menu and the inventory read the same words at the same places. **These host runs need R4's data fills** (D143, #128, not on main yet): without them PAL's Spyro animation tables are the US ones and the host crashes in Spyro's renderer just after the title's flyby. They were run with R4's working fills applied to a copy of this branch only; this PR holds none of R4's code.
  - **The memory card:** a PAL new game on the host writes `save/pal/card0/BESCES-01438SPYRO`, one block, its first 0x7E bytes identical to Redux's card (the template, the icon, the language word: 0x4041 French, 0x4040 English); +0x7E, the save's random id, differs.
  - **The attract demo's end** on the host (cross pressed during the demo): back to the PAL title with its sprites (before the demo end's change, the title came back without them).
  - **US host checks** (`tools/host_checks.sh`, the demo, the tour, routes A and B): **287 of 287 files byte-identical** to main `3f70f0d`'s (`logs/r2b-main-*` against `logs/r2b-pr1c-*`, the logs with their folder and binary names masked; `logs/r2b/hc-cmp-pr1c.txt`). After merging main's D142: see the PR.
  - **`tools/check_no_assets.sh`:** clean.

## 2026-11-11 — the PAL disc: R2, part C, the overlays' dialogue

### D145. Stage R2 of `docs/regions-plan.md`, part C: the level overlays' PAL text, the balloonist's dialogue and the flight levels' results and time-attack screens, as decomp C that compiles with `-DREGION=1` to PAL's own code in every overlay that builds it (`patches/spyro-1-region/0008-pal-overlay-dialogue.patch`), with the 50 Hz timing sites R3 left in those two files (D137) as `RT()`. PAL's flight overlays count letters through a function the executable points to, which `SetOverlayPointers` now sets for PAL. PAL stays refused for players until R4
- **Why:** the second of R2 part B's PRs (D139's "level overlays' dialogue"); R3 left `balloonist.c` and `flight.c`'s timing sites to it because R2 rewrites them (D137).
- **The owner's decisions it relies on:** as D139 (1, 5 and 7 by default; 6 open; checked on PAL image `52bba2f6…`).
- **What PAL does** (read from the overlays and proved by compiling them; the per-screen layouts are in `docs/translation-log.md`):
  - **The balloonist** (`Balloonist4`, PAL's own body): his name banner, "<name> THE BALLOONIST" in the language; his dialogue three lines a page from a table by row and language, each line measured by building it and centred, the box sized to the widest line (German and Spanish lines are longer, so their boxes are wider); the world menu ("WHERE TO, SPYRO?"/"NEED A LIFT?", STAY HERE and the open worlds, two openings where US had three) and the go menus ("GO TO <world or level>"; French's "go to" is empty). `Balloonist3`: the climb in and the step out at 25 Hz (`RT`: lerp 102 → 86 and 84, walk 48 → 40, hop step 32 → 38, step out 64 → 54).
  - **The flight results** (`Flight5`, PAL's own body): the title, objectives, ALL IN ONE, TOTAL, the press-X line (the X placed per language: German's comes first) and TRY AGAIN with its own YES/NO, at PAL's x; the time attack's BEST TIME, YOUR TIME and NEW RECORD. Its freeze frame is PAL's 512×256. `Flight4`: the time shown from a 50-a-second timer (`RT`: 36000/600/6 → 30000/500/5). `Flight2`: the 256-line restore. `Flight3`: PAL clears Spyro's head-look target and resets the camera on try again.
  - **The letter counter** (`func_pal_80081758`, new in every flight overlay): a string's letters, accents ('a'–'f') not counted. PAL's `SetOverlayPointers` stores it in a pointer at PAL 0x8007C0B8 for each flight level; here `g_PalLetterCount` (`region_game.c`, in the save states' `REGN` chunk; the PAL matching build's stand-in in `main.c`).
- **Checked:**
  - **Against the PAL overlays, per function, constants included, relocations' targets checked:** `Balloonist1`–`4` exact in all ten overlays that build them (LEVEL_10, 11, 13, 20, 21, 22, 30, 40, 50, 60); `func_pal_80081758` and `Flight1`–`5` exact in all five flight overlays (LEVEL_15, 25, 35, 45, 55).
  - **US:** `balloonist.c`, `flight.c` (several overlays each) and `overlay_pointers.c` object-identical with `-DREGION=0`; the matching build and host checks: see the PR.
- **Not checked on the host against Redux:** reaching a balloonist and a flight level's results in each language needs a route through the game; R4's per-language tours compare them with Redux.

## 2026-11-11 — the PAL disc: R4, the whole game (first part)

### D143. Stage R4 of `docs/regions-plan.md`, first part: PAL's own data tables come from the player's executable as R1's map's fills (41 tables, from 4), 29 of them only at PAL's own timing; with them the PAL disc plays every level, both endings and the credits on the host, and its attract demos and a new game in Vita3K. The checks' tooling takes the PAL disc: the oracle's addresses (`ORACLE_REGION=pal`), the host tour's warp, PAL pad scripts, the Vita's `region_dev=1`. PAL stays refused for players: its demos don't match the PS1 yet, because the level overlays' PAL-only code (D137's "inserted sequences") isn't ported
- **Why:** R4 (plan §8, §9), the owner's request (2026-11-11): check that the whole PAL game plays, then turn PAL on. R2 part B (the languages, D139) is being written in parallel, so this part does what needs no language: the oracle at 50 Hz, the demos, the routes and the tour in English, PAL 60, saves, the developer switches, Vita3K.
- **The owner's decisions it relies on** (plan §10, unanswered; the recommendations as defaults): 1, approach (c); 2, PAL at 25 Hz by default, PAL 60 an option; 4, `save/pal/`; 5, data through committed maps. **Decision 6 is still open:** everything here ran on the one PAL image, `.bin` SHA-1 `52bba2f6…`, executable `fdd04c04…`, `WAD.WAD` `f1fc05d7…`.
- **The crash:** the first PAL level on the host (the Stone Hill demo, the new game's Artisans) crashed in Spyro's renderer (`SpyroDraw` → `LinkPrim`, `pete_render_hand.c:508`): a depth bucket 1,674 of 288. The asm translation (`--fast-render 0`) crashed the same, so not the hand C. Spyro's models and their tables were byte-identical to PCSX-Redux's at the same point (a RAM dump 10 vblanks after the demo's level, `logs/r4/rx-dump`); his body was at frame 9 of animation 16, which has 9 frames. PAL's animations are shorter (its model has fewer frames, made for 25 Hz) and PAL's executable says so in its own `spyro_AnimationDetails` (16 ends at 8, US at 10); the port still had the US table, so `func_8003CB24` stepped him past the model's last frame.
- **Every table:** a word-by-word alignment of the two executables' data (pointers masked, `logs/r4/data_region_diff.txt`) and `data_map.py --aligned` (new: also the functions that aren't the same, through their difflib-aligned blocks) list 52 labels whose contents differ. The fills (`tools/regions/make_map.py`, `region_map_pal.c`, regenerated):
  - **PAL's in both PAL modes** (model, picture, PAL's recordings): `spyro_AnimationDetails`, `D_8006BC84` (the animations' transitions), `D_8006CB8C` (the sky's clip, 240 → 256), `g_HudMobyTargetPos` and `g_HudEggTargetRect` (the HUD in the 512×256 picture), `g_DemoLengths` (PAL's demos are its own recordings: 720, 760, 768, 736 ticks), `D_8006F140` (two of `LoadLevelScene`'s sound entries), `D_8006E52C` (six of the particle spawner's positions, +144 in x; no function `data_map` pairs reads it, so its PAL address is given and the 16 bytes on each side must be US's).
  - **PAL 50 only, a new `timing` flag** (`RegionFill.timing`, `region_data.c` copies them only when `g_RegionTiming`; PAL 60 keeps the US tables, as `RT()` keeps the US constant): Spyro's slope speeds `D_8006C5B8`–`C5CC` (×1.2; `func_8003EA68`, `func_80047B60`), the turn rates `D_8006C5F0` (`UpdateSpyroTurnMomentum`), `D_8006C6F8` (turn on the spot), `D_8006C704`–`C710` (flight turns), the camera's 18 tables `D_8006C754`–`C820` (×1.2, ×1.44; `func_80034CE8`). R3's sites (D135) were constants in code, so these tables weren't seen.
  - `--region-check` (`logs/r4/region-check`): both PAL layouts, exit 0: 41 tables (3,920 bytes) checked against the executable.
  - **Not fills:** the text, title, card and game-over tables (`g_FairyDialogueBoxSizes`, `g_BalloonistDialogueIndices`, `g_TitlescreenSprites`, `g_MemCardSaveHeader`, `g_HomeworldNamesLength`, the PAL-only data after `g_GameOverLetterDisplayTicks`) are R2 part B's (told). The Sony logo's image `D_8006FCF4` is a different size in PAL (12,516 bytes, US 12,704): not a fill; the boot logo is still the US image. `D_800730C4` (section sizes) and `_ramsize` aren't game tables. The level overlays' data (after each overlay's last function) is PAL's apart from trailing padding, in all 37 overlays.
- **The oracle on PAL:** `capture.lua` with `ORACLE_REGION=pal` reads PAL's addresses for the trace, the pad's state anchors and the endings (`data_map.py`'s placements; `g_CutsceneIdx` 0x8007BF34 and `g_CreditsSequence` 0x8007C078 by alignment, each checked against its neighbours' spacing), and moves a pad script's `poke:` on `g_Spyro` to PAL's `g_Spyro`, so one script serves the host (US layout) and the emulator. PAL has no warp code, so the oracle's tour takes no level numbers there.
- **The host's tour on PAL** (`tour.c`): PAL's inventory has no warp (D134), so a level is reached with the options menu's warp (`port_cheat_request`, D50), asked for again whenever none is pending until the level changes (a flight level's timer can end first; the game drops the cheat, and its results get Cross). US runs the old path.
- **Pad scripts:** `newgame_pal_host_{en,fr,de,es,it}.txt` (the host: a new game in each language through PAL's title, R2 part B's flow, D139; the first version, from the US title overlay before #129, gone) (PAL 60's, steered before #129, is to redo on PAL's title), `newgame_pal.txt` (Redux), `tools/regions/pad_pal.py` (a US route's anchored offsets ×5/6), `route_portal_exit_pal.txt` (route A, cut where the portal takes Spyro), `route_vortex_pal.txt` (route B's first half).
- **The Vita's `region_dev=1`** (`main.c`, `config.txt` only, no menu row): the host's `--region-dev`, for test runs. Off by default.
- **The host's PAL save folder:** with `--save DIR` on the PAL disc the cards and states go to `DIR/pal` (D132), but nothing made it: the cards make one folder level and the states none, so a fresh `DIR` gave "CANNOT WRITE STATE 1" and no card. `main.c` now makes both, as the Vita does (`main.c`, `savestate_vita.c`). US runs are unchanged (`DIR` itself is the card folder).
- **`savestate_compare.py`** takes the audio's 882 frames a vblank from a 50 Hz run's log.
- **Checked:**

Logging every rand() call with its caller on both sides (a Redux breakpoint on PAL's rand, 0x80067B5C; a temporary host build) finds one difference in tick 1: Sparx calls rand() 3 times a tick on the host and 4 times on PAL. PAL's fourth is in a 19-instruction sequence PAL inserted (PAL 0x8008BC40–0x8008BC8C in LEVEL_11), one of the level overlays' "inserted sequences" D137 left. In the moby update functions there are about 154 unique ones (~2,850 PAL instructions; R3's `compare-wad-strict.json`), plus D137's 542 constant sites. On the host Spyro then dies twice in the Stone Hill demo, which he doesn't on the PS1. **That port is its own stage, proposed to the owner** (R3 part 4); until it lands no PAL demo or route can match the PS1 tick for tick.

So PAL 60 has US speeds but PAL's animations: those play in 5/6 of US's ticks, about 20 % faster at 30 Hz. A note for the owner, not changed here.
