## 2026-11-11 — the PAL disc: R3, part 4 (the overlays' PAL code)

### D146. Stage R3, part 4: the level overlays' PAL code. The sequences PAL inserted or rewrote in `moby_update.c`, `moby_spawn.c` and `particle_spawn.c`, and the timing sites D137 left there, are `REGION_IS(PAL)`, `RV()` and `RT()` in the decomp's C, so that with `-DREGION=1` the level overlays compile to PAL's own code. Of the 35 level overlays' functions from those sources, all but two are strictly PAL's, constants included: `UpdateMoby` 33 of 35, `SpawnMoby`, `SpawnParticle` and `UpdateParticle` 35 of 35 each. Counting every function in the WAD's overlays (R2's balloonist, flight and title ones included), 249 of 253 are PAL's (146 before), and no constant differs outside the credits overlay (1,913 before). Two new macros take the retunes PAL computes: `RT_TIME(t)` (a duration's 5/6, with PAL's rounding and its second evaluation of `t`) and `RT_SPEED(v)` (a speed's 6/5)
- **Why:** R4 (D143) found the PAL attract demos leave the PS1's path on the first tick of play. Sparx draws rand() four times a tick on PAL and three on the host; the fourth is in a sequence PAL inserted (LEVEL_11 0x8008BC40). D137 had left those sequences (about 154 in the moby update functions) and 542 constant sites. Until they're ported no PAL demo or route can match the PS1 tick for tick, and PAL stays refused for players.
- **Method:** each round was `REGION=pal tools/build_matching.sh`, then `retune.py sites wad` (D135): every overlay function aligned with PAL's, constants compared strictly, each difference mapped to its C line through the build's DWARF. Each was read beside PAL's MIPS and written as one of these:
  - `RT(us, pal)` when PAL's value is US's ×6/5 or ×5/6 (rounded): the 50 Hz retune. PAL 60 keeps US's value.
  - `RT_TIME()` or `RT_SPEED()` when PAL scales a value the code computes: a random duration, a path node's speed, a thrown arc.
  - `RV(us, pal, jp)` when no timing formula gives PAL's value: a release change, which PAL 60 keeps.
  - `REGION_IS(PAL)` for code PAL added or dropped, with `#if REGION_HAS_PAL` where US's build mustn't see a symbol.
  
  Two gcc 2.7.2 habits mattered:
  - It merges identical blocks, so a PAL block that "jumps elsewhere" was a twin that had been retuned in one copy only.
  - It keeps a much-used constant in a saved register. LEVEL_61's 445 unpaired instructions (a whole function's registers) came from three `MobyMoveWithGravity` arguments that PAL changes from 16 to 19. With them retuned, gcc keeps 2 in `$fp`, as PAL's code does.
- **`RT_TIME(t)`:** `t >= 6 ? (t * 5 + 3) / 6 : t`, PAL's own macro, with its branches in PAL's order. PAL evaluates `t` once for the test and again for the value, so a random duration (`rand()`, `RandRange()`) draws twice on PAL. That is Sparx's fourth rand(), and the butterflies' and the ambient emitters' extra draws. The macro does the same at PAL 50; US, JP and PAL 60 evaluate `t` once, as US's code does.
- **What PAL changed besides the retune** (each a `REGION_IS(PAL)` or an `RV()`; the patch has the rest):
  - **The accents:** classes 365–369 are the accents 'a'–'e' in PAL's text. `overlays/mobys.yaml` names them "Accent a..e (PAL)" in every overlay, and they're spawned and updated with the letters (`case 365 ... 369` under `REGION_HAS_PAL`).
  - **The crystal dragons' props** (class 250): PAL's props hold the second cutscene's length in the four other languages after `m_CutsceneTicks` (D134). This class's shake timer and stored angles are therefore four words further on (`DRAGON_SHAKE`, `DRAGON_ANGLES`), in a patch of their own in both builds' series (`0010-pal-dragon-props.patch`).
  - **The egg thieves** (class 33):
    - their speed by level: 0xEA in Peace Keepers, 0xF0 in Dry Canyon, 0xF6 elsewhere (US 0xCD in all);
    - a turn of their own at a path's end (`m_0x30`);
    - their own catch-up speed.
  - **Language and dialogue:**
    - the ending cutscenes by language (`g_CutsceneIdx = g_Language * 3 + 2` for Gnasty Gnorc, `+ 3` for Gnasty's Loot's ending);
    - an added sound in Gnasty Gnorc's fight, in English only;
    - the Gnorc Gnexus balloonist's random top line;
    - the five homeworld balloonists' greeting, one of 2 (US 3).
  - **Moved or dropped behaviour:**
    - Toasty's Shepherd moves with `MobyMoveWithGravity` (US: `MobyMove`), and each charge goes 0x78 further.
    - Toasty's Sheepdog drops a homing gem and isn't marked killed, and drops nothing when it falls out.
    - The Slap-Happy Armored Monk flips its `m_0x10` when it comes back, not when it's hit.
    - The Gnorc-Adier snaps to the ground under 0x800.
    - Jacques also updates his collision and skips an unused distance.
    - The Metalback Spiders play two sounds US doesn't.
  - **Moby fields set:**
    - The Gnorc's charge knockback sets `m_CollisionRange` 1.
    - The Armoured Ice Gnorc sets `m_DepthOffset` −2 as it lands.
    - The TNT and Gnorc Cove barrels clear `m_Rotation.y` as they roll.
  - **Gnasty's tracers:** the tracer projectiles' rows are 125 points in Gnasty Gnorc's fight (US 150) and 121 for the Dream Weavers cannon (US 145).
  - **Values that aren't timing** (`RV`): the Key Thief's (Gnasty) speeds, the Kiss Fairy's path distance, the Tree Tops thieves', Toasty's, the Cauldron's, the Armored Horrors' frame range.
  - **Order only** (no behaviour): the Machine Gunner and the Barrel Dispenser set two fields in PAL's order, under `REGION_IS`, for the strict match.
  - **R3's mistakes, corrected:**
    - Doctor Shemp's attack test was `RT(9, 6)`; PAL keeps 9.
    - In the port, R3's `0005` had left three `MobyMoveWithGravity` calls in Artisans' 60 Hz classes at `0xC, 0xC`. They're `RT()` now, as in the matching build.
- **The port:** the matching build's edit is the reference.
  - The port's `moby_update.c` gets the same change by a three-way merge: the port's file before, the matching build's before, the matching build's after.
  - The conflicts were resolved by hand: the three calls above, and four 60 Hz lines where `T60_SHARE()` now shares PAL's value (`T60_SHARE(RT(100, 120))` and similar).
  - The patches are `patches/spyro-1-region/0009` (the other four files, and what both builds share), `spyro-1-region-matching/0009` and `0010`, and `spyro-1-region-port/0009` and `0010`. `spyro-1-tick60/0001` was regenerated over them the same way.
- **The PAL matching build links again:** R2's PAL `flight.c` (D145) calls `strcpy`, which the decomp doesn't define. `build_matching.sh` gives PAL's link `--defsym=strcpy=0x80067B9C` (PsyQ's libc in PAL's executable).
- **What's left in the overlays** (`compare-wad-strict.txt`):
  - `UpdateMoby_LEVEL_21` (2 instructions): the Musket Gnorc's (Dry Canyon) two frame stores, in another register and order.
  - `UpdateMoby_LEVEL_64` (1 instruction): the Key Thief's (Gnasty's Loot) speed constant, loaded later.
  
  Both have the same values and stores as PAL's: gcc's scheduling, not behaviour. The credits overlay's two functions (36 constant sites) are hand C in the port (`credits_hand.c`, retuned in D139) and asm in the matching build, so they're outside these sources.
- **PAL 60 and the tracer rows:** `RT()` keeps US's 150- and 145-point rows under PAL 60, with PAL's code around them. Whether PAL's props hold three US-sized rows isn't checked; the props are the level's own, from PAL's WAD. PAL 60 is a developer mode until R4 turns PAL on.
- **Not judged for 60 Hz:** PAL's added code (the egg thieves' turn, the Shepherd's move, the added resets) wasn't judged as D144's classes were. `--tick60` is off on the PAL disc (D138), so nothing runs it yet; it's a follow-up for when PAL gets 60 Hz.
- **Checked:**
  - **The patches:** both series, applied in their real order, rebuild the edited trees exactly. Matching: `moby_update.c`, `moby_spawn.c`, `particle_spawn.c`, `region.h` and `mobys.yaml` are equal to the edit. Port and port + tick60: `moby_update.c` is equal to the merged files. The merge was audited: the port-vs-matching differences after it are the same as before it, apart from the resolved conflicts.
  - **Matching builds:** US `PSX.EXE SHA-1 OK` (`logs/pal-ov/final-us-matching.txt`). `REGION=pal`: 249 of 253 strictly the same (`logs/pal-ov/final-pal-matching.txt`, `build/s1-matching-pal/compare-wad-strict.txt`).
  - **Host build:** the build passes, with the 60 Hz rules (`logs/pal-ov/final-host-build2.txt`).
  - **US unchanged, off:** `tools/host_checks.sh` with main `b8911b4`'s binary and this branch's: **287 of 287 files identical** (demo, the whole tour, routes A and B). The logs were compared with their folder and binary names masked (`logs/pov-main-*`, `logs/pov-br-*`, `logs/pal-ov/hc-cmp.txt`).
  - **US unchanged, interpolated:** `tools/pres_check_ab.sh MODE=chk` with main's binary as the reference: files identical: demo 26/26, wsi 26/26, routes A and B 56/56 each, and `pres check` 6,169, 6,169, 6,669 and 6,669 frames identical, 0 differing (`logs/pov-pc-*`, `logs/pal-ov/pres-check.txt`). gems (rerun once its pad `logs/pads/gems.txt` was copied into this worktree): 19/19 files and 4,553 frames identical (`logs/pal-ov/pres-check-gems.txt`).
  - `tools/check_no_assets.sh`: exit 0.
  - **The PAL attract demos against PCSX-Redux** (R4's capture `rx-pal-demo`, 16,000 vblanks; the host with `--region-dev --disc disc/files-pal`; `trace_diff.py --key tick --where gamestate=0,level=N`; `logs/pal-ov/demo-diff.txt`):
    - Stone Hill (11): the same through tick 166; before this work it differed at tick 1.
    - Doctor Shemp (24) and Icy Flight (55): the same through tick 41.
    
    At each first divergence only `rand` differs; Spyro's position, state and the rest still agree. **Not yet tick for tick.** With the overlays now PAL's, the next differing draw is probably outside them (the main executable's hand C, or a table). Finding it is R4's rand-caller comparison (D143), told.

## 2026-11-12 — the PAL disc: R4, the attract demos (the screen centre)

### D147. The lit-moby renderer puts back the release's screen centre: PAL's GTE offset is (256, 128), and `func_80022A2C`'s hand C (`moby_hand.c`) restored US's (256, 120) after every lit moby, so on PAL everything drawn after the lit mobys without setting its own offset, the particles among them, was projected 8 lines too high, and the particles near the picture's bottom edge counted as drawn when the PS1 culls them. It now restores `RV(120, 128, 120)`, as `Initialize`'s `SetGeomOffset` does (D134). US and JP are unchanged
- **Why:** R4 (D143) checks the PAL attract demos against the PS1 tick for tick. With R3 part 4's overlay code (D146, PR #133) the first rand() difference was at tick 167 of the Stone Hill demo and tick 42 of the other two.
- **How it was found:** every rand() call with its caller on both sides, at 50 Hz (a Redux breakpoint on PAL's rand, 0x80067B5C; a temporary host build on #133; `logs/r4/rx-rand2`, `logs/r4/h-rand2`). In each demo the first difference was particle class 66's update (three rand() calls a step, `particle_update.c`) running for one or two particles more on the host. Its particles die when `m_WasRendered` is 0. The particle lists dumped each vblank on both sides (`logs/r4/rx-part`, `logs/r4/h-part`; PAL's `g_Particles` at 0x8007C0F8 from `data_map.py`) showed the first difference: a point particle, same position on both, drawn on the host and not on the PS1 (Stone Hill tick 163 slot 37, Doctor Shemp tick 38 slot 7, Icy Flight tick 37 slot 5). The particle renderer is the same code in both releases (one of the seven that differ only in relocations, `docs/regions-plan.md` §2), and its bounds are the same; what differed was the offset it projects with. Of the executables' four `ctc2` writes to OFX/OFY, three take registers; the fourth, the lit-moby renderer's restore, is `lui 0x78` in US (0x80023964) and `lui 0x80` in PAL (0x8002899C).
- **What it changes:** PAL's projection after the lit mobys, so its particles, and anything else drawn after them that doesn't set the offset, are at the PS1's height, and its particles live as long as on the PS1. The `--fast-render 0` translation (`m2c_renderers_r_moby.c`, generated from US's asm) keeps 120: a debug path, and PAL with it isn't checked.
- **Checked (PAL image `52bba2f6…`, executable `fdd04c04…`, `WAD.WAD` `f1fc05d7…`, with #133's overlay code; `logs/r4/rx-rand3`, `logs/r4/h-rand3`, rand() callers to tick 2,000):**
  - **Doctor Shemp:** every rand() call the same, caller for caller, through the whole demo (766 ticks; before: tick 42).
  - **Stone Hill:** the same through tick 622 (before: 166). At 623 the PS1 has one call more: a moby on a path in state 2 (`moby_update.c`, `RandRangeSigned(60, 100)` when `m_0x2c >= 4 && g_AnimationFinished`; PAL 0x800857F4) turns a tick earlier than on the host: its animation or its blocked count is a tick ahead on the PS1. Not found yet.
  - **Icy Flight:** the same through tick 64 (before: 41). At 65 the host has one of Spyro's flame puffs hit something (`pete_hand.c`, `func_80048D10`, the same code in PAL, R3's strict comparison) a tick before the PS1 does. Not found yet.
  - **The traces** (`trace_diff.py --key tick --where gamestate=0,level=N`, Redux's `logs/r4/rx-pal-demo` against `logs/r4/h-pal-demo-ofy`): Doctor Shemp's 766 ticks identical in all 11 columns (Spyro's position and state, gems, rand()); Stone Hill identical through tick 622, Icy Flight through 64, Spyro's position included, so both remaining differences start in a moby, not in him.
- **The whole game in each language** (R4's step after R2 part B, D139, D145; host, 50 Hz, a new game through PAL's title with `newgame_pal_host_{en,fr,de,es,it}.txt`, then the tour as D143's, 100,000 vblanks each, main `b8911b4`'s binary; `logs/r4/h-pal-tour-{en,fr,de,es}`, `-it2`): every run exit 0, all 34 warps taken, ending 2, level 64, ending 3, the credits and both cheats; no crash, no hang. The five traces are identical row for row (the language changes only what is drawn); 8 of the 100 shots differ by language: the title (2), the flight results (levels 25, 35 and 55, each in its language, accents drawn: TU AS RATÉ, ABGESTÜRZT, TE HAS CAÍDO, PRECIPITATO) and the pause and inventory screens after the credits (the level names in the language where the disc has them). The credits are the same in every language. The flight results' fourth row (the copters) has no count in any language; not compared with the PS1 (no route reaches those results there yet).
- **The Italian pad:** `newgame_pal_host_it.txt` (D143) pressed its fourth RIGHT and UP at the same vblank, 2050; the later line won, so it chose Spanish (its first tour, `logs/r4/h-pal-tour-it`, was Spanish's to the byte). UP is at 2100 now.
- **US unchanged (corrected after #134 merged).** The "287 of 287 files identical" first written here compared main with main: a docs commit made with `git commit -a` (ad69f43) while the check script had main's `moby_hand.c` checked out put main's file back, so the branch binary had no fix (restored in 76b31e8, which is what main 44e5ba3 has). Measured again on #135's tree: main before #134 (`b0c09a1`'s `moby_hand.c`) against 44e5ba3's, both rebuilt with the objects deleted and the two binaries checked to differ (a first try built the same binary twice: the restarted colima VM's sshfs mount reported stale file times), **287 of 287 host-check files identical** (`logs/r4hc-pre-m8-*`, `logs/r4hc-main-m8-*`); the PAL demos with that binary as above (Doctor Shemp's 766 ticks identical, Stone Hill to 622, Icy Flight to 64; `logs/r4/h-pal-demo-m8`); ctest 30 of 30 (`logs/r4/ctest-m8.txt`); the US matching build `PSX.EXE SHA-1 OK` (`logs/r4/matching-m7.txt`); `tools/check_no_assets.sh` exit 0.
- **The presenter reads `g_Region`** (`render_ctx_link`, red on main 44e5ba3): the presenter's copy of `func_80022A2C` (`moby_hand_pres.c`) now reads `g_Region` through `RV()`, and the check lists every outside name a presenter object may use. `g_Region` is added to its read-only names (`tests/render_ctx/link_check.sh`). It's safe at run time: `g_Region` is written only by `psx_cd_open` (`libcd.c`, `cdimage.c`), which the Vita and the host call once at start-up, before the renderer and any presenter thread start (`vita/main.c` opens the disc before the renderer; the host's replay opens it before its samples); nothing writes it afterwards. Checked with the build's objects and macOS's `llvm-nm`: the old list fails on `g_Region` alone, the new passes, 8 presenter objects.

## 2026-11-12 — the 60 FPS row, 60 HZ on other discs, PAL's 60 fps and its 256 lines

### D148. The menu's 60 FPS row is OFF, INTERPOLATED and 60 HZ (D44's NO ANIM and CAMERA gone, `interp=2` and `3` read as 1); on a disc other than the US one it offers OFF and INTERPOLATED under a `60 HZ LOGIC: US DISC ONLY` row, and the log says what runs; the PAL disc gets a PAL 60 row with D136's restart question; PAL at its own 50 Hz is 60 fps by time (a frame for every display vblank, blended at its own time between two 25 Hz ticks); and presentation frames take the display's height, so PAL's 256-line picture, the title's language row with it, is shown whole
- **Why:** the owner tried main `44e5ba3` on the Vita with the PAL disc (`region_dev=1`; `logs/hw-pal-try-20261112/` in the main checkout, gitignored). They found:
  - the 60 FPS row's five values confusing;
  - 60 HZ did nothing on PAL, silently: `logic60()` is the US disc's only (D138), there was no restart question, and the log still said `present: 60 Hz logic in play (interp=4, D130)`;
  - PAL's frame rate uneven: the FPS counter jumped between the high 40s and 51 (`vblank N: 300 vblanks in 6006 ms (49.9 per second), 302 pictures`: 50 pictures a second on a 60 Hz display, so every fifth display vblank showed a picture again);
  - no language select on PAL's title.
  
  They asked for one PR (`docs/regions-plan.md`'s R4 session, `pal-r4-whole-game`, owns PAL's enabling PR and was told what this touches).
- **The number:** D148 is the next free on main `44e5ba3` (D147); #135, open, adds none.

#### The 60 FPS row
- **Values:** `k_interp_names` is `OFF`, `INTERPOLATED`, `60 HZ` for the values 0, 1 and 4 (`present_vita.c`, the row's `values`). `interp=2` (no animation blending) and `interp=3` (camera only), D44's diagnostic modes, are gone from the Vita: `vita_present_apply` sets `interp_mobys` and `interp_anim` with `interp`.
- **Old configs:** `read_config` (`main.c`) reads a 2 or a 3 as 1, with a log line (`config: interp=2 (no animation blending) is no longer offered (D148): INTERPOLATED (interp=1)`); the menu writes the 1 back at its next save. No new `config_version`: the mapping holds whatever the file's version, and D44's rule (`interp=1` needs version 2 or later) runs first, as before.
- **The label** is `60 FPS`, not `60 FPS (EXPERIMENTAL)`: the selected value is shown between arrows beside the label, and the panel holds 32 characters a row (640 pixels less the margins, 18 a character). `60 FPS (EXPERIMENTAL)` and `< INTERPOLATED >` are 38. The README's known issue now says 60 fps is off by default; it was marked experimental there too. 60 HZ is still described as experimental in the README.
- **Kept on the host:** `--interp-no-anim` and `--interp-camera`, and `PresentConfig`'s `interp_mobys` and `interp_anim`, for comparisons (docs/presentation.md); nothing on the Vita sets them apart from `interp` now.
- `tests/menu_panel`'s sample row is the new one (values 0, 1, 4; it checks the wraps, a load by name and the width).

#### 60 HZ on another disc
- `logic60()` stays the US disc's only (D138: PAL's added code isn't judged for 60 Hz; below).
- **The menu:** with another disc, the keyed 60 FPS row is hidden and a twin shows (OFF, INTERPOLATED; its own copy of the value, no key), with a `60 HZ LOGIC` row under it reading `US DISC ONLY` (an action that does nothing). A config's `interp=4` runs as INTERPOLATED there and stays in `config.txt` for the US disc, saved by the hidden keyed row, until the player changes the twin.
- **The log:** `present: 60 Hz logic is the US disc's only (interp=4, D138, D148): this disc runs 25 Hz logic with the in-between frame (INTERPOLATED); interp=4 stays in config.txt for the US disc until the 60 FPS row is changed` (30 Hz with PAL 60). A build without the rules says so; only the US disc with the rules says "in play".

#### 60 Hz logic on PAL: what it would take (not done)
- **PAL 60** (`pal60=1`: PAL's code, US timing, 60 Hz vblanks) is the near case. The 60 Hz rules' edits (`patches/spyro-1-tick60/`) are already written on PAL's `RT()` text (D144, D146), and PAL 60's `RT()` values are US's. What's left is PAL's own code in what the rules compile again, judged as D144 judged the classes:
  - in the overlays' shared `moby_update.c`: 28 `REGION_IS(PAL)` blocks and 23 `RV()` sites. Among them: the egg thieves' turn toggle, a mover's ground snap, the added sounds, a homing drop not marked killed, and a moby's collision update every update;
  - in the main executable's update path: about 24 sites in `gamestates/update.c` (many are the pause and menu pages, not rates), 4 in `moby_helpers.c` (D134's shared fixes: `func_80039398`/`MobyMove`'s re-test, `MobyMoveWithGravity`'s slope slowdown), 1 in `pete.c`, 6 in the hand C (`pete_hand.c`, `pete_dispatch_hand.c`, `pete_state_hand.c`).
  
  Then: lift the region gates (`logic60()`, the host's `--tick60` refusal in `linux32/main.c`), run a PAL 60 host tour at `--tick60` against one without (R4's PAL pads), Vita3K, hardware. D138's open "PAL 60's cutscenes" (the index assumes 50 Hz) applies with or without 60 Hz.
- **PAL at its own 50 Hz:** the rules split a tick's per-tick amounts over its two vblanks (`T60_SHARE`), so they'd run 50 Hz logic on PAL's 25 Hz constants. Beyond PAL 60's judging, it needs the 83 `RT_TIME`/`RT_SPEED` sites in `moby_update.c` judged, and the rules' own constants that assume 60 (the frame-counted path step, `t60_rules.c`). The presenter needs nothing more: blending by time takes any tick length (one-vblank ticks are the title's).
- **Estimate:** PAL 60 with 60 Hz logic is one session's work, like D144's but on about a tenth of the sites. 50 Hz logic is that plus the retune sites. Neither is small enough for this PR.

#### The PAL 60 row
- A `PAL 60` row (key `pal60`, OFF or ON), shown with the PAL disc only, under the 60 FPS rows. `pal60=` was `read_config`'s own key; the row loads it now (the same key, before the disc opens; `g_RegionPal60`). Read at start, so a change asks D136's question, `PAL 60 BEGINS` (or `STOPS`) `WHEN THE GAME STARTS AGAIN`, RESTART NOW (60 Hz or 50 Hz) or UNDO (`BACK TO OFF`/`ON`), and a `PAL 60 TIMING` row says ON or OFF AT RESTART while it waits. The two questions share the restart code (`restart_ask(ASK_T60 | ASK_PAL60)`).
- With PAL 60, PAL is US timing: 30 Hz logic, 30 or 60 fps (INTERPOLATED, the half blend) as on the US disc.
- The README's options table doesn't list the row: PAL isn't playable for players yet. R4's enabling PR adds it (told).

#### 60 fps by time (PAL at its own 50 Hz)
- **What was there** (D138): the presenter blends one in-between frame a tick, at ½, due at a game vblank. At 50 Hz that's 50 frames a second, shown at the display vblank each 50 Hz vblank starts at: one display vblank in six showed its picture again.
- **Dues in display vblanks:** a platform that shows each frame at its due display vblank sets `psx_clock_set_due_display(1)` once at start: the Vita with 7c's due presents (D112), the host with `--display60`. Then, at 50 Hz only, the dues `present.c` files are display vblanks counted from the same base (`psx_clock_due_at(v)`: our vblank as one, `ceil(v * 60 / hz)`), and `psx_clock_display_due` / `psx_clock_display_to_ours` don't convert. At 60 Hz each is the old arithmetic. The game thread and the host take frames per display vblank (one or two per vblank of ours: `vita_gpu_due_vblank`, `host_present_vblank`). A frame still dropped from a full queue no longer moves the others' dues (`PresentFrame.fixed`). The render thread's rule is the same.
- **A frame per display vblank** (`time_plan`, `time_frame`, `time_rest`):
  - Times are in 1/300 s (a game vblank is 6, a display vblank 5).
  - Tick N, drawn at our vblank c, is shown at `hi = 6(c + 4)`: one vblank of ours after the in-between frame's real frame. That frame is filed after the logic draw's VSync and due a vblank later, at c + 3. The reason: the game's draw makes the tick's `VSync(0)` itself (GamestateDraw ends with DrawSync, VSync, PutDispEnv, DrawOTag), so frames filed after the draw land after that wait. With `hi = 6(c + 4)`, at most one of the tick's display vblanks falls inside it.
  - That first frame is drawn before the logic draw, on the presenter's core beside it, as the in-between frame is. The others are drawn after it, each with a display vblank or more of slack. A frame whose display vblank has passed is dropped.
  - Every display vblank u with 5u in (last tick's time, hi] gets its own frame: 2.4 a tick at 25 Hz, so 2 or 3.
  - Each frame is the presenter's, the two ticks blended with the previous tick's weight `(hi - 5u) / (hi - last)`, unblended when 5u == hi.
  - The logic draw is hidden as in the attract demos (`s_suppress_ot`): its side effects run, its list isn't drawn, and the presenter's frames are the picture.
  - Without a blend (a new gamestate or level, a camera jump, the last tick more than 8 vblanks back, a credits stage that files no frame, `interp_budget`) the tick shows its logic draw's own frame at its first display vblank.
- **The blends at any weight:** `mid_i`, the angles, the particles and `mid_track` take the previous tick's weight `s_wn / s_wd`, written so that at ½ they are exactly the old halves, rounding included (`cur - d * 1 / 2 == cur - d / 2`; `prev + (cur - prev) / 2 == (prev + cur) / 2` for the progress, which never goes back in a blended track).
- **Two wrong turns:**
  - Drawing a tick's later frames from the hook on the logic draw's own `VSync(0)` broke the logic draw: `--pres-check` differed on 2,831 of 2,832 frames, and the game changed.
  - Drawing every frame before the logic draw made the game thread wait for each in turn (Vita3K: about 9 ms a vblank, frames not ready by their vblank).
  
  The presenter sets libgpu's `currentDispEnv` for the logic draw it expects next, so after the draw it's put back (`time_rest`).
- **OFF** on PAL is unchanged: 25 frames a second, each at the display vblank its 50 Hz vblank starts at.
- **Cost:** about 2.4 presenter frames a tick (about 60 a second) instead of one, with the logic draw's GPU work and its walk gone. The game thread waits for the presenter once on a tick with three frames.
- **Stats:** `present N: by time (D148): …` on the Vita and the host (`--present-stats`): ticks, frames, logic draws hidden, ticks shown by their logic draw, and the display vblanks with no new frame.

#### PAL's 256 lines and the language select
- **The cause:** `present.c` captured every presentation frame as 512×240, the US display, whose drawn 224 lines start 8 down; the platforms crop those 8 (`shown_top`). PAL's display is 512×256, all of it drawn (D134). So its frames lost their bottom 32 lines, and the title's language row (under START GAME, between arrows when chosen) sits in them. On the Vita the frames were 960×476, stretched to the screen. The PAL title with `skip_logos=1` and a card shows the row the same way; the owner's start had it off the picture.
- **The fix:** a frame takes the display's height: 256 when `disp.h` is 256, else 240 as before (`capture_h`), for the logic draw's capture and the presenter's. The host's pixel slots are `PRESENT_H_MAX` lines and keep each one's (`port_present_slot_h`, `port_present_shown_h`): `png.c`, the Vita's software renderer (`video.c`) and the GPU renderer's CPU-slot path take them. The GPU renderer already handled a 256-line rect (`shown_top(256)` is 0, the scale 544/256): its frames are 960×544 now. The host's 4:3 shots of a 256-line picture are 725 pixels wide (544 × 4/3), not 777. US is unchanged: its display is 240 lines.

#### Checked
Logs are in the worktree's `logs/d148*` (gitignored). Binaries: main `44e5ba3`'s host build (`build/bins/spyro-main-44e5ba3`) and this branch's (`spyro-d148b`).

- **US unchanged, off:** `tools/host_checks.sh` with main's binary and the branch's: **287 of 287 files identical** (demo, the whole tour, routes A and B; logs compared with folder and binary names masked; `logs/d148hc-main-*`, `logs/d148hc-fin-*`, `logs/d148/host_checks_cmp_final.txt`).
- **US unchanged, interpolated:** `tools/pres_check_ab.sh MODE=chk`, main's binary as the reference: demo 26/26 files identical, wsi 26/26, gems 19/19, routes A and B 56/56 each. `pres check` identical on every frame: 6,169, 6,169, 4,553, 6,669 and 6,669 frames, 0 differ (`logs/d148/pres_check_ab.txt`, `logs/d148pc-*`).
- **PAL, interpolated, the old way unchanged:** `--interp --widescreen` on the PAL disc (a new game into Artisans, 9,000 vblanks, `--save`). Main's and the branch's trace are identical, and 9 of 9 shots are identical (`logs/d148/hp-iw-main`, `hp-iw`).
- **PAL by time** (`--display60`, the same run):
  - The trace is identical to the run without it, so presenting by time doesn't touch the game.
  - `pres check`: 4,719 of 4,719 frames identical (gamestates 0, 13, 14).
  - 10,800 display vblanks, 6 without a new frame (gamestate changes); 4,712 ticks blended by time, 7 shown by their logic draw (`logs/d148/hp-bt4`).
  - The title flow without a card (6,000 vblanks): the trace is identical to the game's with presentation off (`h-pal-bt4`, `h-pal-ref`).
  - `--pres-write-check`: 6,785 presenter frames, none wrote the game's memory (`hp-wc`).
  - The first draft (frames after the draw, without putting libgpu's display environment back) changed the game in that title run; that's what found it.
- **ctest:** 29 of 30 passed on `44e5ba3` (`logs/d148/ctest.txt`). `render_ctx_link` failed on `g_Region`: main's own failure since D147, which #135 fixed. With #135 merged in (main `f0a0e14`), it passes (`logs/d148/ctest-link-merged.txt`).
- **Matching build:** `PSX.EXE SHA-1 OK` on this branch, before and after the changes (`logs/d148/build_matching.txt`, `build_matching_final.txt`); no decomp or patch file changed.
- `tools/check_no_assets.sh`: exit 0.
- **Vita3K** (the VPK built with the Mac's VitaSDK, `logs/d148/v3k-*`; `REALTIME=1`, the PAL image, `region_dev=1`):
  - **The PAL title before the fix:** the language row cut off at the screen's bottom edge (`v3k-pal-title/win-grab.png`). **After:** START GAME with ENGLISH under it, frames 960×544 (`v3k-pal-title3/win-grab.png`).
  - **The PAL menu with `interp=4`:** 60 FPS INTERPOLATED, 60 HZ LOGIC: US DISC ONLY, PAL 60 OFF, and the log line above (`v3k-pal-menu`).
  - **PAL 60 OFF → ON:** the question, RESTART NOW 60 HZ / UNDO BACK TO OFF (`v3k-pal-ask`).
  - **The US menu:** 60 FPS `< INTERPOLATED >`, no PAL rows (`v3k-us-menu`).
  - **`interp=2`:** the migration's log line, then `interp 1` (`v3k-migrate`).
  - **PAL's pacing at 60 fps by time** (the Mac otherwise idle):
    - on the title, 354–360 pictures per 300 vblanks (6 s), 0 not ready by their vblank, 0–3 late;
    - in Artisans after a new game (the host's PAL pad), 358–361, 0 late, 0–2 not ready;
    - the game thread waited for the presenter about 5.7 ms per 50 Hz vblank (the joins before a tick's later frames);
    - main's PAL was 300 per 300 (50 a second; the owner's log).
    
    With the host's checks running beside it, Vita3K's presenter took three times as long, so frames were late and 270–290 were shown: the timings are the emulator's, and hardware decides.
- **Not done: hardware.** The pacing, the presenter's cost at 2.4 frames a tick, and the CPU left for the game thread need a run on the owner's Vita, which needs the owner's yes. It is set up for PAL, with `config.txt.before-pal` as its backup.
  
## 2026-11-12 — the PAL disc: R4, the attract demos (moby collision)

### D149. PAL's `ColMoby` doesn't block on a pass-through volume: in each of its four volume kinds (sphere, cylinder, capsule, vertex) PAL records the moby it hit after the volume's pass-through test (`VOLUME_PASS`), where US records it before. A pass-through volume still damages its moby and still adds its damage word in both; in US it is also the hit `ColMoby` returns, in PAL it isn't, so in PAL it doesn't stop a moby's move (`func_80039398`, `MobyMove`), a flame puff (`func_80048D10`) or anything else that asks `ColMoby` whether something is there. The port's `MobyVolumes` (`collision_hand.c`) now does that for PAL (PAL 50 and PAL 60: a release change, not timing). With it and D146, D147 the three PAL attract demos match the PS1 tick for tick
- **Why:** R4's demos against the PS1 (D143, D147). With D146 and D147, Stone Hill matched to tick 622 and Icy Flight to tick 64.
- **How it was found:**
  - **Stone Hill:** the PS1 makes one rand() call more at tick 623 (`RandRangeSigned(60, 100)` in a path moby's state 2, PAL 0x800857F4, when `m_0x2c >= 4 && g_AnimationFinished`). Every level moby dumped each tick on both sides (Redux `logs/r4/rx-mobys`, PAL `g_LevelMobys` 0x8007C0FC; host `--moby-dump`, `logs/r4/h-mobys`): all 200 the same through tick 613; at 614 two class-10 mobys (30 and 31) stop moving on the host for seven ticks. Their props (Redux `logs/r4/rx-moby`; host `--peek` through `g_LevelMobys`, `logs/r4/h-props2`) match through 613; at 614 the host's blocked count `m_0x2c` starts counting for both. The PS1's own calls of `func_80039398` for them (a Redux breakpoint on PAL 0x8003E5E4 and its return, `logs/r4/rx-move`) return 0 for moby 30 every tick and 1 for moby 31 only from 616. So on the host the move was blocked where the PS1's wasn't.
  - **The code:** R3's strict comparison listed `ColMoby` as not the same (no constant, 12 unpaired ranges), and the plan (D134) had called it identical apart from relocations. An instruction diff with only immediates masked (US 0x8004E3C8, PAL 0x80053A34) shows the one change, four times: US's `addi $s2, $fp, 0` (the hit moby) before `andi $t7, $v0, 2; bnez … next volume` moves after it in PAL (PAL 0x80053CA4, 0x80053D50, 0x80053E68, 0x80054008; the capsule's two copies become a `nop` and one after the branch). The plan's line is corrected.
  - **Icy Flight** (the host's flame puff hitting something a tick early, `func_80048D10`'s `ColMoby(…) == 0 && ColTraceRay(…)`): the same cause; nothing else was changed for it.
- **Checked** (PAL image `52bba2f6…`, executable `fdd04c04…`, `WAD.WAD` `f1fc05d7…`; main 44e5ba3's binary against this branch's, both rebuilt with the objects deleted and checked to differ):
  - **The PAL attract demos against PCSX-Redux** (`trace_diff.py --key tick --where gamestate=0,level=N`, all 11 columns: Spyro's position and state, gems, rand() and the rest): Stone Hill all 726 ticks identical, Doctor Shemp all 766, Icy Flight all 774: every tick of the three demos (a 20,000-vblank capture of each, `logs/r4/rx-pal-demo20k` against `logs/r4/h-pal-demo20k`; the 16,000-vblank one ends 182 ticks into Icy Flight, `logs/r4/h-pal-demo-m9`).
  - **US unchanged:** `tools/host_checks.sh`, **287 of 287 files identical** (`logs/r4hc-pre-m9-*`, `logs/r4hc-main-m9-*`); the matching build `PSX.EXE SHA-1 OK` (`logs/r4/matching-m9.txt`); ctest 29 of 30, `render_ctx_link` failing as on main 44e5ba3 (#135, merged since, fixes it); `tools/check_no_assets.sh` exit 0.
- **Not covered:** the mips2c translation (`ColMoby_gen`, `M2C_ColMoby`) and the replay build's shadow comparison of the two (`COL_SHADOW`) keep US's order; both are debug paths, run on US.

## 2026-11-12 — one 60 FPS row; PAL at US timing, with 60 Hz logic

### D150. The menu's 60 FPS row is the same for every disc, OFF, INTERPOLATED or 60 HZ, and 60 HZ is the default; PAL always runs at US timing (60 Hz vblanks, 30 Hz logic: D138's "PAL 60"), so D148's PAL 60 row and its `60 HZ LOGIC: US DISC ONLY` row are gone, and 60 HZ runs PAL's code at 60 Hz too. PAL's own 50 Hz stays for developers (`--pal50`, `pal50=1`), where the attract demos still match the PS1 tick for tick. PAL's recorded cutscenes keep their real-time speed at US timing, PAL's tracer rows are indexed by PAL's layout, and the pause screen's backdrop fills a 16:9 screen on PAL
- **Why:** the owner tested main `f405fa6` on the Vita with the PAL disc (2026-11-12) and found two rows confusing (60 HZ "US disc only" beside PAL 60), and side bars on the pause screen with PAL 60 on. Their words: "what we want is a unified '60 FPS' option for both PAL and NTSC that replaces these 60 FPS options completely. It should have options; 'off', 'interpolated', '60Hz', and 60Hz is default. For PAL we should make the native game run at 30Hz and have it support 60Hz."
- **The number:** D150 is the next free on main `f405fa6` (D149).

#### The row and old configs
- `present_vita.c`: one keyed row, `60 FPS` (`interp`: 0 OFF, 1 INTERPOLATED, 4 60 HZ), on every disc; the twin row, `60 HZ LOGIC: US DISC ONLY`, `PAL 60` and `PAL 60 TIMING` went, and the restart question is the 60 HZ one only (D136's, unchanged). 60 Hz logic runs on the US disc and on PAL at US timing (`logic60_disc`); JP (not supported) and PAL with `pal50=1` run 60 HZ as INTERPOLATED, and the log says so.
- **The default is 4** (`g_vita_opts.interp`). The key stays `interp` and the values stay 0, 1 and 4: nothing to renumber, and D148's tools and logs keep their meaning.
- **Old configs** (`read_config`, as D68 did for its switches): the menu writes every row's key on every save, so a value it wrote as the default isn't the player's choice. `interp=0` without `config_version=4` (off was the default from D44 to D149) and `interp=1` without `config_version=2` (on before D44, which D44 read as 0) are read as 4, with a log line; the menu writes `config_version=4` and the 4 back at its next save. `interp=1` from D44 on and `interp=4` are the player's and stay. A file without an `interp` line gets 4. The cost, as in D68: a player who chose OFF before D150 gets 60 HZ once and sets OFF again.
- The tools that write `config_version=3` with `interp=0` (`tools/vita/vita3k_run.sh`, `hw.game` and the `fps-runs` files) write 4, so scripted runs stay at 30 fps and compare with the host.
- **`pal60=`** is no longer read: the line is logged (`config: pal60= is no longer read (D150) …`) and the menu's next save drops it. `pal50=1` is a developer's key (no row writes it), like `region_dev=1`.

#### PAL at US timing
- `g_RegionPal60` became `g_RegionPal50`, the other way round: `g_RegionTiming` (RT's PAL values, 50 Hz vblanks) is set for the PAL disc only with it (`cdimage.c`). The host's `--pal50` sets it (`--pal60` is still taken, as the default); the Vita's `pal50=1`. D148's 60 fps by time (a frame per display vblank at 50 Hz) stays: the host's `--display60` and `pal50=1` still reach it.
- The host's `--tick60` refusal (D138) and `logic60()` now let PAL at US timing through.

#### 60 Hz logic on PAL
D148 listed what PAL 60 with 60 Hz logic needed: PAL's own code in what the rules compile again, judged as D144 judged the classes. Every `REGION_IS(PAL)` block and `RV()` site in the 60 Hz set was read (the patched sources, `build/linux32/patched/spyro-1`):
- **`moby_update.c`** (28 blocks, 23 `RV`): one-shot changes on a state change (a collision range, a sound when hit or on Spyro's state 14, a depth offset, the dialogue's random line, a drop that homes in and isn't marked killed, a knock-back's vertical speed), PAL's ordering of stores, an idempotent `m_Rotation.y = 0`, a ground snap on a condition, the egg thief's turn choice (decisions per update, as US's beside it), PAL's per-level speeds (values inside sites D144 judged), the dragon's relocated props, and `MobyMoveWithGravity` called for a knock-back (D130's 60 Hz form). Nothing per tick to mark.
- **`update.c`** (16 blocks, 8 `RV`): the level transition, the pause and inventory pages, the title and cutscenes, all gamestates the rules run at 30 Hz.
- **`pete.c`** (1: the fallback floor ray's states) and the hand C (6: Spyro's three added sounds on a state change, the vertical acceleration kept, level 43's gravity, a model vertex): nothing per tick.
- **`moby_helpers.c`** (4): two collision re-tests, a level-name spawner, and **`MobyMoveWithGravity`'s wall-slide slowdown**: a quarter of the horizontal speed when `MobyMove` returns 4 on a slope. The rules call `MobyMove` every vblank, so it applied twice a tick. It is `T60_TICK_END_DO` now, on the vblank that ends the tick, before the deceleration as in the original (`patches/spyro-1-tick60/0003-pal-at-us-timing.patch`). A slide that only the tick's first vblank finds isn't slowed.

#### PAL 60's recorded data
PAL's data (its WAD) is laid out for 25 Hz ticks, and with US timing some code that used `RT()` read it with US's numbers. Three kinds were found and fixed here; they are the port's only, in a new patch (`patches/spyro-1-region-port/0011-pal60-recorded-data.patch`; the matching builds fold `RV` and `RT` to the same constants and don't apply it):
- **The tracer rows** (a cannon's shots' trails, D146): a shot writes up to 0x78 points into one of three rows in its parent's props, and PAL's rows hold 0x79, 0x6C and 125 points where US's hold 0x91, 0x82 and 150. With `RT()` PAL 60 used US's row strides on PAL's props, so the third row's trail ran past them. The strides are `RV()` now, and at PAL 60 a trail holds at most 0x6C points (`TRACER_POINTS_MAX`, `patches/spyro-1-tick60/0003`): a shot flies as long as US's, longer than PAL's rows were sized for.
- **The title flyby's return points** after an attract demo, `RT(720, 600)` and `RT(1168, 972)`, are positions in PAL's flyby (its own loop points are 976, 1120 and 1274, US's x5/6): `RV()`.
- **The cutscenes' clock** (D138's open "PAL 60's cutscenes"): PAL's intro, endings and dragons' talk are sampled for 25 Hz ticks, so at US timing they ran 20% fast and ended before their voices. `PortCutsceneTicks(d)` (`region_game.c`) gives PAL at US timing `d * 5 / 6` of its 50 Hz vblanks, the remainder carried, and `GamestateCutsceneUpdate` and the dragon's talk (state 4) count with it; `DragonVoiceDelay` scales PAL's delay at any timing. The carry (`g_PalCutsceneCarry`) is saved in the save states' `REGN` chunk (PAL's only; `tools/savestate_globals.py` lists it). Elsewhere, and on US, JP and PAL at its own timing, it is `d`. A 25 Hz sample now shows for one 30 Hz tick in six twice.

#### The pause screen's bars
`present.c`'s `is_screen_copy` tells the pause, inventory and flight-results backdrops (strips of a grayscale copy of the last frame) from the HUD by their CLUT, (512, 224). PAL's is at (512, 256) (D139's `RV(224, 256, 224)` in `draw.c`), so in 16:9 its strips were narrowed to 4:3 like the HUD: the bars the owner saw. It takes PAL's CLUT on the PAL disc. (#136 made PAL's frames 256 lines tall; that wasn't it.)

#### Not changed (PAL at US timing)
- **The title flyby** advances 2 a tick, so PAL's runs 20% fast at US timing. Its loop points are tested for equality on even counts, so the clock above can't drive it; it has no voice to keep time with.
- **Spyro's and the mobys' animations:** PAL's models have fewer frames for 25 Hz ticks (D143), so they play about 20% faster at US timing.
- Other PAL data tuned for 50 Hz that `RT()` doesn't cover is not known; D146's `RV()` sites (PAL's values that no timing formula gives) are kept as release changes.

Run under colima's sshfs it can still fail on the base tree it has just written (`unterminated #if`), as R4's notes warn; its `run.sh`, run again a moment later, passes.

## 2026-11-12 — PAL 60's cutscenes at their exact time

### D151. For PAL at US timing, the intro, the endings and the dragons' talk are placed at the cutscene's exact time, in sixths of PAL's 50 Hz vblanks, instead of at its tick: every 30 Hz tick moves 5/6 of a 25 Hz sample, so the characters' animation and paths step evenly, and the camera moves between its samples instead of holding one tick in six. US, JP and PAL at its own timing run the original's code
- **Why:** the owner played main `8ca72b6` (D150) on the Vita with the PAL disc at 60 HZ: "characters in opening seemed a bit choppy" (`logs/hw-pal-8ca72b6-20261112/` in the main checkout). The log's pacing in the opening (gamestate 14, vblanks 1715–4570) was clean: 150 ticks and 150 in-between frames a window, none late or skipped.
- **The cause: D150's cutscene clock.** PAL's cutscene data is sampled for 25 Hz ticks, and D150 counts PAL's 50 Hz vblanks at US timing with `PortCutsceneTicks`, 5/6 of ours with the remainder carried, so the cutscene's tick moves 2, 2, 1 a 30 Hz tick. `func_8002BFE0` places the mobys and the camera from that tick:
  - a moby's animation time (its frame and 64ths) moved 32, 32, 16 a tick (16, 16, 8 for the slower models): a whole step on two ticks and half a step on the third;
  - the camera, one sample per 2 of the tick, held for one tick in six.
  
  The speed was right (D150: 49.0 s, PAL 50's 48.96), the steps weren't even. The dragons' talk (`DragonCutscene`, `CutsceneCamera`, `CutsceneAnimate` in `gamestates_hand.c`) did the same.
- **Not the presenter:** it blends the cutscene mobys as it does a level's. On the host's opening with `--interp`, US blends 30,678 animation tracks and keeps 18, PAL 27,978 and 46, at single-digit microseconds a tick: the hardware log's 8–9 us "blend" is a cutscene's few mobys, not mobys left out. Both skip the in-between frame 11 times (the camera's cuts), before and after this change.
- **Not D150's open "animations 20% fast":** that is play (PAL's models at 30 Hz ticks); a cutscene's mobys are placed from the cutscene's time, which D150 already keeps.
- **The fix** (`src/game/region_game.c`, `patches/spyro-1-region-port/0012-pal60-cutscene-time.patch`, `gamestates_hand.c`):
  - `PortCutsceneTime6(tick)`: the cutscene's exact time, `tick * 6` plus the carry `PortCutsceneTicks` keeps, in 1/300 s; it moves exactly 10 a 30 Hz tick. -1 when PAL isn't at US timing.
  - `PortCutscenePlay()`, called first in `func_8002BFE0` (port only, `#ifndef REGION`): in gamestate 14 with PAL at US timing, each moby's frame and 64ths from the time (`func_8002BFE0`'s four frame lengths, PAL's 50 frames an animation) and its position between two samples as before; the camera between its two samples, angles the short way. The title's flyby keeps the original's code (its own tick, D150).
  - **Cuts:** two samples more than 0x2000 apart on an axis (the presenter's jump, `present.c`) or an eighth of a turn apart are a cut, and the camera holds the first until the second's time, as the original does. Without it the first Vita3K run showed in-between frames dropped where the camera was half way between two shots.
  - The dragons' talk: `CutsceneCameraAt(t6)` between frames, and `CutsceneAnimate` from the time.
  - The camera between samples is the port's own: the original shows one sample a tick, and 25 Hz samples have no 30 Hz one. A sample itself is the data's, angle and all.
- **A tool:** `--cutscene-trace FILE` (host, `trace.c`): in gamestate 14 and a dragon's talk, each vblank, the cutscene's tick, the camera and each cutscene moby's position, frame and 64ths.

#### Checked

Main `8ca72b6`'s tree is D150's commit's (`git diff` empty), so D150's binary `spyro-u60c` stands for main; this branch's is `spyro-c3` (the host checks) and `spyro-c4` (`c3` with the cut test; `objdump` differs in `func_8002F3E4`, `PortCutscenePlay` and the new `PortCutsceneCut` only).

## 2026-11-13 — the vertex cache with the in-between frame

### D152. With SUB-PIXEL VERTICES or PERSPECTIVE on, the in-between frame's polygons get the vertex cache's answers as the logic frame's do: the walk tells the cache where each polygon's answers go, whichever walk it is, and the cache records the presenter's projections while it draws inline. Before, with an in-between frame, every polygon of every frame was drawn with answers left from an older frame: thin lines along polygon edges, a line along a quad's diagonal, torn letters
- **Why:** the owner on the Vita, main `4d3e1da` with the PAL disc (60 HZ, 16:9, `persp=1`, `subpixel=1`, `tex_filter=0`): "you should be able to see clear lines between textures" (`logs/hw-pal-4d3e1da-20261113-seams/` in the main checkout: the title's flyby, the title, the save menu). The log's `vcache` line said 48–56% of the title's polygons found their vertices; play at 60 Hz logic says 93–98%.
- **Not D151's code (#139), nor D150's.** Two older faults, both in how the in-between frame (the presenter, D125–D126) meets the vertex cache (D39, D43). D150 made them show: 60 HZ is the default since, and with it the title, the menus and the cutscenes run 30 Hz ticks with an in-between frame. Play at 60 Hz logic has no in-between frame and never showed them. INTERPOLATED with either option had them since 7f.
- **Cause 1, the lines: the answers went to the buffer drawn last.** For every polygon vertex the cache leaves its answer (the sub-pixel offset and the depth) in `g_vcache_side` at the vertex's index, and the renderer reads them in order (D43). `psx_vcache_poly` found that index from the vertex buffer `GR_UpdateVertexBuffer` had been given last (`psx_vcache_set_base`), and wrote nothing for a vertex outside it. Since 7f (D126) the presenter's walk has a vertex buffer of its own, so with the two walks taking turns each one's vertices were never in "the last buffer drawn": no polygon's answers were written, in the logic frame or the in-between one, and both were drawn with whatever the array still held from the last time one walk ran twice in a row (a dropped in-between frame). A vertex then moved by another vertex's sub-pixel offset (up to half a pixel each way, so two polygons sharing an edge no longer met) and took another's depth (a quad's two triangles mapped differently: the diagonal); 2D polygons, which find nothing and should stay as they are, took them too (the letters).
  - Measured on main with a counter for the polygons whose answers weren't written (Vita3K, the owner's settings, the title): **every polygon of every frame** once the frames alternate, e.g. 1,434 of 1,434 a frame at vblanks 300–600 (`logs/u62/v3k-before`), and the window shows the owner's lines (`win-2100.png`).
- **Cause 2, behind it: the in-between frame found nothing.** The presenter's renderers have their own hooks for the cache, and they were null "until 7e wants them" (D123) and stayed so: its projections were never recorded, so its polygons found no vertex (the 48%: the logic frames' 98% and the in-between frames' 0). With cause 1 alone fixed, an in-between frame would be drawn at integer positions with affine textures between two frames that weren't: a 60 Hz shimmer. The presenter already runs inline when the cache is on, for the cache's sake (D126: "whose tables are the game thread's"), and its `DrawOTag` already closes the cache's frame.
- **The fix:**
  - `psx_vcache_poly(vertex, xy, n, side)`: the walk says where the polygon's answers go, `g_vcache_side` at its first vertex's index in the buffer it is filling (`native_draw.c`), so there is no "last buffer" to be wrong about; `psx_vcache_set_base` is gone (`gpu_gl.c`, `gpu_thread.c`). `psx_vcache_poly_ref` is the three-argument form for `--walk-check`'s reference walk (no answers). One array still serves both walks: while the cache is on, only one walk fills a buffer at a time and draws it before the other starts (the game's, on the parse thread too, is joined before the presenter's inline draw; the presenter's worker never runs with the cache). `psx_vcache.h` says so.
  - `pres_draw_vcache()` (`pres_draw.c`), called by `presenter_pass` for an inline draw that is shown: the presenter's value hook is the game's (`rtp_value_hook`), so its native projections are recorded in the frame's table as the game's are. Its register-file hook stays null (the presenter has no register file since D124, and that hook reads the game's). Not for the worker (never with the cache) nor for `--pres-check`'s recorded list.
- **Cost:** none where the cache is off. With it on, the in-between frame now records its projections (as many as the logic frame's) and its polygons' lookups find them. In Vita3K, on the title, the presenter's part of a tick went from 0.98 to 2.78 ms (the logic draw 1.9–2.0 ms both times; `v3k-before`, `v3k-after`). On the Vita, on the title, about 11 ms against about 6 (below). Where the in-between frame no longer fits, `interp_budget` skips it (the `present` line counts them).

#### Checked
Logs are in the worktree's `logs/u62/` (gitignored). Main `f4f2f04`'s tree is D151's commit's (`git diff` empty).
- **Vita3K, the owner's settings** (the VPK with the Mac's VitaSDK, PAL disc, 60 HZ, 16:9, `persp=1`, `subpixel=1`, `tex_filter=0`, `draw_distance=100`; main's VPK is main with the counter and its log text only):

  | polygons a frame whose answers weren't written | all of them (875–1,434 of 875–1,434) | none (the argument) |
  | polygons with every vertex found | 48–49% | 96–99% |
  | projections recorded a frame | 1,353–2,021 | 2,719–4,023 |
  | the window (`win-2100.png`) | the owner's lines on the sky, the mountains and the ground | none |

  So: either option, any disc, and only with an in-between frame. How bad it looked depended on what the array held from the last time one walk ran twice in a row, from near right to the flat screen.

- **The Vita** (the owner's yes for this run, 2026-11-13; `tools/vita/hw_run.sh`, the VPK of `c39e00f`, `logs/hw-d152-c39e00f/`; the owner's settings as above, PAL disc, a new game by `logs/pads/pal60_gems.txt`, 7,000 virtual vblanks, `shot_hd=1`):

  - Polygons with every vertex found: 96–99% on the title and the save menu, 99% in the opening, 91–95% in play (48–56% on the title in the owner's log of main).
  - **Cost:** on the title the presenter's part of a tick is 8.3–13.5 ms, mostly 10.5–11.6 (5.1–6.7 ms in the owner's log of main), beside a logic draw of 6.3–8.9 ms. No in-between frame skipped for the budget in the title's steady windows (150 of 150); 32–62 a window in the four windows with the start, the save menu's load and the opening's two loads.
